What a callback asks for next (the source items of an appearance) is filed under whoever asked for the first, since that stack holds only the listener.
  - *Full refreshes by trigger:* the reason the caller gave (`skills`, `quests`), otherwise the event the core's frame was handling, otherwise the line that called `RefreshItems`, which is what a call from outside the addon gets (the test addon's forced refreshes). The newest thirty are kept with what the caller said had changed. A refresh that was about one thing only (`worn`, `collection`; notes: Refresh) is counted under that as well, and says so in its line.
  - *Redraws:* how often the windows drew for those refreshes (`Counters:NoteRedraw`, called by `RefreshItems`' timer as it starts on the features), each filed under the trigger of the newest refresh before it. That is how the reader takes the test addon's forced ones out of a session's figure. Refreshes a minute says how often something was thought to have changed; redraws a minute is the work. Evidence: offline (`standalone/counters.lua`; the code from before fails it, and faults planted one at a time each failed it). Forever client (1.60.1.70938, a hunter, a login and a minute and a half, 2026-11-17): six refreshes were four redraws. The login's first three (`settings` and `skills` before `PLAYER_LOGIN`, `pvp` as the world arrived 2.3 s later) were one, filed under `pvp`: the tenth of a second the second one started had not run out by the client's count when the third came, so a wait begun behind the loading screen ends with it. Not seen: a run of refreshes in play (a turn-in, a fight); retail.
  - *Item answers from the client, whoever asked* (`Counters:NoteAnswer`, from the listener's event, which comes for every item): the session's total, the last minute, the last five, and the most in one second. The addon's own loads are a small part of what passes through the client's item cache, and the cache drops data by how much is loaded (notes: Upgrades), so this is the figure that says whether something flooded it.
  - *Draws that waited for a verdict to be ready* (`Counters:NoteLateDraw`, from the draw of a button whose verdict was not ready at first): how many, the wait on average and at the longest, and the same by what the verdict first waited for, which `GetCaerdonStatus` names as its second value where it is not ready (its tooltip, the collection's answer, the data of gear it is compared with, the Catalyst's answer, housing data). It is what a player sees as icons that come late.
  - *A piece's data missing when a comparison needed it* (`Counters:NoteWithoutData`): by the place that asked (Pawn's value of a piece, a piece's PVP floor, a loadout's own piece that is then kept as none for the refresh), how often, how often the client had no item info for the link at all, when first and last, and the last item. The client drops item data again, for worn pieces too (notes: Upgrades), so this is how an export says how often that reaches a verdict in play. A loadout's piece with no value is kept as none until the next refresh, which leaves every piece for its slot without a verdict; that is counted and not yet changed.
  - *What the watcher compared* (`watched`): per watch the events that came, how often it read its state and how often that was a change, and any event the client refused. Events against changes is the noise a watch kept out.
- **The profile has a switch of its own** (`Counters:SetProfile`) and costs nothing until it is first used: the functions are only wrapped then. Wrapped and off, a call costs one extra function call.
- **It does not follow Enable debug.** That setting makes every draw do more (it records each button's geometry and adds tooltip lines), so a profile taken with it describes an addon nobody plays. Nothing in the addon switches the profile on; the test addon does.
  - *Per function:* calls, time, worst call, memory, each also as "own": without what it spent in the other wrapped functions it called. The worst call of a function that is an item's own names its item (`worst 6.50 (item 20882)`), which is what says why one call in a refresh took many times the others. `GetCaerdonStatus` is counted per outcome (computed, cache hit, tooltip not ready, no link); the three `Counters:Mark` lines in it say which.
  - *Wait in the queue:* from the first request for a button that is not yet drawn to its draw.
  - *Drains:* the queue at work, from the first pass after it was woken to the pass that leaves nothing to draw. Time and memory are taken around each pass, so they are the whole pass, hooks of other addons on the core's functions included, and nothing of the frames in between. A pass ends when its time is up (Processing Pipeline), so the longest pass says how well that holds: it is the budget plus the one draw that ran over it.
- **A wrapper on a mixin counts every item,** whenever it was made, since items and item data find their methods through their metatables. Functions on the frame and on the features are reached at once: each feature holds its own copy of the display rules, made when it registered.
- **Memory is `collectgarbage("count")` around the call,** which is every addon's memory, not this one's: it is right as long as nothing else runs inside the call. A sample in which the collector ran is negative, is left out and is counted (`collected`), and one in which it only swept reads low. The test addon's memory per refresh (`GetAddOnMemoryUsage`) is the exact figure; this one says where it goes.
- **The time of a wrapped call includes the clock reads of the wrapped calls inside it** (two per call), so with the profile on a refresh costs more than without. The test addon measures without and counts in separate runs.
- **What a verdict is made of is named part by part:** the asking for the link's tooltip (`GetLinkTooltip`, a method of its own for this), the item's data, each signal's entry point, what the cache's key asks of a copy (`MayBeConverted`, `GetEquipmentSetsOf`, `GetOwnedQuantity`), and the data of each kind of item that is not equipment. Unnamed, they were the status's "own" time, and a tooltip fetch that moved there read as the status having doubled. The profile costs more for it (a bank refresh 21 ms more with it on, 2 to 6 before), so compare profiled runs with profiled runs. Seen on a retail bank (213 items): the tooltip 20 ms to ask and 20 to read, the gear sets asked of every copy 16, the auction value 10, the ingredient check 10, the look after a change 5. The gear sets are since asked only of what can be worn (`StatusCacheKey`), which is an equip location other than `INVTYPE_NON_EQUIP_IGNORE`: both clients answer that, and not an empty text, for what cannot be worn (a gate that went by the empty text asked of everything still: 376 calls a refresh, as before). And the walk of the sets, which is made once for every frame in which anything asks and was most of the cost (`BuildEquipmentSetIndex`, about 0.6 ms a frame over a refresh's twenty-odd frames), reads the set pieces' links only where an item asks by its link (one that lies nowhere: the mail, a merchant's row) and for a second after, so a window of bags or a bank is walked without them. Measured on the same bank (215 items): 225 calls a refresh where it was 376, 7.2 ms where it was 13.4 to 15.8, 0.5 MB where it was 1.3, and no call above 0.4 ms where one took 1 to 3. What is left is the walk itself, about 0.3 ms for each frame of a draw.
- **The export's recent refreshes are one line for a run of the same caller,** so a timing's thirteen do not push out what came before them.
- **A timing run says why a refresh drew another number of items than the others** (`/cwtest timing`; `Journal.lua` notes for the run every refresh that comes during it, by who asked, and every draw that had waited for data, by what it waited for). Asked to refresh again, or items that were not ready and were asked for again when their data came: both are the addon's own doing, the medians stand, and the record says which (`REFRESHED AGAIN BY`, `WAITED FOR DATA` in `tools/test-results.lua`). Only a refresh with neither is `NOT A CLEAN RUN`. Before, every such run read "something else was drawing too" and asked to be run again, which hid a real second refresh for a week.
- **A new function to time** is one `Probe` line in `Counters:Attach`. One that a wrapper cannot see into gets a `Mark`, or an `if CaerdonCounters.on` line where it happens; keep those to places the wrapper cannot reach.
- **Seven probes are outside any draw,** for what the addon costs with no window open: the two handlers that run on every item tooltip anyone fills (`CaerdonAPI.OnTooltipItem`, and the Tooltip feature's `OnTooltipSetItem`, which the counters reach through the addon's namespace table as `NS.Tooltip`), the one the client asks before every requirement line of every tooltip (`OnRequirementLine`, the same way; Ensembles), `CaerdonAuctionValue:OnBagsChanged`, which runs on every `BAG_UPDATE_DELAYED`, `CaerdonQuestItems:CompareWithAnswers`, which runs after every run of quest events and is what such a run costs when it refreshes nothing (not yet counted in a client; the export's `[Quest Value]` section says how many quests it would judge again), and `CaerdonQuestItems:CompareWithClient`, which runs after a run of the client's answers about quests it was asked to load, with the quest client's `Pump`, which puts those requests from a timer (both keep their time in the export without the profile too; a Forever client's figures are under Quest Value). Switch the profile on, play, and read their calls and time (`/cwtest counters on`, then `/cwtest counters`; `on` does not start the figures from zero, so `reset` first when a timing ran before). Offline, and counted in play on a Forever client (a rogue, 2026-11-16): an item tooltip cost 0.007 ms in the first handler and 0.034 ms in the second, half a KB in each, and a held tooltip is filled about five times a second; a change in the bags 0.15 ms.
  - **A tooltip handler makes as little as it can per tooltip.** `OnTooltipItem` writes into the table it keeps, and the lists of names it tries are constants above it; made inside it they were three tables a tooltip. What it can still make is an item location for a tooltip whose data carries a GUID, which the debug hotkey reads. Offline (`scenarios/always-on.lua`), and on the same Forever client after the change: 1527 tooltips over bag and vendor items made nothing in it, so none of them came to an item location there. The second handler is the tooltip's cost now, unchanged at 0.033 ms and 0.4 KB each.
- **Evidence.** Offline: the real module, listener, feature mixin and queue against a mocked client whose clock and memory figure are the mock's own, so every expected figure is exact (calls, own time, memory, the outcomes, a collector run, an error inside a wrapped call, a function that is gone); the item and status code and the drawing were mocked. Forever client (1.60.1.70938, a rogue with BetterBags): callers and triggers are named as above, every function is counted, computed plus cache hit equals the draws, the drains account for 80 to 88 % of the memory the client reports per refresh (the rest is allocated outside the passes), and the profile adds about 2 % to a refresh's addon time. Retail not seen.

## Tooltip Lines in the Export

- **Every line, every field (`DebugFrameMixin:GetTooltipReportRows`).** A tooltip line carries more than its texts, and neither flavor documents what. So the export prints all of it, not a chosen few: a row per line with its type (the number and its `Enum.TooltipDataLineType` name), both colors and both texts, and below it every other field of the line, sorted. `bonding` and `requirementType` get their enum names. A `Data:` row holds the fields of the answer itself (`dataInstanceID`, `guid`, `id`, ...). A field the item code is to rely on is first seen there, in an export from each client.
- **A feature's `GetTooltipData(item, locationInfo)` is asked for a Pet Cage and nothing else.** A caged pet in the mail or a guild bank is one item for every pet, so the status takes the tooltip of the place it lies in from the feature, and judges every other item from its link's. A feature whose window has no such place has no getter: the mixin's answers nil, which is "ask the link". A place's tooltip is for what belongs to a copy, never for requirements (below). Offline: `scenarios/pet-cage.lua`, hand-made, since no recording holds a cage; it also reads every feature file for a getter that only asks for the link's tooltip.
- **An item in a slot has two tooltips.** `[Tooltip Lines (Raw)]` is the slot's (`GetBagItem`, `GetInventoryItem`), which knows the copy: bound, locked, worn down. `[Tooltip Lines (Link)]` is the bare link's (`GetHyperlink`), the one the status is computed from (`CaerdonItemMixin:GetTooltipData`; only the lock is read from the slot). The link's lines are printed when one of them differs from the slot's in a text, a color or a field, and counted otherwise. A difference is something the status cannot know about that copy. An item without a slot has the link's tooltip alone, under `(Raw)`.
- **The lines are the game's alone, and a status is decided from nothing else.** Other addons write on tooltips by adding lines to a tooltip frame while it is being filled. `C_TooltipInfo` returns what the frame is filled from, before any of that: the frame asks for a table of its own, shows its lines and only then runs the addons' callbacks (`Blizzard_SharedXMLGame/Tooltip/TooltipDataHandler.lua`, the same in both trees). So the rule is: read `C_TooltipInfo`, never a tooltip frame's text, and never the frame's own table (`GetPrimaryTooltipData`), which every addon's callback was handed and Blizzard itself writes into (`lineIndex`). The item code and the export follow it. `features/Tooltip.lua` is the exception and decides no status: it keeps the "Already known" line off an ensemble's tooltip, for which it reads the kind of the line the client is about to add and the item ID of the data the frame is being filled from (Ensembles), and it compares the frame's item ID. It reads no font string's text. What reading cannot settle is checked in the client by the contract `tooltip.gameOnly`: a line it adds to a frame of its own is not in the data, a table changed after it was handed out does not come back, and no `C_TooltipInfo` function is an addon's replacement (`issecurevariable`).
- **Copy Details waits for the data of an item that lies in a slot.** The item's own wait does not: an item made from a bag or equipment slot counts as loaded whatever the client has (`CaerdonItemMixin:IsItemCached` only asks about an item made from a link or an ID), and the equipment wait reports once at its start, before the sources' items have come. A retail client twice had no data for a piece lying in the bags a while after gear was swapped, and the export was the one line "Retrieving item information" with every section empty. So `CopyCurrentItemDetails` asks for the data itself (`C_Item.RequestLoadItemData`), waits three seconds at most and says under `[Item]` that it waited or gave up. Why the client had no data for those pieces is not known, and whether a bag button is judged in that state has not been looked at. The same goes for the item info of worn pieces by their links, which one export had for none of them; `[Set Bonuses]` names the slots it had nothing for. Offline (`scenarios/export.lua`); retail client (12.1.5.70961): two of three exports waited, and the data came within 0.2 s both times.
- **The copy box shows an inline texture as its text.** A texture or atlas escape in the export (the crafting quality mark in an enchant's tooltip line) is drawn in the edit box and makes its line taller than the others, after which the selection highlight no longer lies on the text, the same fault `SetSpacing` gave. `ShowCopyOutput` doubles the pipe of those escapes. Deduced from the one export of three that had such a line and the fault; not seen fixed in a client.
- **The account-bound label is read from the binding line's number.** The binding line (type 20) carries `bonding`, a value of `Enum.TooltipDataItemBinding`, which both listings have with the same values. `Account`, `BnetAccount`, `BindToAccount` and `BindToBnetAccount` give the label (`ACCOUNT_BONDING` in `CaerdonItem.lua`), whatever the line's text is. The text is read only on a line without the field, against four global strings that are two texts in each client and not the same two (the fallback, which no client has needed so far). The tooltip's label counts where the bind type gave none or the copy is bound; "until equipped" (9, 10) has its label from the bind type and is not read here. Whether number and text say the same is asked of a client by the contract `tooltip.accountBonding`, for the slot's and the link's tooltip of every bag item: it fails on a binding line without the field, on the field on another line type and on a number and a text that disagree, it is `info` on a toon with no account-bound item, and what it saw (`seen`: side, value, the value's name, text) adds up over the runs. Evidence: offline (`scenarios/binding-label.lua`: every value, every text without the field, and the contract with faults planted one at a time). In a client, from the bag exports made before this was built: retail has shown 1 with `Warbound` in a slot and 5 with `Binds to Warband` on a link, Forever no account-bound item at all. The contract has not run in either client.
- **What the toon does not meet is read from a line's fields, and from red text only where a line has none** (`IsUnmet` / `IsMet` in `CaerdonItem.lua`). A requirement line (type 43: level, class, skill, reputation, "Already known") carries `usable`; the slot line (21) carries `isValidInvSlot` for the slot on the left and `isValidItemType` for the armor or weapon type on the right, which is where WoW Forever's classic armor proficiency shows ("Wrist | Mail" on a hunter below 40). An item that goes in no slot has a slot line with its type alone in the left column, with `isValidItemType` and no `isValidInvSlot` ("Housing Dye", retail 12.1.5.70961); the contract asks that one field of that one column. Those are read first, whatever the colour. A line of those types without its field, and every other line (an error line such as "Locked", a plain line), has its colour alone. Red is the client's `RED_FONT_COLOR`, read once when the file loads, with `ffff2020` where a client has none: both clients make that global from their colour table when Blizzard's `Color.lua` loads, so no source file holds its value and the mocked client has none. `bindingResult.unusableItem`, `raceClassRestricted` and `skillRequirementMet` all come from this. The contract `tooltip.unmetLines` asks a client whether the two agree: on both tooltips of every bag item a field is false exactly where its column is red, `RED_FONT_COLOR` is `ffff2020`, the lines a field called unmet are kept by field with their text (`unmet`), and red on any other line type is listed with examples (`redElsewhere`: the lines colour is all there is for). It is `info` on a toon that meets everything it carries. Evidence: both flavors' recordings (the fixtures: on every line of the two types the field is false exactly where the column is red; red without a field on type 41 only), which replay through the fields; offline `scenarios/unmet-lines.lua` (a field that contradicts its colour, a line without the field, another client's red, the contract with faults planted). The contract has not run in either client.
- **A line found by its whole text is asked of every line, whatever its type.** The type the client gives such a line is not the same in every build, and a check that sits among the typed lines never sees it. "Requires Previous Rank" (`TOOLTIP_SUPERCEDING_SPELL_NOT_KNOWN`, a recipe's higher rank) is an `ErrorLine` on retail 12.1.5; read only as a plain line, the recipe had the star where it is `lowSkill`. Evidence: a retail export of such a recipe (item 170211, rank 3) with status `own`; offline `scenarios/recipe-by-spell.lua`, the line as an error line and untyped.
- **Across the bags:** the test addon's contract `export.tooltipLines` asks the export for every bag item at login, checks each line of the client's answer against its row (the text, every field), and collects which line types differ between slot and link, with a few examples each; lines that differ only in their numbers count as one kind (`test-results.lua contract:export.tooltipLines`). An example says a line is on one side only, not what the other side has in its place: so each example names its item (`slotItems`, `linkItems`), and for the first two kinds of a line type and side the item's whole export is kept (`cases`), both tooltips as a Copy Details would show them. `tooltip.lineFields` is the list of fields per line type.
- **Evidence.** Offline only (`scenarios/export.lua`): the sections for a bare link, a bag slot whose link says the same and one whose link does not, a worn item, a slot with no answer, fields and a line type no listing has; then the contract on three bag items, and against an export that leaves out a field or a line. Faults planted one at a time in the export and in the contract each made it fail. Forever client (1.60.1.70938, a hunter, 2026-11-16): the contract passes on 60 bag items, and a Copy Details of a bag item shows both sections. For 32 items the link's lines equal the slot's. For the other 28 they differ in three line types only: the sell price (the slot's is for the stack), the binding (`Soulbound`, `bonding` 3, where the link says `Binds when picked up`, 6) and plain lines of type 0. Of those the slot alone has a running cooldown, the durability, `<Right Click to Read>` and `<Right Click to Open>`; the link alone has `<This item can be read>`. So a link says that an item can be read in other words than its slot, and does not say that a container can be opened. The slot's answer has a `guid` and no `hyperlink`, the link's the reverse, and each call gets a `dataInstanceID` of its own. Retail client (12.1.5.70783 and 12.1.5.70961, a druid, the bags at every login from 2026-11-16 to 2026-11-18): the contract passes, and the same three line types differ, in more kinds. The binding is `Soulbound` (3), `Warbound` (`bonding` 1) or `Warbound until equipped` (9) in the slot where the link says `Binds when picked up` (6), `Binds when equipped` (7), `Binds to Warband` (5) or `Binds to Warband until equipped` (10). The slot alone has charges, the durability, `Equipment Sets: ...` and `<Shift Right Click to Socket>`; no link has a plain line its slot lacks. A fourth type differs there: a few links carry a requirement line (type 43) their slot does not, `Requires Level 78` (seen on crafted profession tools) and `Classes: Druid`, each of them met (`usable` true), and no slot has a requirement line its link lacks. The status reads requirements from the link and takes nothing from a met level or class line, so no verdict rests on it; the `cases` (12.1.5.70961, 2026-11-18, two items) show that nothing stands in the line's place: a trinket's slot lacks the link's `Requires Level 90` and a set helm's its `Classes: Druid`, with every other line the same but the binding and the slot's own (durability, equipment sets). Both copies are soulbound and both requirements met; whether an unmet one stays in the slot has not been seen. So requirement lines are read from the link, never from a slot. The PVP floor line (`PVP_ITEM_LEVEL_TOOLTIP`; type 0, green) was the same in the slot's and the link's tooltip of the seven bag pieces recorded with one. `tooltip.gameOnly` passes in both clients: its added line was on the frame and not in the data, a changed table did not come back, and none of the 82 `C_TooltipInfo` functions was an addon's. The Forever export, taken with Pawn, Auctionator and other addons loaded that write on item tooltips, holds the game's seven lines and none of theirs. Offline the contract runs against a model of the tooltip frame written for the scenario.

## Text Read Off a Tooltip Line

Some facts are on a tooltip as a line of text and nowhere else. This is how each is read, and what holds in a language other than English.

- **A line the client writes from a format string is read back through that string** (`CaerdonFormatPattern.lua`, `NS.FormatPattern.Compile(format, options)`, then `matcher:Match(text)`). It is the one place a pattern is made from a client string: the skill and rank an item asks for (`ITEM_MIN_SKILL`), a bag's size (`CONTAINER_SLOTS`), the PVP floor (`PVP_ITEM_LEVEL_TOOLTIP`), the durability line (`DURABILITY_TEMPLATE`), Forever's "Equip:" stats (`TOOLTIP_ITEM_STAT_*`), and an item set's lines (`ITEM_SET_NAME`, `ITEM_SET_BONUS`, `ITEM_SET_BONUS_GRAY`; Set Bonuses). A matcher is made once and kept by its caller.
  - **What the strings hold, from all eleven locales of both listings:** arguments by position (`%2$s de %1$d casillas`, so `Match` answers in the order of the arguments, not of the text); full-width punctuation (`需要%s（%d）`); grammar escapes (`%1$d |4ячейка:ячейки:ячеек;`, `%d|1으로;로;`), which the client turns into one of their forms when the text is drawn. Whether a tooltip line still holds the escape or already the form has not been seen in a client, so an escape matches either.
  - **Why one builder:** there were five, each written for the strings its author had seen. The skill line was never read in a zhTW client (the full-width brackets were handled for `zhCN` by name), and a bag's size fell back to its second way of reading wherever the format has a plural escape and the line has the form (esMX, frFR, itIT, ruRU).
- **Which words start an "Equip:" line** is not the same string everywhere: the French Forever client has `Equipé :` as `ITEM_SPELL_TRIGGER_ONEQUIP` and writes its stat strings `Équiper : ...` and `Équipé : ...`, the Brazilian one `Equipado:` and `Equipar: ...`. So an untyped line is an "Equip:" line where it starts with that string or with what one of the client's stat strings starts with, up to its colon (`IsEquipLine` in `CaerdonItem.lua`). Three Brazilian stat strings are malformed in the client itself (`+%1 $d`); what it prints for them is not known and they match nothing.
- **A food's restore line has no format string** (it is a spell's description). The line is the one with a number and the client's word for health or mana; the words are `HEALTH` and `MANA`, and beside them the form a language uses after an amount, taken from what the client's `HEALTH_COST_PER_TIME_NO_BASE` and `MANA_COST_PER_TIME_NO_BASE` differ in (Russian has `Здоровье` and writes `61 ед. здоровья`). The amount is the number before the word, since the line's first number is the duration in German, Korean and Chinese (Outgrown Food and Drink).
- **A recipe item's name** is its prefix, a colon, and the recipe's name in every language looked at; the colon may have a space before it (French) or be full-width with none after it (Chinese). `CaerdonRecipe:GetRecipeNameFromItemName` takes what follows the first colon. In Spanish and Russian the item's name has the recipe's in lower case (`Receta: elixir de agilidad menor`) and the profession's list has it with a capital (`Elixir de agilidad menor`), so a name the saved recipes lack is asked once more with its first letter as a capital (`CaerdonRecipe:FindByName`, `CapitalizeFirstLetter`: a to z and the two-byte Latin and Cyrillic letters, which the client's `string.upper` does not know). That mends the case and nothing else: Classic's data words some recipes differently on the item and in the list in both languages (`Receta: pececito luminoso` teaches `Pececito brillante`), and a Russian prefix can come without a colon (`Формула чар для нагрудника: мана I`), so such an item finds no recipe by its name and is judged as one nobody recorded. The pairs are Wowhead's names of an item and of the spell it teaches, not a client's.
- **A Pet Cage is known by its item ID** (82800), not by its English name, which no other language has.
- **What is still English only,** because it is a phrase of a spell's description or an item's name and the client has no string for it: the `L[...]` use phrases in `GetTooltipData` and `HasTeleportUse` ("Use: Re-learn", "Use: Study to increase your", "... reputation", "Marks your map with the location", "Unlocks this customization", the teleport words), the spell name `L["Studying"]`, "Combine" and "Arrange" in a use spell's description (`GetCopySpellFacts`), and the English prefixes by which a recipe filed under Consumable is told from a consumable (`GetCaerdonItemType`). In another language those items are judged without that signal. Each needs either a translation in `localization/` from someone who has the client, or a field the client does not have today; no typed replacement was found for any of them.
- **Tests.** `scenarios/locale-text.lua` writes each format-string line with the real string of each locale of each client and reads it back through the item code in a client of that locale (the mocked client's `format` takes arguments by position for this, as the game's does). The food lines and recipe names in it are Wowhead's Classic tooltips of two items and a recipe in nine languages, not a client's: the comment at its head says what was changed in them. Against the code from before it fails for the zhTW skill line, Russian food and drink, the amount in German, Korean and both Chinese, the bag size by format in four locales, and the "Equip:" word in French and Brazilian Forever. The contract `text.readBack` asks a client the premise for what the toon carries: a skill line read and written back is the line, a bag's size is read by the format and not by the fallback, the slot's tooltip has the durability line the format writes; it keeps what the food code made of each food carried and lists a food it could not read. It is `info` on a toon that carries none of these.
- **Evidence.** Static: both listings' strings in eleven locales (`update.sh` checks them all out). Offline: the scenario. No client other than enUS has run any of it; in an enUS client the contract and an unchanged bag snapshot say that nothing moved.

## Transmog on Forever

Selling an item to a vendor teaches its appearance ("added to your appearance collection"), with no equip or bind. The collect APIs do not model this, so three flags on `GetTransmogInfo()` cover it. All need both collect verdicts (`PlayerCanCollectSource`, `AccountCanCollectSource`) to be ready.

- **`validSourceNotCollectable`** (and `...KnownLook`): `isValidSourceForPlayer` true, no `useErrorType`, both collect verdicts false. Becomes `own` / `ownPlus`. Red tooltip text is not required: cloth on a hunter (Spellbinder Vest 2969) has this shape with a white tooltip, as does mail under level 40 (Soldier's Wristguards 6550) with a red one. It is `own`, not `lowSkill`, because selling teaches it at once (confirmed in game).
- **`proficiencySourceNotCollectable`** (and `...KnownLook`): `isValidSourceForPlayer` false, `useErrorType` 10, both collect verdicts false (Blazing Wand 5212 on a hunter). Becomes `other` / `otherPlus`: `own` is strictly for appearances the toon can transmog into (author decision). Retail answers "can collect" for such sources, so the flag does not fire there. Errors 7 / 8 / 9 are not covered. Not confirmed in game.
- **`noCollectionCategory`:** both flags are skipped when the item's own `sourceInfo.categoryID` is `Enum.TransmogCollectionType.None`. Arclight Spanner 6219: `C_TransmogCollection.GetItemInfo` is nil, the dress-up fallback still yields a source, and `CanItemTransmogAppearance` is false. Such a source teaches nothing (inferred, not tried in game). `needsItem` / `otherNeedsItem` still follow the collect APIs.
- **A look can list a source whose item this client cannot load.** Appearance 63650 of Arclight Spanner 6219 has a source that belongs to item 216679, which `DoesItemExistByID` accepts and the server refuses. Asked about such a source, `C_TransmogCollection.GetSourceInfo` (it returns a table without `name`), `C_Item.GetItemSpecInfo` and `C_Item.GetItemInfo` each make the client ask the server, and each question is answered about 0.3 s later with `GET_ITEM_INFO_RECEIVED` and `false`; `RequestLoadItemDataByID` with a failed `ITEM_DATA_LOAD_RESULT`. `C_Item.GetItemInfoInstant` answers at once and asks nothing. The client does not remember the refusal: the same question draws the same event again. Other addons redraw on that event, so a question repeated on every refresh costs more than the call (seen in game with `/cwtest sources`). A later probe in the same client (three such sources, a rogue) drew a refusal from the two item calls and none from `GetSourceInfo`, so that one may only ask the first time in a session.
  - **So what the client said about such a source is kept.** `types/EquipmentMixin.lua` notes every item the client refuses (`refusedItems`, from `GET_ITEM_INFO_RECEIVED` without success, whoever asked) beside the ones whose load failed (`failedSourceItemLoads`), and `InvalidateCaches` leaves the cached source info of those items in place. The source still counts in the verdict with what was said the first time; it is only not asked about again. The first sight still asks, and so does every refresh until the refusal has arrived.
  - **A change of the collection forgets it once.** The core's four collection events call `CaerdonEquipment:NoteCollectionChanged()` before their refresh, and that refresh keeps nothing, so a kept answer never outlives a change of what is collected.
  - **The recorder forgets it too** (`ForgetRefusedItems`), since a replay starts with nothing known.
  - Evidence: offline (`scenarios/equipment-once.lua`). Forever client (1.60.1.70938, the rogue that carries the Arclight Spanner, at a BetterBags vendor): every forced refresh drew the vendor once, where the refusals used to bring a second pass in most (66 draws each time, against 64 and then 101), and 216679 was refused three times in the whole session, not twice per refresh.
- **`PlayerCanCollectSource` is true on Forever for a shirt:** Brawler's Harness 6125 on a hunter answers true for the toon and for the account, with a valid source and no use error, and the addon shows `own` through the ordinary path (probe, Forever client 1.60.1.70938). It was false for every other uncollected piece seen from that hunter (armor of every type, weapons). Whether a shirt is the only kind is not known.
- **A look the toon can collect is one to learn now, whatever the piece asks for, in both clients** (the status's `needsItem` branch in `CaerdonItem.lua`; author decision). Where the collect APIs say the toon can collect a source, the status was `own` only with the piece's requirements met, and `lowSkill` otherwise (a level the toon has not reached, a standing it lacks, any red line), on the view that a look is learned by putting the piece on. Selling the piece to a vendor teaches its look with nothing put on, so what the piece asks of its wearer is no bar: the status is `own` (`ownPlus` for a look known from another item), and `lowSkill` is no longer given for a look. *What this rests on:* on Forever a sale has been seen to teach a look whatever the class (above); that it does so below the piece's level, and that retail does the same, is the author's word from play, with no export of such a sale. A sale that taught nothing would bring the old rule back for that client (to settle it: sell a piece above the toon's level whose look is not known, and see whether the collection says it was added). It also puts right a difference between two pieces of one kind on Forever: a piece the APIs refuse had the star already (`validSourceNotCollectable`), and one they accept, of a level to come, had the gear. The test addon's three retail verdict tests for a look with a requirement not met expect `own` and `ownPlus` now (version 2 each). A scenario's client says which client it is: the harness's stock game is WoW Forever by its build and project ID, whatever source a scenario loads, and `scenarios/pawn-scales.lua` sets retail's. Evidence: offline (`scenarios/equipment.lua`, `scenarios/verdicts.lua`, `scenarios/recorder.lua`). Forever client (1.60.1.70959): the auction house's list has the star where the gear of a look for later was. Retail has not been looked at.
- **Unconfirmed, on Forever and on retail:** completing a quest seems to teach the look of every reward choice, whichever one is taken; a class-specific exception may exist (author observation, no export). If it holds, a learnable icon on a choice is no reason to pick it, and the order for choices would be upgrade, then sell value.
- **Red tooltip text is authoritative for equippability.** `CaerdonItem` scans every tooltip line, both columns, for `RED_FONT_COLOR`. Armor proficiency red sits in the right column of the `EquipSlot` line. `bindingResult.unusableItem` forces `canEquipForPlayer = false` and drops `isUpgrade` unless the shortfall is level-based (`upgradeLowSkill`).

## Ensembles

Ensembles teach **sources**, not appearances. Check `sourceInfo.isCollected`; do not skip a source because the appearance is known from another one (difficulty variants are separate sources and count toward set completion).

- **`own`:** uncollected sources wearable by this toon. **`ownPlus`:** an ensemble for another class with wearable extras (cloak, cosmetics). **`other`:** nothing wearable, but learnable for the collection. **`otherNoLoot`:** every remaining source needs another class, race or faction.
- **`sourceInfo.useErrorType` is the only reliable discriminator:** 7 Class, 8 Race, 9 Faction are true restrictions; 10 ItemProficiency (armor type) is not. `PlayerCanCollectSource`, `AccountCanCollectSource`, `meetsTransmogPlayerCondition` and `isAnySourceValidForPlayer` answer the same for both kinds once the account has a toon of the required class.
- **Mixed:** `other` if any remaining source is only armor-type restricted; `otherNoLoot` only when all are truly restricted.
- **Vendor gray-out:** never fade `own`, `ownPlus`, `other`, `otherPlus`, `lowSkill`, `lowSkillPlus`, `otherNoLoot`.
- **"Already known" is kept off the tooltip of an ensemble that still teaches something** (`TooltipMixin:OnRequirementLine` in `features/Tooltip.lua`). The client says it once the toon has used one like it, also while sources of the set are not collected. The rule is the one the icon has: a consumable that teaches a transmog set (`C_Item.GetItemLearnTransmogSet`) with a source that is not collected; the set's own `collected` is asked first and is not enough, since it is true once the looks are known from any source.
  - **The line is never added, instead of blanked afterwards.** The client asks registered callbacks before it adds a line (`TooltipDataProcessor.AddLinePreCall`, by line type; `TooltipDataHandlerMixin:ProcessLineData`, the same file in both trees), and a true answer leaves the line out. The tooltip's data still holds it. Before, the tooltip was filled first and every font string's text read to find the line, which left an empty row and was the one place the addon read text off a tooltip frame.
  - **Which line and which item:** a requirement line (type 43) whose `requirementType` is `NotAlreadyKnown`; where a requirement line carries no `requirementType`, the words of the data line (`ITEM_SPELL_KNOWN`). The item is the ID in the data the tooltip is being filled from (`GetProcessingTooltipInfo`, which for a tooltip that shows two things is the one being added), and only data of an item's tooltip counts. A value the client keeps secret is neither compared nor handed back to it (`issecretvalue`). The same words on a plain line (type 0) are not asked about and would stay; no client has shown the line in that form.
  - **The callback runs for every requirement line of every tooltip,** so it leaves at the first comparison for a level or a class and asks the game only for a line of the kind. It is the counters' fifth probe outside a draw (Counters).
  - **Once any addon registers a line callback, the client passes every line of every tooltip through its dispatch,** whatever the line's type (`ProcessLinePreCalls`: a table and an attribute change per line). That part is Blizzard's and no probe of the addon sees it. Addons in wide use register one as well, so in most clients the addon's is not the first.
  - **Evidence.** Offline (`scenarios/already-known.lua`, both flavors, under Blizzard's tooltip code): ensembles by what they still teach, a book, an item that is no consumable, the line with and without its kind, a tooltip of another type, appended data, a tooltip filled again after the last source is learned, secret values, an error in the game's answer; the code from before fails it where it blanked a row, read font strings, and blanked a second item's line on a tooltip with appended data. Faults planted one at a time each failed it. That the line is a requirement line of that kind was seen once in a client, on another item (Housing: the service item, retail 12.1.5.70783); no recording holds one. The contracts `tooltip.alreadyKnown` and `tooltip.linePreCall` ask a client. Forever client (1.60.1.71037, a hunter): `tooltip.linePreCall` passes, on a bag item with one requirement line: the callback was asked once, before the line was on, the data being filled from was the item's, and with a true answer the tooltip had six lines for seven; `tooltip.alreadyKnown` is `info`, since nothing in those bags was already known, so the line itself has still not been seen there; the bags show what they showed before, and no error came. Retail client (12.1.5.70961, a mage): the kind is on a line with other words too, "Already Known" on three cosmetic weapons whose look is collected (type 43, kind 14, unmet, in the slot's and the link's tooltip), which the addon reads as known by the kind and marks `sellable`; so the kind, not the text, is what says known. Nobody has looked at an ensemble's tooltip with this in a client.
- **Open question:** ensemble 241380 taught a Monk-only source (78758, `useErrorType` 7) to a Druid, so error 7 may only block equipping and `otherNoLoot` may be too strict. Test: ensemble 139167 (Paladin-only item 144275) on a level 45+ non-Paladin plate wearer.

## Known Looks

A piece whose look is already known from another item is a second source, not a new look. Which status it gets depends on who can learn this source and on who can use the source that is known (`CaerdonItemMixin:GetCaerdonStatus`, the equipment branch; `isCompletionistItem` on the transmog info). The Plus statuses are drawn only with "same look, different item" on; with it off such a piece shows nothing to learn.

| Who can learn this source | The look is known from | Status |
|---|---|---|
| this toon | a source this toon can use | `ownPlus` |
| this toon | only a source this toon cannot use | `own`: learning it is what makes the look usable here |
| another toon | a source this toon cannot use | `otherPlus`, whether or not this toon could wear the piece |
| another toon | only a source this toon can use | `other`: nothing says the other toon has the look |

- The third row used to depend on whether this toon could wear the piece: a shield on a mage read `other` whatever was known, and with the setting off as well (author decision, 2026-11-16).
- The addon cannot ask which toon would learn a source or what that toon can use. "Known from a source this toon cannot use" stands in for "the toon it is for has the look"; where that is not known the piece stays a look to learn, because hiding a new look is the worse mistake.
- On Forever most uncollected pieces do not come this way (the collect APIs say no for them): see Transmog on Forever, whose flags call any collected source of the look a known look.
- Evidence: offline, a case per row with the setting off and on (`scenarios/equipment.lua`). In a client: the retail verdict tests `retail.appearance.knownLook`, `knownLookUnusable`, `otherToonKnownLook`, `otherToonKnownLookHidden`, `otherToonKnownLookUsable`; only the first has had a case.

## Looks After a Change

A season's piece whose own look is known can still be the way to a look that is not: raised on its upgrade track it takes the next track's look, and the Catalyst turns it into the class set's piece for its slot. Such a piece is one to keep, status `transform`, so it is not `sellable` and gets no auction icon. `CaerdonTransformLooks.lua`, asked from the equipment branch of `GetCaerdonStatus`; the answer is kept on the transmog info (`transformLook`).

- **A status of its own, not `own`** (author decision). The look takes crests or a Catalyst charge and may be a while off, so the piece is flagged, not announced: the icon is the smaller size and does not move. `own` says "learn it now".
- **The icon says where the piece is taken** (author decision; `CaerdonTransformLooks:GetRoute`, kept on `transformLook.route` and `.secondRoute` for the drawing). The Catalyst's mark (atlas `CreationCatalyst-32x32`) where the Catalyst alone gives the look; the mark the client shows over an item upgrade vendor (`Crosshair_Reforge_32`) for a piece that has to be raised. Where both have a part (a look at each, or one that takes the conversion and the raising), the vendor's mark is the icon and the Catalyst's rides on it in the upgrade badge's place: the mark alone, without the ring, at the ring's size (`caerdonBadgePlain`, `caerdonHasRouteBadge`). It was the other way round first, and the vendor's mark, which is the busier drawing, was lost at the small size on the author's screen; the Catalyst's is plain and bright and reads there (seen by him on retail). Each mark has a plain texture for a client without the atlas (`TRANSFORM_ICONS`). A draw with no word of where is the Catalyst's mark.
  - *Two ways, not three.* Recrafting gives a piece no other look, so there is no mark for it.
  - *The Icons page shows both marks* inline in the block's description, each with where it sends the piece, since a button shows one of them and nothing says what the other would be.
  - *The exact look is not on the button.* Which look, and by which steps, is in the export alone; no tooltip line says it.
- **It stands in place of `collected` only.** A piece with a look of its own to learn is what it was, and nothing is asked for it. A quest that takes the piece comes before it, as before `collected`.
- **An upgrade stays an upgrade, and its arrow stands** (author decision). The look is worked out for it all the same (`FindLookRoute` in the equipment branch, kept on `transformLook`), and where the arrow is not drawn the mark is drawn in its place: the arrow switched off, a window that shows none, an upgrade for another spec that is not shown. The status is `upgrade` either way, so the piece is not for sale. The level gain keeps its own setting and is drawn beside the mark.
- **Not a gear set's piece** (author decision). The set keeps such a piece from being sold and has a mark of its own, so the piece stays `collected` and is drawn as any set's piece is, whether the set shows as its icon, its name or not at all. Nothing is asked of the client about it. The mark comes when the piece is out of its last set, with the refresh a set's change brings (`EQUIPMENT_SETS_CHANGED`). The export still works out what the piece would gain, and says which sets hold it.
- **A look known from another item counts** only with "same look, different item" on, as for any second source. There is no Plus form.
- **Shown where the learnable icon is** (`displayInfo.ownIcon`), so the bags and bank follow that icon's setting.
- **One setting,** `Icon.ShowLooksAfterChange` ("Mark gear that can become a look I do not have", default on, its own block on the Icons page). Off: nothing is asked of the client and the piece is judged like any other whose look is known, so it can be `sellable`.
- **For this toon.** The Catalyst's answer is "this copy, this toon". A look gained by raising the piece counts where the toon's class can use the piece (`transmogInfo.canEquip`). A piece that is not bound can go to another toon, which could raise it, or convert it into its own class's set: that is not looked at, and the client has no answer for it.
- **In the Catalyst's window** the slot for the converted piece shows that piece's own verdict (`features/ItemConversion.lua`, the link the client names): a look it teaches is the ordinary `own` star there. Rests on reading.

The client has no call for "what would this look like afterwards", and the module has no table of seasons. What it goes by:

- **Upgrade.** `C_Item.GetItemUpgradeInfo(link)` names the track (`trackStringID` 972 Veteran, 973 Champion, 974 Hero, 978 Myth) and the rank. The four tracks stand for the looks of Raid Finder, Normal, Heroic and Mythic gear, which are sources of one item with the modifiers (`itemModID`) 4, 0, 1 and 3. The look to gain is the source of the same item with the next track's modifier, unless the piece has that source already. The rank at which the look turns (it differs by season) is not needed: a piece past it has the source.
  - *The other source is looked for beside the piece's own* (`FindSiblingSource`, eight IDs either way, the item checked with `GetSourceItemID` before `GetSourceInfo` is asked): the client has no call from an item and a modifier to a source, and the sources of one item are made together.
  - *Not raised:* a piece at its track's last rank, on the Myth track, on a track with no look of its own (the ones below Veteran), with a track of no ranks (what a past season's piece keeps), or with the tooltip's upgrade line greyed out (`bindingResult.upgradeLevelDisabled`: line type `ItemUpgradeLevel` in the colour `808080`).
- **Catalyst.** `C_Item.IsItemConvertibleAndValidForPlayer(location)` says whether the copy is one the Catalyst takes. What it becomes is a piece of a set of the toon's class, found among the client's sets (`C_TransmogSets.GetAllSets` with the class's mask alone, a set and its variants as one family, read once a session):
  - *by the icon* the client names for the converted piece (`C_Item.GetItemConversionOutputIcon`), which also tells a past season's set. A set piece has the icon when its item has it or one of its sources does (`C_TransmogCollection.GetSourceIcon`): a difficulty's look can have an icon of its own, and the client's icon is that of the look the converted piece has.
  - *Nothing is said until the icon is here.* The client sends for it at the first ask and names the copy when it has it (`ITEM_CONVERSION_DATA_READY` with the item's GUID). *The copy's verdict waits for that, once:* for three seconds from the first ask the answer is `pending`, the status is not ready, and when the client's word comes the queue asks again for the buttons that wait (`CaerdonWardrobe:RetryNotReadySoon`), so that one button is drawn. A copy the client sends no word for in that time is judged without the icon, and its word coming later draws the icons again (reason `conversion data`), no more than twice for one copy, so a copy the client never has an icon for cannot keep them redrawing. Before, every copy was judged without at once and everything drawn again when its word came: a window full of such pieces was a redraw of every window for each in turn (retail 12.1.5.70961, a bank opened after a login: nine full refreshes in twelve seconds, five of them in the five seconds after the bank drew). Evidence: offline (`scenarios/transform-looks.lua`: the word while the verdict waits, no word, the word after). Retail client (12.1.5.70961), a bank opened after a login: no refresh for conversion data and no redraw after the bank drew; fourteen copies' verdicts waited for the client's word; the ten draws that waited for the Catalyst's answer waited 0.7 seconds on average and 1.6 at the longest, which is the client's own time to answer, and the thirty-three that waited for a tooltip 0.2 on average and 0.4 at the longest. A guess at the set while waiting was tried and was wrong: "the newest set of the class with a piece per difficulty" named a set of the expansion before for a current helm.
  - *A set can list more than one item for a slot,* and an item can be in more than one set; an item's sources are all that any set lists for it. Where several items of a set have the icon, the one with the most sources is the set's piece; items with as many sources and the same looks are as good as each other, and with other looks nothing is said.
  - *The converted piece keeps the bonuses of the one put in,* so its source is the client's answer (`C_TransmogCollection.GetItemInfo`) for the piece's own link with the set piece's item ID in it. Where that source has another icon than the client's and one source alone of the set piece has the client's, that one is the look instead (a PVP piece's bonuses carry no difficulty). *Where the client has no data for the set piece, it is asked for and no verdict is made* (the answer is `pending`, the status says not ready and keeps nothing, and the queue asks again; the data is back two frames after it is asked for). It is asked for every time it is found missing, because the client's item cache drops data again (notes: Upgrades, the item cache), and a set piece that was here is gone once a window full of items has drawn. It used to be asked for once a session, with "no look to gain" answered meanwhile and the icons drawn again when it came (reason `converted piece`): with its data dropped later, the piece had no look to gain for the rest of the session and was `sellable`, which is the one thing it must not be. Seen (retail 12.1.5.70961): the export of a belt in a bank, whose converted piece has two sources that are both missing from the collection, ended in "the client has no data for item ... yet" and "a look to gain: none", 4774 item answers into the session. The belt's `sellable` did not come from this: with the rule in, it was `sellable` still, from a verdict made while the data was there (next point). The verdict keeps what the look code answered when it was made (`transformLookWhy`, `transformLookAt`), and the export prints that before what it asks afresh: the two can differ, and only the first is what the icon came from.
  - *More than one set of the class can have a piece with the icon,* and the first is not always the Catalyst's. A season's PVP set shares its looks' icons with the class set and is listed before it: for a belt on the Champion track the search took the PVP set's belt (two items of the same two looks, sources with the modifiers 159 and 160), for which the client names no source by the converted link, where the Catalyst's own window names the class set's belt, an item with four sources of the modifiers 0, 1, 3 and 4, and its Normal source (retail 12.1.5.70961, the converted piece copied from the window's output slot). So the set's piece is the one with the icon *that the client names a source of* for the piece's link with its item in it; a set whose piece it names none for, or names another item's source for, is passed over and the next set with the icon is asked. Where no set is left, nothing is said. A set piece whose data is not in the cache is asked for and waited for before it is passed over. Before, the first set with the icon was the answer, and such a piece had no look to gain and was `sellable`: eleven pieces in one bank. A rule tried in between, that the piece has a look to gain where every source of the set piece found is missing, was taken out again: it reasoned from the wrong item and was right by chance. Evidence: offline (`scenarios/transform-looks.lua`: no source named, another item's source named, two sets with the icon; five faults planted, each caught). Retail client (12.1.5.70961): with the rule in, the belt is `transform`; the verdict's own record passes the PVP set's belt over ("not this set's piece"), takes the class set's (item 250021, four sources) and names its Normal source, the one the Catalyst's window had named for the converted piece. A set piece the client says it has no data to give for is not asked about again and the piece is judged without it, as before. Evidence: offline (`scenarios/transform-looks.lua`: no data, the data gone again, none to be had; the code from before fails nine of its checks); not seen in a client yet.
  - *Converted and then raised* is a third look: the set piece's source for the next track's modifier, for a piece that can still be raised.
- **Asked of the copy.** A bare link has no copy to ask the Catalyst about, so a season's armor in a slot the Catalyst takes that lies somewhere has a cache entry of its own (`|x`, `CaerdonTransformLooks:MayBeConverted`; see Sellable Rules, "a new fact that is asked of the copy"). "A season's" is told by the link having upgrade info of any kind, asked once per link in a session. On WoW Forever no item is expected to have it, so nothing further is asked there and no entry is split.
- **Where an answer is missing or does not fit the others, nothing is said,** and the piece is judged as before: no icon yet, no set with the icon, items of a set that have the icon and not the same looks, a source named for the converted link that is not the set piece's.
- **Only for the toon's own class.** A piece bound to the account that another class would convert is not looked at.
- **Export:** `[Looks After a Change]` prints every answer for the copy: the gear sets that hold it, the track, the piece's own source and its modifier, whether the Catalyst takes it, the icon, the set and how it was told, every source of the set piece, each look's source and whether it is known, and where the piece is taken (the icon and the small mark on it). With the copy lying in the Catalyst's window it also names the piece the client gives (`C_TooltipInfo.GetItemInteractionItem`, which answers nowhere else) and says whether its source is the one worked out: the one check there is of the link with the set piece's ID in it.
- **Evidence.** Offline: `scenarios/transform-looks.lua`, a hand-made game; the drawing by two transcripts of `scenarios/draw-path.lua`, which differ in the new status alone.
  - *Retail client (12.1.5.70961), one export: a druid's honor PVP belt of the season in the bags, away from the Catalyst.* The track is named (972 is Veteran), with rank 0 of 0 and no upgrade line in the tooltip, so a track with no ranks is also what a current PVP piece has. The Catalyst's question and the converted piece's icon are both answered away from the Catalyst. The icon told the set, whose belt `C_TransmogSets.GetAllSourceIDs` lists with four sources. The client named a source of the set piece for the link with its ID in it. The mark was seen in the bags.
  - *Open from that export:* the belt's own source has the modifier 0 on a track that stands for the Raid Finder look, and the link made up from it names the set belt's modifier 0 source (Normal). The Catalyst may give the track's look instead. The verdict is wrong only where one of the two looks is known and the other is not; the export's Catalyst window line settles which it is.
  - *Retail client, a second export: a delve helm on the Hero track, rank 1 of 6, an upgrade, in the bags.* 974 is Hero and the piece's own source has the modifier 1; the item's other sources have 0, 3 and 4 and lie within three IDs of it, so the Mythic one was found beside it. 971 is Adventurer (a trinket's export). **The no-icon fallback named the wrong set there:** asked before the client had the icon, the newest set "with a piece per difficulty" for the head was one of patch 110200, the expansion before, though the belt's export shows a set of patch 120000 for the class. Why that set was passed over for the head is not known; the export now names every set passed over and why (no piece, more than one item for the slot with their icons, or the number of sources). The author then saw it in the bags: the Catalyst's mark for a moment, and with the icon here the upgrade vendor's alone, though the helm has a look to gain at both. So the icon's search found no set for a head either. The guess is gone since, and the search takes a set with several items for a slot and a look's own icon.
  - *The same helm with the search as it is now (one export, the icon here):* the set found is of patch 120100, and its head slot lists two items that have the client's icon, which is why the search as it was said nothing. The one taken has four sources with the modifiers 0, 4, 1 and 3 on four IDs in a row; the source named for the link with its ID in it is the Heroic one, as the Hero track would have it, and its icon is the client's. The mark the author saw is the vendor's with the small Catalyst's on it, and it stayed. *Not seen:* the icons drawn again at `ITEM_CONVERSION_DATA_READY` for a copy by its GUID (the client had the icon at the first ask after a reload); which the second item of the slot is (the export names the others since); a look's own icon telling a set, or choosing the look against the link.
  - *Rests on reading other addons' current code and not on a client:* the track IDs 972 as the Raid Finder look's and 973 and 978; that a piece raised to the end of its track has the next track's look; that a past season's piece has a track with no ranks, and the grey of a dead upgrade line.
  - *The two marks:* offline, `scenarios/draw-path.lua` (the mocked client has no atlas, so the plain textures are what it draws) and `scenarios/transform-looks.lua` for where a piece is taken. The Catalyst's atlas was seen in the bags. The vendor's atlas is in retail's atlas listing; no client has drawn it here, alone or as the small mark.
  - *Not known at all:* whether the sets list a cloak, bracers and boots as they do a belt, and one item alone for a head; whether the sources of one item always lie within eight IDs (two items have shown it), what reading every set costs, and whether WoW Forever really has upgrade info for no item.

- **What a link converts to is worked out once and kept for the session** (`convertedByLink` in `CaerdonTransformLooks.lua`, by link and the converted piece's icon: the set's piece and the source the converted piece has, with the notes of how the set was found, which the export repeats under "as worked out earlier this session"). Working it out needs the set piece's item data, and the client drops that again (Upgrades, the item cache), so after every refresh of everything some piece's verdict waited until the client had sent it once more: nine draws of a bank, 0.8 s on average (retail 12.1.5.70961). Whether the look is collected is asked each time, and needs no item data (an export named a set piece's sources with their state while the client had no data for the item). Not kept: that a link converts to nothing, which is cheap to find again, and the client's icon for a copy. A replay and the harness start from nothing (`ForgetEverything`); a scenario that changes the client's answers for a link calls it. Evidence: offline (`scenarios/transform-looks.lua`).
## Upgrades

`CaerdonEquipmentMixin:GetTransmogInfo()` carries the upgrade verdict and its inputs.

- **`isUpgrade`:** the verdict for what is worn, else the unique-upgrade heuristic, else `upgradeItemLevelDelta > 0` (unless unique-blocked). `pawnIdentifiedUpgrade` is true only for that verdict (yellow arrow; green means item level).
- **The arrow for what is worn is the addon's own comparison** (author decision), as the arrows for the other loadouts are: the piece's value against the worn pieces' on the one scale that is the worn gear's (`GetWornJudge`, `JudgeWorn`, on `JudgeLoadout`, which every loadout's verdict and every tooltip line is made from). Pawn gives the values and no longer the verdict for that scale.
  - *Why:* with two judges the icon and the line could differ, Pawn's own options steered the arrow (measured against the best piece it remembers instead of the worn one), and what Pawn refuses to compare could never be compared for the worn gear.
  - *The scale:* the worn loadout's (a tied gear set's, the played spec's, the leading tree's), where Pawn shows it, or hides it and it is scored all the same (below, the hidden scale). Under a gear set of no spec the hidden scale is still judged; with the scale shown, that gear is Pawn's alone to judge, as before.
  - *Pawn's verdict stays* for whatever the addon has no verdict on (no value, a trinket, the worn piece itself, a kind of weapon the scale is told not to show, a hand with nothing in it), and for every other scale that counts as the toon's own (one of the player's own making, shown beside it), but for a ranged weapon of the other kind than the one worn on a scale that weighs ranged damage (below). Pawn's word for the judged scale is left out of what its verdict stands for (`IsPawnUpgradeOffTreeOnly`, `judgedScale`).
  - *Not changed:* Pawn's item level upgrade (its "Show item level upgrades") is still Pawn's verdict and yellow. On a client with talent trees a toon that hides its leading tree's scale and shows another's is judged by Pawn on the one it shows: that scale is a better sign of what it plays than its points.
  - *Weapons are weighed by the hands they take* (`HAND_KINDS`, `WeaponComparison`; author decision), for every loadout. The client's slot for a type is one slot (the main hand for every one-hander), which is right for an item level and wrong for the scoring.
    - *A two-hander against both the pieces it would replace:* its value against the sum of the main hand's and the off hand's. Against the main hand alone a staff looked like an upgrade over a mace and shield that were worth more together. Pawn's values are sums of what a piece's stats weigh, so two pieces' values add; Pawn adds them itself for its total.
    - *A one-hander against a two-hander, with a partner:* its value and that of the best piece carried for the other hand (`BestPartner`), against the two-hander's. By itself where nothing is carried. The partner is a piece in the bags that Pawn says the toon can equip (`CanEquip`); the bank is not at hand. Two weapons are a pair only for a toon that holds one in each hand (`CanDualWield`, in both listings), and then one of them has to be for either hand.
    - *Like against like:* a one-hander against the main hand's piece, and against the weaker hand for a toon that holds two weapons, as Pawn has it; a piece for the off hand against the off hand's. With no verdict where a hand is empty, Pawn's stands, which calls a piece for an empty hand an upgrade (the one empty hand the addon fills is the off hand of a scale with a half for it, below).
    - *A scale that puts no weapon in the off hand* (`ScaleTakesOffHand`: the scale's `IsOffHand` is Pawn's "ignore"). Pawn's classic scales for a rogue's three trees are such, with a fourth scale for the off hand's weapon, and so are those for a protection warrior and paladin, who hold a shield there. On one a one-hander is measured against the main hand's piece only, as Pawn measures it; a second weapon is no partner for it against a two-hander; and neither a weapon for the off hand nor a two-hander against two weapons is weighed. A shield or a held item is weighed there as anywhere, where the scale gives it a value: a tank's weapon and shield against a two-hander is the case the pair rule is for. The off hand's weapon is scored on the scale's other half, where it has one (next point). Seen before the rule (WoW Forever 1.60.1.70959, a rogue with two weapons): a dagger worth less than the main hand's sword on the tree's scale was called an upgrade against the off hand's blade, where Pawn said none.
    - *The off hand's weapon has a scale of its own there, and the addon scores it* (`OffHandScale`, `HandsApart`, `JudgeOffHand`; author decision). Pawn's classic scales for a rogue's trees come with one more, "Rogue: Off Hand": a scale for the class and for no tree that leaves out the main hand's weapons (its `MainHandDps` is "ignore") as the trees' leave out the off hand's. The two are one set of weights in two halves, a scale having one weight for a stat. The half is found by that shape and not by a name: the toon's class, no tree, a provider's, main-hand weapons ignored and the off hand's not; of several the one from the provider of the loadout's own scale.
      - *What is judged:* for a toon that holds a weapon in each hand (`CanDualWield`), a one-hander for either hand has up to two verdicts, one a hand: the main hand's on the loadout's scale, as before, and the off hand's on the half, against the off hand's piece and that piece alone. A weapon for the off hand alone has the second only. The piece is an upgrade where either hand's is (`EitherHand`). Every loadout is judged so: a tied gear set against its own piece for the off hand.
      - *Why not Pawn's verdict for that scale:* Pawn measures a one-hander on it against the weaker of both weapons, the main hand's valued as an off hand's would be (a weapon for the main hand alone is worth nothing there, so anything beats it); it is there only while Pawn shows the scale; and the addon had no line for it. The addon's verdict for the hand does not go by whether Pawn shows the half (author decision): Pawn's switch decides what Pawn writes, as with the played spec's hidden scale.
      - *Whether Pawn shows the half or not,* as the played spec's scale is scored where Pawn hides it: it is the other half of the scale the worn gear is judged on, not a scale to choose, and a value is asked by the scale's name.
      - *Pawn's word for the half is left out wherever the addon has a verdict of its own for the hand* (`judgedScales` in `IsPawnUpgradeOffTreeOnly`), and so is its tooltip note for it, whether the addon's verdict is an upgrade, with its line, or none (`GetOffHandScaleJudged`): left in, the note said "upgrade" under an icon with no arrow. For any other piece Pawn's verdict on the half stands as it did (the half gives a ring a value too). Its "your best" marks for the half do not stand: they are left out on every piece, wherever what is worn is judged on the half (below, Pawn's marks).
      - *An off hand with nothing in it is a hand to fill,* for what is worn and beside a one-handed weapon in the main hand: the line says "upgrade" with no percentage. Elsewhere an empty hand has no verdict, the off hand not being every class's to fill; a toon whose scale has a half for the off hand's weapon has that hand.
      - *No verdict for the off hand:* a toon that holds one weapon, either scale told not to show one-handers, the loadout's own piece for either hand (a link does not tell a second copy of the main hand's weapon from the one worn, and Pawn's verdict stands there), a gear set with nothing in the off hand, a piece the half gives no value into an empty hand, and under a two-hander, where the pair rule holds.
      - *In the lines:* a grey note after the gain says the hand, "(in the main hand)" or "(in the off hand)", on each line of a weapon whose hands are judged apart. The off hand's comparison tooltip has the gain or the loss on the half, where it had no line; a listing's percentage is the better hand's. The export's `wornVerdict` has the off hand's numbers, its verdict and Pawn's word for the half after the main hand's, or why that hand has no verdict; `offHandScale` names the half found, whether Pawn shows it and whether the toon holds two weapons.
      - Evidence: offline (`scenarios/pawn-scales.lua`, 12c, on a stand-in for such a pair of scales). The scales' shape is read in Pawn 2.14.4 (`ClassicHawsJon.lua`: the rogue's four in each classic client's block, WoW Forever's among them; no other class has such a half there, and no retail scale). Forever client (1.60.1.70959, a rogue with a sword in the main hand and a blade in the off hand, the half hidden in Pawn): the half is found, and a dagger worth less than the sword on the tree's scale (63.68 against 71.46) and more than the blade on the half (150.30 against 137.00) is an upgrade, with the arrow in the auction house's column; both of the dagger's values are what the two scales' weights give, and each hand's comparison tooltip had a line of the addon's. With the half shown in Pawn: the dagger's tooltip had the addon's line with its hand note and no note of Pawn's, the off hand's comparison tooltip the gain and the main hand's the loss, and Pawn's "your best" mark for the half was gone from the off hand's worn piece. With nothing in the off hand a one-hander was an upgrade for that hand. A dagger worth less than either hand's piece (63.35 against the sword's 71.46 on the tree's scale, 124.24 against the blade's 137.00 on the half) had no arrow while Pawn, showing the half, called it an upgrade there: by the half's weights the sword is worth 110.83 on it, the weaker of the two, which is what Pawn measured against. A weapon for the off hand alone has been in no client, nor have a listing's percentage and a tied gear set. A recording does not carry a scale's weights (the recorder's outline of Pawn's table goes one level down), so a rogue's weapon would not replay as it was recorded.
    - *Not weighed:* a two-hander against a ranged weapon in the main hand (retail's bows), as Pawn has it; two two-handers in two hands are measured against the main hand's.
    - *The arrow of a piece for the off hand under a two-hander* was always held back (it cannot be held beside one); it stands where the pair is the yellow upgrade.
    - *A verdict that went by a partner depends on the bags,* where only the slot that changed is drawn again: a bag event with other one-handers carried than were read refreshes ("weapons carried", about the worn gear), and only once a partner was looked for since the last refresh.
    - *In the lines:* a grey note after the gain, "(for both hands)", "(with <the partner's name>)", "(with the other hand empty)"; the same in a comparison tooltip, where a two-hander stands against the off hand's piece as well. The export's `wornVerdict` and `pawnComparison` say "in both hands" and name the partner.
    - Evidence: offline (`scenarios/pawn-scales.lua`, 12b). Forever client (1.60.1.70959): a rogue's one-hander measured against the main hand's piece alone on its tree's scale, the addon and Pawn agreeing; a hunter's one-hander weighed by itself against the two-hander worn, where Pawn has no verdict, with the note "(with the other hand empty)" in its line. Not seen: a partner from the bags and the refresh when one arrives, a two-hander against two pieces, a shield as a partner.
  - *A thrown weapon and a weapon that shoots are not weighed against each other* on a scale that gives a ranged weapon's damage a weight, which is a hunter's (`RANGED_KINDS`, `ScaleWeighsRangedDamage`, `UnlikeRangedPiece`, `UnlikeRangedScales`; author decision). A weapon that shoots is a bow, a gun or a crossbow (a wand has their equip location; no class holds a wand and a thrown weapon).
    - *Why:* with a thrown weapon in the slot the class's shots cannot be used (seen, WoW Forever 1.60.1.70959: they ask for a ranged weapon), and what a bow shoots adds damage that is in no weapon's value (an arrow's "Adds 7.5 damage per second"). Pawn compares the two as any two pieces for the slot, scores a thrown weapon's damage with the weight a bow's has, and has no slot for ammunition (read in Pawn 2.14.4). Seen before the rule: a thrown weapon worth 78.00 against a bow worth 74.61 on a hunter's scale was Pawn's upgrade and had the yellow arrow.
    - *What it does:* the addon's verdict for a loadout is "no upgrade" for a ranged weapon of the other kind than the loadout's piece for the slot, in both directions, with the note "unlike" in place of a comparison. A gear set is asked by its own piece: a thrown weapon can be the upgrade of a set that holds one. Pawn's word does not count on any scale it shows that weighs ranged damage, where the piece worn is of the other kind (its verdict is against what is worn): not for the yellow arrow, not as another spec's upgrade, and its tooltip note for such a scale gives way (`GetScalesNotWeighed`). A higher item level gives no arrow either, with or without a scale the addon judges. The comparison tooltip of the worn piece has no gain, a listing none, and the auction house makes no room for a column.
    - *Which scales:* one whose `RangedDps`, `RangedMinDamage` or `RangedMaxDamage` is above zero. On any other the slot carries stats, a warrior's or a rogue's, and the two are compared as any pieces; Pawn's verdict on such a scale stands, one of the player's own making among them. With nothing in the slot there is no other kind, and the piece fills it.
    - *Not done:* adding what the ammunition carried is worth to the bow's side. With the shots gone there is nothing to weigh it against.
    - *In the export:* `wornVerdict` says "not weighed" with both values, and names the other scales on which Pawn's upgrade was not counted.
    - Evidence: offline (`scenarios/pawn-scales.lua`, 12d). Forever client (1.60.1.70959, a hunter with a bow worn): with a thrown weapon equipped the shots ask for a ranged weapon, and with the rule a thrown weapon worth more than the bow on the tree's scale is no upgrade. A thrown weapon worn, a second scale shown and a scale of the player's own have been in no client. Like the off hand's scale, it goes by a scale's weights, which a recording does not carry.
  - *A scale's values are kept until Pawn says they changed* (`otherSpecValues`), so a test that changes a value has to say so as Pawn would.
  - *Export:* `wornVerdict` has the addon's comparison with its numbers and Pawn's own word for that scale side by side, and the other scales Pawn calls the piece an upgrade for. For a row known by a link that is not its own it says the comparison is not made: it printed the numbers of the item without the row's suffix, which the status had not used. Evidence: offline (`scenarios/pawn-scales.lua`: the two agreeing, each alone, each against the other). The recordings of four WoW Forever items ask Pawn for a value their recording does not have; they replay as before and are worth recording again.
- **`equalItemLevelEquipped`:** same item level as equipped and equippable. Such a collected item is not `sellable`; it shows the neutral balance icon (`Icon.SameLevelBehavior`) unless it is off-spec, a tabard / shirt, or in a gear set.
  - **Not for a piece the toon cannot wear now.** Red text in the tooltip (mail before the proficiency, on a toon that has the piece's level) takes both away: by the time the piece can be worn it has been outgrown (author decision). The status code says so on the transmog info (`unusableNow`), for itself and for the drawing; `canEquipForPlayer` cannot carry it, because both read it with `canEquip` as the fallback, which is true for a piece the class can use one day. Evidence: offline for the status (`scenarios/equipment-once.lua`); the drawing's test of the same field rests on reading.
- **`identicalToEquipped`:** the exception to the line above. A tie is only worth keeping when the piece does something the equipped one does not, so a piece that matches every equipped piece it ties is a duplicate: no protection and no balance icon under any `SameLevelBehavior`, and it counts as redundant (its use or equip effect is one the toon already has). Two checks, both must pass (`IsIdenticalToTiedEquipped`):
  - `C_Item.GetItemStats` tables equal. The table has no weapon speed or damage range, no effects and no set bonus, so it cannot decide alone; when either item has no table the lines decide.
