  - `C_TooltipInfo.GetHyperlink` lines equal as text, in order. Ignored: name, binding, sell price, item level, flavor text, usage requirements, blank lines and the durability line. Everything else counts, including the slot line's armor / weapon type and any line type not listed, so a doubt reads as "different".
  - An enchant on the equipped piece alone is skipped; one on the candidate counts. With two slots, a tie against a different piece in the other slot keeps the protection. Empty line lists (data not loaded) never match.
  - A different item level with the same stats needs no rule here: the upgrade verdict or `betterItemEquipped` already decides it.
  - **Export:** `identicalToEquipped` and `equippedLineComparison` (per slot: what matched, or the first difference). Confirmed in game for two plain cloaks with the same armor; the rest is offline, and what `GetItemStats` returns on Forever (armor, enchants) has not been recorded.
- **`canEquipForPlayer`:** Blizzard's collect logic plus the tooltip scan. **`upgradeMatchesSpec`:** false for a piece the client lists for other specs than the toon's loot spec (`C_Item.GetItemSpecInfo`): a weapon or an off-hand with another primary stat, a trinket for another role. Such a piece is judged as any piece is, and what it is drawn as goes by who calls it an upgrade.
  - *The played spec's scale calls it an upgrade (Pawn's verdict, `pawnIdentifiedUpgrade`): one for what is worn* (author decision). The scale has weighed the piece with the stat that does nothing for the spec at nothing, so the number is a fair one, and the player is to know of it wherever the piece turns up. It is drawn whatever "Also for gear I am not wearing" says, arrow or badge and level gain, and in the other spec's red, not Pawn's yellow: the icon is all a loot roll window shows, and the client itself counts a Need for such a piece as off-spec (retail: `Enum.EncounterLootDropRollState.NeedOffSpec`, "Need (Off-Spec)" in `LOOT_ROLL_NEED_SELF_OFF_SPEC`). Its tooltip line and its comparison line are the worn gear's, in that red, and say whose gear the piece is after the gain, in grey as the client greys the stat: "(Balance / Restoration gear)" (`OtherLootSpecsOf`, `OtherSpecNote`; a loss says no more than a loss). Pawn's own note for the scale gives way to that line as to any line for what is worn.
  - *Only its item level makes it an upgrade: one for another spec.* Item level knows nothing of the stat. Drawn only with "Also for gear I am not wearing" on (`Icon.ShowOtherSpecUpgrades`, next point), red, with the level gain. With it off, which is the default, it gets neither; its status stays the upgrade it is, so it is not offered for sale.
  - Evidence: offline (`scenarios/draw-path.lua`, and the drawing compared call by call with the code from before: the one case that is now drawn differs, and it is drawn as with the setting on under every status; `scenarios/pawn-scales.lua`). Retail client (12.1.5.70961), before the lines went by the arrow: an Intellect staff on a Guardian druid had the red arrow, Pawn's "+3%" note alone in its tooltip and nothing in the comparison. Not seen in a client since.
- **A gear set's piece that is an upgrade** keeps what says it is a set's: with the gear set icon on, that icon stays and the arrow rides on it as the small badge; with only the set's name shown, the piece gets the arrow beside the name. The level gain is drawn in both. `Icon.ShowUpgradeOnGearSets` (default on, "On gear set pieces" under the upgrade arrow) switches the arrow and the badge off for set pieces, which leaves the set's icon or name and the level gain. On by default (author decision): a piece of another set that beats what is worn is one to save into this set too, and a set worn for another purpose (PVP) is told apart by its mark or its scale, not by hiding the arrows.
  - *An upgrade for another spec only gets neither on a set's piece,* whatever the setting: one that only another spec's Pawn scale sees (`pawnOffTreeUpgrade`), or gear for another loot spec that only its item level makes an upgrade (`upgradeMatchesSpec == false` without `pawnIdentifiedUpgrade`). The set is where the player put the piece for that spec, so the red arrow says nothing new there. A piece of no set keeps it.
  - *Why a setting:* a set is often what another tool chose for the slot, and its verdicts need not agree with Pawn's; a player who trusts the set wants no arrow on its pieces. The cost of having none is a piece saved in one set that would be better in the set being worn, which then shows nothing unless its item level is higher.
  - *The set's icon used to be drawn for the status `collected` only,* so a set piece lost it for as long as it was an upgrade (worn piece taken off, War Mode on with a PVP set in the bags). It is drawn for the upgrade statuses too, whatever the setting.
  - Evidence: offline (`scenarios/draw-path.lua`; with the setting off the drawing is call for call that of the code from before, apart from the set's icon on an upgrade). Not seen in a client.
- **Item sets (tier bonuses) are not part of any verdict; the piece's tooltip says what wearing it would do to one (Set Bonuses), and the export has the readings** (`[Set Bonuses]`, for a piece of equipment). An upgrade is scored piece against piece, so a piece that would break a set bonus still looks like one. What the client says: the item info names the piece's item set (its 16th value, `C_Item.GetItemSetInfo` for the name), `C_Item.GetSetBonusesForSpecializationByItemID` lists the bonus spells for a spec, and the tooltip has the set's line with the count worn (`ITEM_SET_NAME`), its pieces (one not worn is a `DisabledLine`) and its bonuses. A bonus the toon lacks is written with its piece count (`ITEM_SET_BONUS_GRAY`), one it has is not (`ITEM_SET_BONUS`), and no function found in either flavor gives the count, so whether taking a piece off loses a bonus cannot be read off directly: the count is remembered from a tooltip that showed it. The section also lists the worn pieces by item set and what is worn with the same equip location. Offline (`scenarios/export.lua`). Retail client (12.1.5.70961, a druid wearing four of a five-piece PVP set, the fifth in the bags): the section names the set, its two bonus spells, the count "4 of 5", each piece worn or not, and both bonuses as ones the toon has, neither with a piece count; the worn pieces of three item sets are listed, a two-piece trinket set among them. With three worn, the same piece's tooltip has the first bonus as one the toon has and the second as "(4) Set: ...", a `DisabledLine`; a PVE tier piece with one worn has both as "(2) Set:" and "(4) Set:". So a bonus's piece count is there whenever the bonus is not active, which it is for every set before the toon has collected it: the counts seen are remembered, and the tooltip's line says "may" where none was. The count worn in the set's line is the one to use; the number after the slash and the list of pieces are not the set's size (the same set read 4/5 and then 3/8, another 1/2). The bonus spells' names say "2pc" and "4pc" for a class set and nothing of the kind for the PVP set. Forever has not printed it.
- **Tabards** never show as upgrades.
- **Legendaries** with a use or equip effect are never `sellable`.
- **Pawn vs. item level:** `betterItemEquipped` only overrides a Pawn upgrade when PVP scaling took part (`HasBetterOrEqualEquippedItem`'s second value). Otherwise Pawn's stat weights win.
- **A PVP floor counts only where the client applies it** (`IsInPvpContext`: a battleground, an arena, War Mode), for the level comparison and for "PVP scaling took part" alike. Two pieces that both carry a floor used to be compared by their floors anywhere: a crafted PVP ring of level 242 (floor 298) showed "+13" and the item-level arrow over a worn PVP ring (floor 285) that is the higher of the two outside PVP. Evidence: a retail export of that ring out of War Mode, with the worn ring's name from another addon's record; offline `scenarios/equipment-once.lua`. Not seen in a client since.
  - *The floor is a level and nothing more: the client does not give a piece's stats at it.* Seen (retail 12.1.5.70961, a head piece of level 276 with a floor of 298, in the bags): with War Mode on, `C_Item.GetItemStats` for the link, `C_Item.GetDetailedItemLevelInfo` and every line of the slot's and the link's tooltip are what they are with it off. Pawn scores the link, so its values are the piece's own level's in every context. Not asked: a worn piece's slot tooltip in War Mode.
  - *A link for the piece at another level can be made, by its own level bonus.* One of a seasonal piece's bonus IDs carries its level: left out, the piece answers with its base level (the head piece: 12798, 276 with it and 44 without; its other four change nothing). The IDs near it are the season's other levels, in runs of eight, and one put in its place gives the piece at that level with the stats to match (12831: item level 298, Agility 110 to 135, Stamina 1975 to 2579, and Pawn's value on the Guardian scale 9733.08 to 12182.07). The client answers the level of any such link at once (`C_Item.GetDetailedItemLevelInfo`), so the ID for a level is found by asking, with no table of seasons; a bonus the client does not know is ignored without an error. What does not work: one more bonus ID of the old "N levels more" kind (1472 + N: the level and stats stay), and the level bonus of a dungeon journal link (3524 on the journal's link of an item of the same look: it does nothing on another piece, and in the place of the piece's own it leaves the base level). The level an ID gives is the same on every item seen (a worn piece's on an honor belt: 12798 gives both 276), and an honor piece has one as well (a belt of level 250 with a floor of 285: 12782, and 12805 gives 285); a crafted piece carries its level in two IDs. Not known: a conquest piece and a crafted PVP piece, whether two IDs for one level give the same stats, and that the game's raising of a piece in a battleground or an arena is the same as the piece at that level (expected). *The character sheet in War Mode does not show a piece at its floor* (retail 12.1.5.70961, a druid in a set of PVP gear, no player fought): with War Mode on, inside a sanctuary and outside, the Haste and Mastery ratings are what they are with it off, and taking the head piece off costs its own level's 103 and 51 in both. Agility is higher by one factor for all of the gear (1.284 on that toon: 1533 to 1968 worn, 1423 to 1827 without the head piece, the piece's share 110 to 141, where its link at the floor has 135), which is no raising of a piece by its level: 1.07 of it is the bonus of four pieces of the War Mode set ("Increases your Agility by 7% and your Stamina by 10% while in War Mode"), the remaining 1.20 is not placed. So a reading in War Mode can neither confirm nor refute the link at the floor; a reading inside a battleground would. *The levels can only be checked roughly without one:* the client has a PVP average (`GetAverageItemLevel`'s third answer, the character sheet's "PvP Item Level"), and the export of a piece with a floor sets the worn pieces' own levels and their floors beside it. On that toon the own levels come to the client's equipped average exactly (244.56) and the floors to 291.12 where the client has 291.81. The two are not the same count: the client's PVP figure stays as it is when a worn piece is taken off (equipped 244.56 to 229.75, PVP 291.81 both times), so it counts gear in the bags as the overall figure does, and a better piece in the bags accounts for a difference as well as a floor would. A worn piece is read by its slot there (`C_Item.GetCurrentItemLevel`, `C_TooltipInfo.GetInventoryItem`): by its link the client is at times without a worn piece's level and item info, for every worn piece at once, some seconds after an export that had them all. *That is the client's item cache dropping data, which it does by design:* Blizzard's own item object says so (`ItemMixin:IsDataEvictable`, "Item data could be evicted from the cache"), and its container of loads checks every item again once the last has loaded, because one may have gone meanwhile (`ContinuableContainer:RecheckEvictableContinuables`). `C_Item.GetItemInfo`, `C_Item.GetDetailedItemLevelInfo` and a tooltip by link answer only while the data is there; what a slot holds (`ItemLocation`, `C_Item.GetCurrentItemLevel`, `C_TooltipInfo.GetInventoryItem`) is answered regardless. Being worn does not keep a piece's data: in one session's exports the worn pieces and the equipped bags had item info in one export and none in the next, 22 to 38 seconds later, with as few as 13 loads asked through the addon in between. *What decides when data goes, measured (retail 12.1.5.70961, `/cwtest cache`; notes: Test Addon):* the cache holds about two thousand items and drops the least recently used. An item loaded and left alone went when about 2050 others had loaded after it, whenever it was loaded; an item touched meanwhile with `C_Item.GetItemInfo` stayed through 5500; reading `C_Item.IsItemDataCachedByID` is no use and keeps nothing. Time does nothing (75 seconds with no item event, nothing went) and neither does the Lua collector. *For a worn piece whose data is gone the client still answers* its link (`GetInventoryItemLink`, `C_Item.GetItemLink` by slot), `C_Item.GetItemInfoInstant` and the average item levels; *it does not answer* `C_Item.GetCurrentItemLevel` by slot (0), the slot's tooltip (`C_TooltipInfo.GetInventoryItem`: a line or two), nor anything by link or ID (item info, level, stats, tooltip), and `C_Item.IsItemDataCached` by slot says so. Pawn answers only what its own cache still holds. Asked for, the data is back two frames later, not in the same frame. *How soon that is reached:* 25 seconds after a reload the client had given 2304 item answers, 589 of them in one second as the toon entered the world, with the addon's own ledger knowing of 788 (777 of those the test addon's): a login passes more through the cache than it holds, and the worn pieces, loaded early and not used since, were gone by then. An export does not do it (30 answers between two). So the worn gear a comparison reads (its level, its floor, its uniqueness, Pawn's value of it) can be without data when a refresh first needs it, and all of that is read once a refresh and kept. *The rule that follows: the data of the gear a piece is compared with is a precondition of its verdict, as the piece's own is.* Before `GetTransmogInfo` begins, every worn piece is asked about (`WornGearThere`, by `GetInventoryItemID` and `C_Item.IsItemDataCachedByID`, at most once a frame while it holds); a gear set's piece in the bags when a loadout first needs it (`LoadoutBaseline`). A piece without data is asked for (`C_Item.RequestLoadItemDataByID`), the verdict being made answers `isNotReady` (with `comparedGearMissing`), which the queue's own retry picks up, and nothing made of the piece is kept. Only the client's own "not cached" counts: no answer is not that, which is also what a recording replayed by the harness gives. A piece asked for and still without data three seconds later is one the client does not give (some items never load) and is taken as it is from then on, so a verdict is never put off for good. Nothing keeps the worn pieces in the cache on purpose: each refresh uses them, and a verdict put off costs one retry. *The retry comes when the data does, not after the queue's second* (`CaerdonWardrobe:RetryWhenLoaded(itemID)`, which listens for the item's `ITEM_DATA_LOAD_RESULT` only while something is waited for, and `RetryNotReadySoon`, a twentieth of a second after it so that items asked for together are waited for together): with the second, every piece of equipment in a window waited a second for the worn gear and a piece for the Catalyst up to three, which was seen as a bank whose icons came late. Such a try is not counted against the ten of a tooltip that never loads. The counters say how many draws waited and how long (notes: Counters). The counters say how often it happens (notes: Counters). Evidence: offline (`scenarios/pawn-scales.lua`: a worn piece gone and back, one never given, a set's piece in the bags; with the rule's parts taken out one at a time, each failing it). Retail client (12.1.5.70961), the case made on purpose with `/cwtest cache evict` and the bags opened after it: the fifteen worn items were each asked for once, at one moment, none was given up, and the verdicts were made with every worn piece's level and floor there again. In ordinary play it comes and goes: in one session (the bags opened 16 seconds after a reload, a vendor, seventeen refreshes, 5831 item answers in 91 seconds) it never arose, each refresh reading the worn gear again; in others the first draw after a reload found ten worn pieces without data, or one piece twenty seconds in. Not measured on Forever, whose cache may be another. The export says of up to three such pieces what the client does have (by ID, by link, the tooltip). *A piece the client cannot say enough about is not taken for one without a floor:* nothing is kept for it, it is asked again the next time, and until then it counts as a piece with no link for its floor, so the comparison is at own levels. Before, such a piece was scored as it is beside one that was raised (offline: 98 against 50 where it is 59 against 50 at own levels and 98 against 85 at the floors), and its value was kept as the one at the floor for the rest of the refresh. A link whose raised link is already kept for the session is not touched by this. Evidence: offline (`scenarios/pawn-scales.lua`, with each part of the rule taken out failing it); not seen in a client, where the moment cannot be made on purpose.
  - *A gear set marked for PVP is scored at PVP levels in every context* (author's ask; `IsPvpSet`, `PvpLinkOf`, `LoadoutValue` in `types/EquipmentMixin.lua`). The mark is the set's second switch on the Icons page, "PvP", offered for a set that is scored (a spec's, or one tied to a scale) and holds a piece with a floor; kept with the toon (`Icon.PvpSets`, by set ID; a deleted set takes it along). For such a set's loadout, and for what is worn while it is the set worn, every piece with a floor above its level is valued as its link at the floor, the set's own piece and the one looked at alike: the bonus IDs that carry its level are found by leaving each out, and the ID that gives the floor is taken from the client's whole table (`CaerdonLevelBonuses.lua`, next point), every ID it has for that level tried on the piece, the nearest to the piece's own first. A link is used only where the client answers the floor for it, and one that leaves the piece its own quality comes first: a season has more than one run of such IDs, and one of another run can change more than the level (a rare honor ring of level 263 with a floor of 331 has 13650; no ID within 120 of it gives 331, and 12853, the client's one ID for that level, makes the ring epic). Where no ID gives the floor at the piece's own quality, one of another quality is taken, because quality does not move a piece's stats and the link is only ever scored, never shown: that ring at its own level by either of two IDs that make it epic has exactly the stats it has as a rare, and at 331 by 12853 its Stamina (2124) lies on the line of the same season's 263, 292 and 344 (retail 12.1.5.70961; one piece, which is then compared at the floors with the worn ring, 11365.67 against 7798.35). The export says when a link is of another quality and counts, for the piece, its own level by the table's other IDs by quality and by whether the stats stay. Such a link is looked for again when the table has changed. The rare honor gear with this floor is the first PVP gear a toon buys, so without this the fallback to own levels would be the common case there. *Never one piece raised and the other not* (`SetPvpMode`): a comparison is made at the floors only where every piece in it that has a floor to reach has a link, the one looked at and the loadout's for the slots; otherwise each counts at its own level, as for any other set (`loadout.plain`, with the set's values kept both ways). Seen: the ring above, scored at 263 against a worn ring at its floor, came out as no upgrade. A piece with no link is asked again when the table has changed. The link and the ID are kept for the session: they follow from the link and the client's data. The tooltip's line ends "(at PvP levels)", since Pawn's own note and the pieces' tooltips are at the open world's. Only the values change: the level comparison and its gain still count a floor in a PVP context alone, and War Mode itself changes nothing about how an unmarked set is scored. Evidence: offline (`scenarios/pawn-scales.lua` on a made-up season, `scenarios/character-settings.lua` for the switch; faults planted one at a time each failed one of them); the two exports above are what the rule was read from. No client has run it. The export's `[PVP Item Level]` has the floor, the level, the ID swapped, the link, and its stats and Pawn's values beside the piece's own.
  - *Pawn's own tooltip note gives way wherever the addon calls the piece no upgrade for what is worn* (`CaerdonEquipment:GetWornScalesJudged`, taken out of what Pawn's tooltip step is told, `features/Tooltip.lua`; author decision: no line of Pawn's that contradicts the addon's verdict, whatever gear is worn). Once the addon has a verdict of its own on the scale the worn gear is judged on, Pawn's for that scale does not count for the icon (`ownJudgedScales`), and Pawn's note says "upgrade" wherever it says anything: it said "+35% upgrade" for an open-world ring that the icon and the comparison called a quarter worse (retail 12.1.5.70961, a set marked for PVP worn: 5917 against 4370 as Pawn has the worn ring, 7798 at its floor). Where the addon says upgrade its own line takes Pawn's place already; where it has no verdict, Pawn's stands and so does its note. *And for the spec's other scales Pawn shows* (the played spec's own scale while a set tied to a scale of its own is worn, and a copy's siblings), where the player asked for the arrows for other gear: the worn gear is not judged on them, so Pawn's note there is a comparison the addon does not make, and it stood beside the addon's line for the spec's own set with another number.
  - *Pawn's "your best" marks go for the scale the worn gear is judged on, whatever kind of scale it is* (`CaerdonEquipment:GetPawnScalesJudged`). They were left out by the spec's number, and a gear set tied to a scale is no spec's by its number: with such a set worn, "Guardian: your best" stood on the worn ring shown beside the piece looked at (retail 12.1.5.70961). Pawn prints those marks only where it names no upgrade at all for the piece (`PawnAddValuesToTooltip`, 2.14.7), which is why they show on the worn piece of a comparison and not on the piece looked at. Evidence: offline.
  - *The player can have Pawn's notes back* (`Tooltip.KeepPawnUpgradeNotes`, off by default; the option sits under the switch of the addon's own lines on the Icons page; author decision). On, Pawn is told what it said itself, notes and "your best" marks, and the addon's lines are added as ever, so the two can be compared.
    - *Why the two differ is said on the page, under the option, and not in its hover text* (author decision): leaving another addon's output out is something the player has to be able to read about without looking for it. The text names the three differences: the gear set a scale is for and not what is on, PVP levels for a set marked for PVP, and weapons by what both hands would hold (a one-hander with the best one-hander in the bags against a two-hander, a two-hander against both weapons it would replace, which Pawn does not compare; `WeaponComparison`). Pawn's side is written from its source (2.14.7, `PawnIsItemAnUpgrade`: against the worn gear, or with its "track for each scale" option against the best it has seen worn, at the pieces' own levels). Keep the text true when either side changes.
    - *With the notes kept, the addon's number stands under a note of Pawn's it does not share* (`CaerdonEquipment:GetLoadoutLossList`, `AddLossLine` in `features/Tooltip.lua`; author decision): where Pawn names an upgrade on a scale and the addon calls the piece none for gear scored on it, a grey line with the name the addon's lines have, the percentage ("-24%") and the notes that say what it is by ("(at PvP levels)", the piece a weapon was weighed with). For what is worn and for the gear the upgrade lines are for; not in a comparison tooltip; none with the notes not kept. It goes by the gear and not by the scale: two gear sets can be scored on one scale, and one set's upgrade line does not stand for the other's number. The played spec's own set has none where the worn gear has a verdict on its scale, which is the answer to Pawn's note. Without it Pawn's "+35% upgrade" stood alone and read as the addon's view too.
    - Evidence for all of it: offline (`scenarios/pawn-scales.lua`, with faults planted). The tooltip, the option and the text on the page have not been seen in a client; how the text wraps there is not known.
  - *Which bonus IDs give which level is a table made by asking the client* (`CaerdonLevelBonuses.lua`, `NS.LevelBonuses`). One piece's link is asked with every ID from 1 to 3000 past the last that gave a level, in the place of its own; the level an ID gives is the same on every item seen, so one piece's answers stand for all, and whoever uses an ID checks what it gives the piece at hand. Made the first time something needs it, in the background (250 questions a frame), and saved by the client's build (`CaerdonLevelBonusData`), so that a later session asks nothing. Made anew or carried on where the client's data has moved on, which shows three ways: another build than the saved table's; a piece whose own level ID the table does not have (all of it again, once a session); a level asked for that no ID gives (the IDs after the table's end, once a session for a level). Until it is there a caller goes without (a set marked for PVP then compares at own levels), and when it is done the icons are asked again (reason `level bonus IDs`). In its place before: a search around each piece's own ID and through the gear worn and carried, which asked as much every session, in the middle of a draw, and knew only the runs the toon had gear of. *One level a making cannot show:* the level the piece asked with has with none of these IDs. An ID that gives exactly that level reads as one that gives none. A piece can have a level of its own beside its ID's (a ring of level 292 by its ID is 298 without it), and 298 is a level pieces are raised to. So the table keeps the level it is blind to, and when a piece that is not blind to it asks for that level, every ID is asked once more with that piece. What a making took (questions, seconds, frames, the slowest frame's share) is kept with the table and is in the export's line. Evidence: offline (`scenarios/pawn-scales.lua`: the making, the saving, a new session, another build, an unknown ID, a level past the end, the blind level; faults planted in it each failed the scenario but one that changed nothing). Retail client (12.1.5.70961, a druid with a set marked for PVP): the table was made by itself after a login, 4841 IDs for 309 levels, asked up to ID 17250 with the last level ID at 14048, and the icons were asked again when it was done (`level bonus IDs`, five seconds before the bags were first drawn). A ring of level 292 with a floor of 344 was raised by 13848 in the place of 12833, an ID of another run than its own (Stamina 1353 to 2466, the socket kept, Pawn 7915.66 to 12821.64), and compared at the floors with the worn ring; the same toon's head piece against the gear set of its played spec, which is not marked, stayed at its own level (9733.08 against 9630.32). A making there took 17250 questions in 0.86 s over 69 frames, the slowest frame's share 5.8 ms, and a second making gave the same 4841 IDs. A refresh of a vendor's window with a bag window open costs the same with the set marked and without (three runs of `/cwtest timing vendor`, within their noise): a piece's link at its floor is found once a session.
- **The spec being played is judged on its own scale whatever Pawn shows** (author decision; only on a client with specs). Pawn's verdict covers the scales it shows, and in its manual mode a player can hide the played spec's.
  - *What went wrong without the rule:* with no scale shown Pawn had no verdict on anything, so every piece with a higher item level got the arrow; with only other specs' scales shown, those counted as the toon's own (the old "one visible scale counts, whatever spec it names").
  - *The rule:* where no scale of the toon's own is shown (the played spec's is hidden and no custom or other-class scale is shown), the addon scores the piece on the hidden scale against the worn gear, by the comparison every loadout has (`JudgeLoadout`: unenchanted values, the weaker of two slots, Pawn's 0.5% threshold, and what Pawn refuses refused). An upgrade there is the toon's own (`pawnIdentifiedUpgrade`); a worn piece rated at least as high takes the item level's arrow (`pawnPrefersEquipped`); no verdict (a trinket, no value) leaves the item level to decide. A shown scale of another spec is then the other spec's, even shown alone.
  - *A custom scale shown instead is the player's choice:* with one shown the hidden scale is not asked.
  - *The hidden scale* is the provider's scale for the class and the played spec's index (`GetPawnOffTreeScales`' third return value). Its values are kept with the other specs' (`otherSpecValues`), since Pawn calculates a value on a scale it does not show at every call.
  - *Export:* `pawnScaleFilter` says "kept: none shown, so <scale> is scored though Pawn hides it", `pawnComparison` has the numbers with "(not shown in Pawn)".
  - *Not done for talent trees* (WoW Forever): what a hidden tree's scale should mean there has not been looked at in that client. *Evidence:* offline (`scenarios/pawn-scales.lua`, rule 9). Retail client (12.1.5.70961, a druid playing Guardian, Pawn in manual mode): with the played spec's scale hidden the arrows looked right to the author; no export of a piece in that state was taken, so the numbers have not been read.
- **"Also for gear I am not wearing" (`Icon.ShowOtherSpecUpgrades`) is every red arrow:** gear the client says is for another loot spec, an upgrade only another spec's visible Pawn scale sees, and an upgrade for a loadout found by the addon itself (another spec, or the played spec's gear set where its piece is not the one worn). Off: none of them is drawn (arrow, badge, level gain); the status stays the upgrade it is. Default: on where the client has talent trees in place of specs, off where it has specs; a player who had the loot spec option on keeps it on.
  - *The arrow's meaning is by loadout* (author decision; only on a client with specs). What is worn is the current loadout: a piece is measured against the worn gear first, and that arrow is yellow or green. Beside it the toon keeps other loadouts, each measured on its own and drawn red: another spec, and the played spec's gear set where its pieces are not the ones worn. A gear set with no spec (a PVP set) is no loadout of its own: it is judged only while it is worn, as the worn gear. A loadout never takes an arrow away from a piece that beats what is worn.
  - *Another spec, whatever Pawn shows* (`GetLoadoutUpgrades`, only for a piece that is no upgrade over what is worn). Pawn's verdict is over its visible scales, which in its automatic mode is the played spec's alone, but `PawnGetSingleValueFromItem` scores an item on any scale by name (read in Pawn 2.14.4). The piece is scored on each other spec's provider scale and compared with what that spec wears: the gear set assigned to the spec (`C_EquipmentSet.GetEquipmentSetForSpec`), or the worn gear where none is assigned or the set leaves the slot alone. Worn gear is a poor stand-in for another spec, hence the set first (author decision).
  - *A spec with a gear set is measured against the set where Pawn shows its scale too* (`GetShownSetSpecs`). Pawn's verdict for a shown scale is against the worn gear, so with another set on (PVP pieces) it called pieces upgrades for a spec whose own set has better, and the arrows came and went with what was worn. For such a spec Pawn's verdict does not count and the set decides; a verdict that names only such specs, or specs switched off, is no upgrade as far as Pawn goes (`IsPawnUpgradeOffTreeOnly`'s third value). A shown scale of a spec with no set keeps Pawn's verdict. Showing the scale still says the spec is wanted, so its set is asked with the setting off as well, as Pawn's verdict was: the piece keeps the status and the drawing leaves it out. What moves from Pawn's check to the addon's with this: the set's piece is the baseline, and a piece the client lists for other specs only gets no verdict.
  - *The played spec's gear set is a loadout wherever its piece is not the one worn* (author decision). With a PVP set on, or a piece put on that the set was not saved with, the set assigned to the spec being played is not what the yellow arrow measures, so a piece that is no upgrade over the worn gear is also scored on that spec's provider scale against the set's piece for the slot. A piece that beats it is one to save into the set, or to buy, whatever is worn in its place: whether the worn piece is another gear set's or in no set is not asked. So a set that was not updated after a new piece was put on shows arrows against its old piece; asking for another set's piece to be worn would spare that, and would show nothing for gear never saved as a set. Where every one of the set's pieces for the item's slots is the worn one, or the set leaves the slots alone, nothing is said: that is the worn gear, compared already. The spec's switch on the Icons page is not asked (it is for when another spec is played).
  - *What Pawn's own check refuses before it compares a value is refused for a loadout too* (`IsArmorTypeRefused`, `IsWeaponKindRefused`; `PawnIsItemAnUpgrade`, read in Pawn 2.14.4). The loadout scoring has Pawn's values and not its check, which can only compare with the worn gear, so it applies the two itself. Armor that is not the class's type: asked of Pawn (`PawnIsArmorBestTypeForPlayer`), kept by link until a refresh that is not about the worn gear, and not answered or kept for a piece Pawn has not read. A one-hander or an off-hand piece against a two-hander and the reverse, by the loadout's weapon where Pawn goes by the worn one, and what the scale is told not to show (`DoNotShow1HUpgrades`, `DoNotShow2HUpgrades`). Without it a two-hander was weighed against a set's main hand alone. A pair of one-handers against a two-hander is weighed by neither: a piece is judged alone.
  - *Pawn's armor rule by client* (read in Pawn 2.14.4, `Core.lua`): on retail the class's own type from level 27; on WoW Forever, which Pawn takes for a classic client, any type the class can wear, with mail and plate from level 40. Whether Forever's server gives anything for matching armor is not known. None of the loadout scoring runs there.
  - *A verdict of Pawn's that does not stand colours nothing.* Where Pawn's upgrade is only for specs switched off or specs whose gear set decides, and the piece is an upgrade by the unique-equipped rule (it would replace a lower piece of its family), the arrow is the piece's own and not the other spec's red one.
  - *Which of them:* under the option the Icons page has one list of what the toon keeps beside what it wears (`AddLoadoutList`, kept per character: see Settings, the toon's own settings): a row per gear set, then, on a client with specs, one per spec of its class that no gear set speaks for (`GetSpecsWithoutGearSet`: not a spec the client ties a set to, nor one whose scale a set is tied to). A row has a switch, and a gear set's row the scale it is scored on (next points). The switch of a spec's row and of the row of the set the client ties to that spec is the spec's (`Icon.OtherSpecs`, keyed by class file and spec index); a set tied to a scale that no spec owns has one of its own (`Icon.OtherSets`, by set ID, on until clicked); a set with neither spec nor scale is not scored on its own and has none to click. A spec or set that is off gets no upgrade from the addon's scoring and none through a scale of it that Pawn shows; the scale of a set that is off stays the set's. One list and not a grid of specs beside a list of sets (author decision): the two were one thing, a loadout, picked in two places. With talent trees in the place of specs the rows are the gear sets alone, and for a tree with no set Pawn's own list of shown scales is the choice. Offline: `scenarios/pawn-scales.lua` for the rule, `scenarios/character-settings.lua` for the page.
  - *A spec nobody chose follows the game and Pawn* (`ReadOtherSpecDefault`; author decision): every toon starts from the default, and "every spec" was arrows for specs a toon never plays. It is wanted where a gear set is assigned to it (`C_EquipmentSet.GetEquipmentSetForSpec`: the clearest sign, and what a piece is scored against), or where Pawn shows its scale by the player's choice. Not in Pawn's automatic mode (`PawnOptions.AutoSelectScales`), where Pawn shows the played spec's scale and hides every other by itself (`PawnOnSpecChanged`, read in Pawn 2.14.4); leaving manual mode Pawn shows every scale of the class, so every spec is wanted there until scales are hidden. The answer is kept by spec until the next refresh, which a gear set's assignment and a scale's showing each bring.
  - *A click stands:* a switch that was clicked is `true` or `false` in the toon's table and no longer follows anything. The page shows the default as the switch's state (`default` as a function, `CaerdonEquipment:IsOtherSpecWantedByDefault`) and writes only the switch that is clicked. There is no way back to "follow" short of the saved file.
  - *The switch's tooltip says which it is* (`OtherSpecTooltip` in `config/Icons.lua`, from `CaerdonEquipment:GetOtherSpecChoice`): on or off by the player's choice, or because of a gear set or of what Pawn shows, since a switch that is on without having been clicked reads as a choice otherwise. An option's `tooltip` can be a function for that, asked as the tooltip opens. The reasons have one set of names in the equipment code (`OTHER_SPEC_REASONS`), worded there for the export and on the page for the player.
  - *Export:* `otherSpecsWanted` under `[Transmog (Caerdon Processed)]` names each spec, on or off, and why (chosen, a gear set, Pawn's scale, Pawn's automatic mode).
  - *Not covered offline:* the guard that takes every spec as wanted on a client with talent trees (no scenario drives Pawn's scales per tree). *Seen on retail (12.1.5):* a spec with a gear set assigned shows as on with its scale hidden in Pawn; the default and the tooltip's reason looked right to the author on one druid. *Not told apart in what he looked at:* a spec with neither sign showing as off, a set assigned with the page or the bags open, Pawn's two modes.
  - *No verdict for a loadout:* a trinket, a slot the set has empty or whose piece is not to be found, no value from Pawn, a spec the piece is not for by the client's list, the set's own piece, and what Pawn's check refuses (above). With two slots the weaker one is the one to beat. The loadouts, their sets and what each has in a slot are found once per refresh (`loadoutCache`, which no refresh keeps: it follows from what is worn and from the gear sets); where a set's piece lies is asked as the slot is first needed, since a piece moves in the bags without a refresh.
  - *Pawn's values for a scale it does not show are kept across refreshes* (`otherSpecValues`, scale and item link to value). Pawn calculates such a value anew at every call, and a refresh asks for every piece again. A link names the item with its enchant and gems, so the value stands until Pawn says its scales changed (the hooks of `OnPawnChanged` drop them all). After the first draw of a piece a refresh therefore costs a table lookup per spec and the comparison.
  - *A client with talent trees has what is worn, on the leading tree's scale* (`ReadTalentTreeLoadout`; author decision, for a start). WoW Forever has no specialization to go by, so the tree with the most points (`CaerdonAPI:GetPrimaryTalentTree`) is taken for what the toon plays, and what is worn is scored on the scale Pawn ships for the class and that tree (`PawnSpecIDsForTalentTree`; a feral druid's tree has two, damage and tank: the one Pawn shows, then the lower number). That gives Forever the line in the item tooltip, the line in a comparison and the percentages of an auction house item's page, named by the tree. The worn gear is that tree's whatever gear set is on, since a set cannot be tied to a tree there, and there is no other loadout: another tree's shown scale colours the arrow as before (`GetPawnOffTreeScales`). Nothing where no tree leads (no points, or a tie) or Pawn has no scale for it. *Not the end state:* a toon that plays two trees is not told, and neither client can say which Pawn scale a gear set is for; tying a gear set to a Pawn scale is to settle both, on both clients. A feature built for retail is not finished until Forever has it. Evidence: offline (`scenarios/pawn-scales.lua`, with the client's tree readings stood in for). Forever client (1.60.1.70959, a Combat rogue): the loadout is read, by the export's `gearSetWorn` line ("the worn gear's own tooltip line: yes"); the lines themselves have not been exported.
  - *A gear set can be tied to a Pawn scale by the player* (`TiedSetScale`, `AddTiedSets`; author decision). Gear sets are the one thing both clients have: retail can tie a set to a specialization and WoW Forever to nothing (its gear set menu is the same one, `MENU_PAPERDOLL_FRAME`, without the entry), and in neither can a set say which weights it is for (a PVP set; the set for the other half of a toon that plays two trees). So the tie is the addon's, set by set, kept with the toon (`CaerdonWardrobeCharacterConfig.Icon.GearSetScales`: set ID to scale name; a scale Pawn no longer has ties nothing). A set nobody tied is as it was. A tied set:
  - *A set nobody tied is scored by its name where the name says what it is for* (`NamedSetScales`, read through `TiedSetScale`; author decision). A gear set named as a specialization of the class is, or as a talent tree is on a client with trees, with no spec assigned in the character window and no scale tied by the player, is scored on the scale Pawn ships for that spec or tree, as if the player had tied it there: every reader of a set's scale goes through the one function, so the loadouts, the switch, the page and the export follow. The client's assignment and the player's own tie say more and come first. Not for a spec the client has a set assigned to, not on a scale a set of the same kind (PVP or not) is tied to, not where two sets have the name. Names are compared without case and outer blanks. The page's dropdown says "By its name (Feral)" as its first entry, which is the one in effect; another scale is chosen below it. Evidence: offline (`scenarios/character-settings.lua` for the page on both kinds of client, `scenarios/pawn-scales.lua` for the worn set and the set kept beside it); asked for from a retail client whose sets were named after its three specs and assigned to none.
    - *that is the one worn* (`ReadActiveSet`): what is worn is its loadout, on its scale and named for the set, in both clients and before spec or tree. That scale is then the toon's own for Pawn's verdict (`GetPawnOffTreeScales`, `GetTiedScales`): scored whether Pawn shows it or not, and every other scale of the class is another's while the set is on.
    - *that no spec is tied to, and is not worn:* a loadout of its own, measured against the set's pieces, red like another spec's. Pawn's verdict for a scale tied to such a set gives way to the set (`GetShownSetSpecs`, by the scale's name), also for a scale of the player's own, which otherwise always counts for what is worn.
    - *that the client ties to a spec:* that spec's loadout as before, on the tied scale in the place of the one Pawn ships for the spec; the spec's switch still says whether it is wanted.
    - *A spec with no set of its own, whose scale a set is tied to, is no loadout beside that set:* it was measured against the worn gear for want of anything better, and would say the same piece twice by two measures.
    - A set's ID is the client's and is given again after a set is deleted, so a new set can come by a deleted one's scale: the client's delete function is hooked and takes the tie away (both clients delete through `C_EquipmentSet.DeleteEquipmentSet`, from the confirm dialog). The hook is tried protected, since the client refuses a secure hook on some functions, and the export's `gearSetScales` says whether it took. A set deleted where the addon was not running keeps its tie.
    - *A spec given to a set in the character window, or taken from it, comes with no event* (retail 12.1.5.70961: two sessions that did both recorded no refresh for it), and it changes which scale a set is scored on and which sets go by their names. The client's two functions for it are hooked the same way (`C_EquipmentSet.AssignSpecToEquipmentSet`, `UnassignEquipmentSetSpec`) and refresh as a change of what is worn does (`RefreshItems("gear sets", ..., "worn")`); the export's `gearSetScales` says whether both took. Without it the equipment code kept which sets go by their names until the next refresh, and the Icons page, opened after the character window (the settings window shuts every other), still said "By its name" of a set that had a spec by then. Evidence: offline (`scenarios/character-settings.lua`; without the hooks it fails).
    - *A click on a settings page names what it wrote:* the refresh a click brings carries the value written as its detail (`settings (Icon.OtherSets.1 = false, this toon's)`), so a session's record says which option was clicked and when. A switch found off that nobody remembers clicking could not be traced without it.
    - *Where the tie is made:* the gear set's row in the list under "Also for gear I am not wearing" (above, *Which of them*), with a dropdown that is usable whatever that option says, since the tie also decides what the worn gear is scored on: "Not tied" (or "By its specialization", for a set the client ties to a spec), then the scales Pawn has for the toon's class in the order of its specs, then every other scale that is not another class's (`GetGearSets`, `GetGearSetScaleChoices`). Not there without Pawn. See Settings, a list that changes. *A scale is one set's* (author decision), as a spec is one set's in the client: a scale another set is scored on, by a tie or through the spec the client ties that set to, is greyed in the menu and names the set. *And one more's where one of the two is marked for PVP* (author decision): the mark tells them apart, a toon need not have weights of its own for PVP, and the copy of the spec's scale that the page asked for before had the same weights. Such a scale is offered as "(also used by ...)", to the marked set and to the other alike; a second set of the same kind is still not offered it. The PvP mark is therefore offered for any set that holds a piece with a floor, scored yet or not: it has to be there before the scale can be chosen. The scoring needed nothing for it: the scale Pawn ships for the played spec stays the toon's own whatever is tied to it (`GetPawnOffTreeScales`), the marked set worn is judged on it at its floors with the spec's own set a loadout beside it, and the spec's set worn is judged on it at own levels with the marked set a loadout beside it (`scenarios/pawn-scales.lua`, "one scale"). All of this is the page's rule and not the scoring's: two sets of a kind still come by one scale where a spec is assigned in the character window after a tie, or a mark is taken away; each is then a loadout on it, and neither row greys its own. *A copy of a scale:* a scale the player copied from Pawn's own keeps its class and spec in Pawn, so by its spec it reads as the toon's own. Tied to a set that is not the one worn it is that set's all the same (`GetPawnOffTreeScales`: only a scale with a `Provider` stays the toon's own beside a tie), or Pawn's verdict on it, which is against the worn gear, gives the yellow arrow of what is worn. Seen (retail 12.1.5.70961): a copy of a druid's Guardian scale tied to a PVP set, and two rings in the bank yellow by it. Evidence: offline only (`scenarios/pawn-scales.lua`, both kinds of client stood in for); nothing changes for a toon with no set tied (the fixtures replay as before); `scenarios/character-settings.lua` for the page.
  - *Nothing worn in the slot: the line says "upgrade", with no percentage* (`emptySlot` on the entry). A piece for a slot of what is worn that has nothing in it fills the slot, and there is no piece to say by how much. Without the line the addon said nothing and Pawn's own note for the scale stayed, beside the addon's lines on every other piece (seen in the Forever client: shoulders for a rogue with none on). For what is worn alone, since a gear set with nothing for a slot is not asking for a piece, and not for a weapon's hands, where an empty off hand is not every class's to fill. A slot with a piece on that Pawn has no value for is not an empty one. Evidence: offline (`scenarios/pawn-scales.lua`). Forever client (1.60.1.70959): a helm for a rogue with none on has the line.
  - *A piece of a level the toon has not reached is an upgrade to come* (`atLevel` on an entry, `LevelToCome`; author decision). Its line stands, so that Pawn's note does not stand in its place, with the mark the addon has for "usable later" (the grey gear of `lowSkill`) where the arrow would be and "(at level N)" after the gain; so in a comparison, and in the short text of an auction house listing. Evidence: offline (`scenarios/pawn-scales.lua`). Forever client (1.60.1.70959): the line of a helm 38 levels off, as the author wanted it.
  - *On a button an upgrade to come has the mark for "not yet"* (`SetLaterMark`, in `DrawUpgradeIcon` and the badge; author decision). The addon has one mark for what the toon is not yet of a level for, the grey gear of the `lowSkill` status, and an upgrade to come has it too: in the arrow's place, with no ring, at the size and in the colour it has as that status's icon, with the level gain beside it; as the badge on a look to learn it is the gear alone, at the ring's size. Before, such a piece had the arrow in the colour that says whose upgrade it is (Pawn's yellow) on a ring a shade greener than an upgrade's, and read as one to take now (seen in the Forever client: a helm 38 levels off, in the auction house's list and on its page's button). A first answer to that greyed the arrow, which was refused: a state the addon already has a mark for takes that mark, and no new look. The item tooltip's line has the same gear (above). *And an upgrade's arrow never moves:* where the arrow or the gear is drawn in the place of a status whose own icon moves (a look to learn whose icon the window does not show, or a button that is there for the upgrade alone), the animation is not started; it was, and the arrow of a list's upgrade column rocked like a star. Evidence: offline (`scenarios/draw-path.lua`; the drawing compared call by call with the code from before: the sections that differ are those of an upgrade to come, as status and as badge, and those where the arrow or nothing stands in for a moving status). Forever client (1.60.1.70959): the upgrade column and the page's button as the author wanted them.
  - *The item tooltip says what a piece is an upgrade for and by how much* (`features/Tooltip.lua`, `CaerdonEquipment:GetLoadoutUpgradeList`): one line for what is worn and one per loadout the piece would be an upgrade for, the comparison the addon's own in every case (author decision: it is the one that knows the gear sets, and the one to be taught more), built like the line Pawn adds for a scale it shows, since the two sit in one tooltip: icon, name, an arrow, and "+16% upgrade" with the percentage a whole number. The icon and the name are the gear set's where its piece was the one to beat (a set with no icon gets the question mark), the spec's where the worn gear stood in; the name is in the scale's colour as Pawn has it (`Color` on the scale, else Pawn's default). Nothing says "gear set" in words (author decision: it added no clarity).
    - *The arrow and the gain are the button's:* the texture the addon draws on the piece's button (`Interface\Buttons\JumpUpArrow`) in the button's colour for that kind of upgrade, yellow for what is worn and red for other gear, without the round backing, which a line of text cannot lay under it. The line and the icon then read as one thing (author decision; a first version copied Pawn's green).
    - *What is worn has a line too, named for the gear set being worn* (`loadouts.worn`; author decision). The piece is scored on the played spec's scale against the worn pieces, the worn gear being a loadout of the same shape as the others with no set, so the same refusals apply. A piece put on that the set was not saved with is taken for the set's: a piece that beats it has the one yellow line, and the played spec's set has no red line of its own beside it (one line, not two: author decision). A piece that does not beat it and beats the set's saved piece has the red one. With no gear set being worn the line is named for the spec.
    - *Which gear set is being worn* (`ReadActiveSet`, `CaerdonEquipment:NoteGearSetPutOn`; author decision). The client keeps no such thing, and what is worn does not say: two sets of one toon often share most of their pieces, so the set with the largest share on can be the wrong one. It is the set the player last put on: `EQUIPMENT_SWAP_FINISHED` names it (`result`, `setID`, in both flavors' documentation), and it is kept for the toon between sessions (`CaerdonRefreshData[realm][toon].chosenGearSet`). It stands while pieces are changed by hand. It is checked against the client at every reading (`C_EquipmentSet.GetEquipmentSetInfo`: `isEquipped`, `numItems`, `numEquipped`): where it no longer exists or none was ever noted, the set on in full counts, else the one with the largest share on, above half; where it is not on in full and another set is, that one counts. Among equals the played spec's own. The export says which and why (`gearSetWorn`).
    - *A set with no spec, or another spec's, being worn gets no line of the addon's* (author decision). The played spec's scale is not that gear's and the addon has no scale for it, so nothing is said about what is worn and Pawn's own lines and arrow stand. The played spec's own set is still measured beside it, in red.
    - *The yellow line and the yellow arrow are one comparison* (`GetWornJudge`): the line stands where the worn loadout's scale is the one the addon judges for the arrow. Where the arrow is Pawn's alone the line is left out, since it would stand beside an icon that says something else.
    - *Added from Pawn's own tooltip step, after Pawn's lines* (`OweUpgradeLines`, `PawnAddsValues`). The addon's item handler is one of every addon's post calls (`TooltipDataProcessor`, in the order they registered), so a line added there lands in the middle of the tooltip. Pawn adds its lines after the tooltip's Set method has returned, in one global function, `PawnAddValuesToTooltip(tooltip, itemValues, upgradeInfo, ...)` (read in Pawn 2.14.4, the same in both clients' copies). The addon wraps that function at load: its handler only notes the item for the tooltip, and the wrap adds the lines after Pawn's own, at the bottom. A filling the handler did not see (Pawn's own windows) passes through untouched, and so does everything when reading fails. Without that function the lines are added in the handler, to the client's own item tooltips alone.
    - *Pawn's upgrade note gives way to the addon's line* (`WithoutScales`, `GetPawnScalesGivenToSets`; author decision: the addon's comparison is the one shown wherever it has one). The wrap hands Pawn its `upgradeInfo` without: every scale the addon has a line for that is the same comparison or a better one (what is worn; another spec against the worn gear or against its set), and every scale whose gear set decides, whether the addon has a line for it or not, since Pawn's note for it is against the worn gear (seen side by side: 6% against the Feral set's cloak, 2% against the worn one, one name and icon). Not for the played spec's own set: that line is against the set's saved piece, and Pawn's note for the scale is the worn gear's. A note of Pawn's for a scale the addon says nothing about stays, but for the scale for the off hand's weapon where the addon judged that hand (above, the off hand's own scale), and for a scale on which a ranged weapon is not weighed against the other kind worn (above, a thrown weapon and a weapon that shoots). The table handed on is a new one and never nil (without one Pawn says other things): Pawn prints the scale's line without a note, or none where it shows upgrades only. Nothing of Pawn's is changed with `Tooltip.ShowLoadoutUpgrades` off. What the wrap cannot reach, since Pawn settles it before that step: the blank line above Pawn's block and Pawn's coloured tooltip border, which stay for a note that was left out. Where the lines are added without the wrap, Pawn's stay and ours is left out where Pawn's has the same percentage (`saidByPawn`).
    - *Pawn's "your best" and "your second best" marks go the same way* (`GetPawnScalesJudged`, `WithoutMarks`; author decision). They are the two arguments after `upgradeInfo` (scale name to true), taken by Pawn from the pieces it has seen worn, and printed where the piece is no upgrade for any scale. For a scale the addon judges itself what the toon's best piece is, is what its gear set holds or what it wears, and "Feral: your second best" was seen above the addon's "Feral: +14% upgrade". So they are left out for the played spec's scale where the worn gear has a line of the addon's, for the other specs' scales the addon measures (with the arrows for other gear on), and for every scale it has a line for. The scale for the off hand's weapon goes with the scale it is the half of (above, the off hand's own scale): its marks are left out wherever what is worn is judged on it hand by hand, while Pawn shows it, on every piece (author decision: a ring's as a weapon's, since the mark on a ring says nothing of that hand) and so on the off hand's worn piece itself, which has no verdict of the addon's (seen: "Rogue: Off Hand: your best" on that piece's comparison tooltip, above the addon's "+10% upgrade" for the piece looked at). They stay where no off hand is judged: a toon that holds one weapon, either scale told not to show one-handers, what is worn judged by Pawn alone. A scale of the player's own making, another class's and a spec switched off keep theirs. Pawn has no setting that hides these marks alone (its tooltip modes: values and upgrades, upgrades only with the marks, values only, which puts a number per shown scale in their place), which is why this is done in the wrap and not left to a recommendation.
    - *A comparison tooltip says what the piece looked at would do in the place of the worn piece it shows* (`CaerdonEquipment:GetReplacementGains`, `AddReplacementLine`, `CatchUpComparisons`; author decision). A piece for a slot that comes twice is judged against the weaker of the two, so its own line does not say which ring it is for. The client's comparison tooltips (`ShoppingTooltip1` / `2`, `ItemRefShoppingTooltip1` / `2`) each show one worn piece, and get one line: for the loadout that is on. That is what is worn (the played spec's scale, where the worn gear is the addon's to judge); where the gear set being worn is another spec's, that set's loadout, if the set has that very piece in the slot and the arrows for other gear are on. *No line for another loadout whose set has the worn piece too* (author decision): the client's comparison is about what is on, and what the piece would be for the other loadouts is in its own tooltip, beside this one. What is worn is asked whatever specs the client lists the piece for, as in the item's own tooltip; another spec's set only for a piece listed for its spec. A gain has the arrow and colour of the line it belongs to; a loss is the signed percentage in grey.
    - *Those lines are the last of the client's stat changes* (author decision), not among Pawn's. The client builds a comparison tooltip in `TooltipComparisonManager:SetItemTooltip`: it clears the tooltip, fills it with the worn piece (`ProcessInfo`, where the addon's handler and Pawn's step run), then adds "If you replace this item" and the changes (`TooltipComparisonManager.lua`, the same in both flavors). Nothing of the addon's is called after those lines, so its handler only counts the building (`OweUpgradeLines`, `pendingBuilds`), and the lines are added when a tooltip's `OnUpdate` next runs (`CatchUpComparisons`): a comparison tooltip's own, and that of the client's item tooltips. A bag button's tooltip is set again every 0.2 s from `GameTooltip_OnUpdate`, which builds the comparison again and clears the lines; a script hooked on that `OnUpdate` runs right after it, before the frame is drawn. A comparison built from somewhere else (a window's own timer) gets its lines at the next `OnUpdate` of a watched tooltip, which can be a frame later.
    - *The pieces are read off the tooltips* (`ItemShownIn`: `TooltipUtil.GetDisplayedItem`): the worn piece off the comparison tooltip, the piece looked at off the tooltip that owns it (`GetOwner`; the manager makes the item's tooltip the owner at every comparison). Not taken from what the addon's handler saw: the client starts the comparison from a post call of its own for the item's tooltip (`TooltipDataRules.FinalizeItemTooltip`), registered as its files load and so before every addon's, so the comparison tooltips are built before the addon's handler has seen the piece looked at.
    - *Not a secure hook after `SetItemTooltip`.* Retail client (12.1.5.70961; a bag button, Blizzard's bags, Pawn loaded): at every refresh of the button's tooltip each comparison tooltip was built four times; the hook ran after the first and the third building and not after the second and the fourth, which cleared the line it had added, so the line was in the tooltip's count and never on screen. The addon's own handler ran in all four. Why the hook did not run is not known. The client's code asks for the comparison twice where a bag button sets its tooltip (`FinalizeItemTooltip`, and `ContainerFrameItemButtonMixin:OnUpdate` after `SetBagItem`), both through `C_TooltipComparison.CompareItem`, a protected function that calls back into `TooltipComparisonManager:CompareItem`.
    - *Export:* `[Comparison Tooltips]` has each comparison tooltip as it stands when the export is made (shown or not, its line count against the count the addon left it with, whether its last line lies inside its frame), and under it what the addon last made of it (`NoteComparison`): the two pieces, the buildings since it last looked, what it added or why nothing (the second result of `GetReplacementGains`), how often in a row, and how the tooltip stood 0.1 s after its lines were first added. Fewer lines then than the addon left it with says something built the tooltip again after the addon's turn, which is how lines were once added and never seen. It is kept as it happens: an export is made after the pointer has left the item, when the comparison is gone. None of it can be seen offline. Seen in the retail client (12.1.5.70961; a bag button, BetterBags and Pawn loaded): the lines stand under the client's stat changes, steady, with four buildings of a comparison tooltip at every refresh.
    - *The worn piece's own upgrade lines are not shown in a comparison tooltip* (author decision): what the worn piece would be for another gear set, right beside the lines of the piece in question, reads as being about that piece. Pawn's notes and marks are left out there by the same rules as anywhere. On the character sheet the worn piece's tooltip is an ordinary one and has its lines.
    - *No hook on a tooltip's `ProcessInfo`.* Adding the lines once the client had filled the tooltip was tried: the hook also ran for the tooltip Pawn reads item data from (`PawnPrivateTooltip1`, filled like any other), called Pawn for the item, and Pawn filled that tooltip again: a C stack overflow at every draw of a piece Pawn had not read yet. The handler therefore calls nothing of Pawn's, and nothing is added to a tooltip that is not shown to the player.
    - *Asked of the piece, not read off its status,* for two reasons: a piece with the yellow arrow can be an upgrade for a gear set as well, which its icon cannot say; and the tooltip's item is made from a link and never loaded through the addon, so a status made for it would be kept under the link's cache entry without its sources loaded, and served to a mail or merchant row.
    - `Tooltip.ShowLoadoutUpgrades` (Icons page, in the Upgrade block, default on) hides the lines; with `Icon.ShowOtherSpecUpgrades` off those for other gear are not said and the worn gear's is. Offline (`scenarios/pawn-scales.lua`, sections 14 and 15: the wrap against a stand-in for Pawn's function). Retail client: the line was seen in two earlier forms and the hook's error once, each of which gave a reason above. With the wrap (12.1.5.70961): the addon's red line stood at the bottom of an item tooltip, below Pawn's own lines, and no error was reported; that tooltip showed Pawn's marks beside it, which is why they are left out. Not seen: the worn gear's yellow line, a tooltip with Pawn's notes left out, the set last put on being noted.
  - *Export:* `pawnComparison` has each loadout's numbers and what they were measured against (the set by name, or the worn gear), `loadoutsCompared` lists the loadouts and, for the played spec's set, the slots where its piece is not the one worn.
  - Evidence: offline only (`scenarios/pawn-scales.lua`, `scenarios/draw-path.lua`). Not seen in a client: that Pawn's one-off value for a scale it does not show is what its tooltip would say, and what the scoring costs on a full bank. Retail client (12.1.5.70961, a druid playing Guardian with a gear set each for Feral and Guardian and one with no spec; Pawn showing both scales), with the first version of the played spec's rule (another set's piece worn): the first bag snapshot with the loadout rules differs from the one before in one piece of 117, a ring that became an upgrade for the Guardian set while another set's ring was worn, with its numbers against that set's weaker ring; so a set's location for a piece being worn does name the slot it is worn in (`C_EquipmentSet.GetItemLocations`, `EquipmentManager_GetLocationData`), for a ring at least. A cloak's export has Feral's numbers against the Feral set's cloak. Not seen in a client: the played spec's rule as it now stands (a worn piece of no set), Pawn's two refusals in the addon's scoring, the unique-equipped case, and a piece that loses Pawn's arrow because the other spec's set has the better one. On WoW Forever a gear set cannot be given a spec: the gear set's menu there has the edit entry alone (Camelot `PaperDollFrame.lua`, `GearSetEditButton_OnMouseDown`; static), though the `C_EquipmentSet` functions are all in its listing. So nothing ties a set to a scale there, and none of this runs.
- **`pawnPrefersEquipped`** blocks the item-level fallback when Pawn scores the equipped piece at least as high on every visible scale. `PawnShouldItemLinkHaveUpgradeArrowUnbudgeted` returns false for both "not better" and "cannot judge", so `PawnPrefersEquipped` compares scores itself (`PawnGetSingleValueFromItem`, the weaker of two slots, 0.5% threshold) and gives no verdict for trinkets, an empty slot, missing data or no visible scale. Equal at zero on every scale counts as "not better" (author decision; the cost is that gear a class's scales ignore never gets an item-level arrow).
- **The item-level fallback with Pawn loaded is two settings of the addon's own.** `Icon.ShowItemLevelUpgrades` ("Also higher item level gear", default on): a higher item level than the worn piece is an upgrade where Pawn has no verdict (a trinket, no values). Off: only Pawn's verdict earns an arrow. `Icon.ItemLevelOverPawn` ("Even when Pawn rates the equipped piece higher", default off): the higher level stands against Pawn's scores too, for a player after a gear score or in a mode that goes by item level. Without Pawn item level is the whole rule; the page shows both checked and greyed out then (`forcedOn` in `config/ConfigPanel.lua`).
- **A piece with no look is the toon's to use by its tooltip's equip slot line** (`IsTypeRefusedInTooltip`, in `GetTransmogInfo`). A relic, a ring, a trinket or a thrown weapon has no appearance source, and it is the source that says whether the class can use a piece, so such a piece counted as usable whatever it was. Red text in the tooltip only made it "usable later" where the level was red as well, because red text with the level not met is read as the level alone. Now the equip slot line decides for a piece with no look: where the client marks the type as not the toon's (`isValidItemType` false; the right text in red where a client has no such field), the toon cannot use it, and it is no upgrade and has no level gain at any level. A ring or a trinket has no type on that line and is as before. A piece with a look still goes by its source, so mail on a hunter under 40 keeps its arrow for later. Seen in the Forever client (1.60.1.70959): an idol, "Relic | Idol" with `isValidItemType=false`, had the arrow and +57 on a rogue of 20 (`canEquipForPlayer: true`). Nor is such a piece one to use later: the status gave any piece that is no upgrade and asks for a higher level `lowSkill`, and the refused type is now said in its own field (`typeRefused`) and leaves it with nothing to do. *No piece with no look is `lowSkill` now* (author decision): the gear stands for something to learn later, and a ring or a trinket has nothing to learn at any level. One of a level the toon has not reached that would be an upgrade is an upgrade to come; one that would not is `collected` and goes by the rules for a known piece, as a piece with a known look in that state always did. Evidence: offline (`scenarios/pawn-scales.lua`). Forever client (1.60.1.70959) after the first half: the idol's row has no arrow and no level gain (`canEquipForPlayer: false`); it then had the grey gear of `lowSkill`, which the second half took away: the row has no icon (seen there).
- **A piece known by a link that is not its own is not put to Pawn** (`IsKnownByAnotherLink`). The auction house's list of results names a piece by its item key (item, level, suffix), and the link the client gives for that key (`C_TooltipInfo.GetItemKey`'s `hyperlink`) is the plain item's: at its own level, with the stats still to be rolled ("+55 Random Stat 1", `ITEM_MOD_MODIFIED_CRAFTING_STAT_1` in both flavors). Whoever makes the item passes the piece's level (`extraData.overrideItemLevel`: the auction feature for a row; the tooltip code for a tooltip filled by `GetItemKey`, whose second argument it is, `PieceLevelOf`). Where the link's own level is another, nothing read off the link is the piece's: Pawn's arrow and scores are the plain item's, so they are not asked, the loadouts are not asked, and the piece's item level decides as it does without Pawn (`Icon.ShowItemLevelUpgrades` still switches that arrow off). Such a piece has no scored line in its tooltip and none in the comparison, which says why in the export. With the link's own level the piece is judged as its link is, unless the key names a suffix the link does not carry. *A key's suffix:* a row "of the Monkey" is that one piece, with the suffix's stats, and the link the client gives for its key is the item's without the suffix, at the piece's own level and with none of those stats. Seen in the Forever client (1.60.1.70959): the key's link of a leather helm was `item:15185::::::::20:1488:::1:3524`, named without the suffix and with its armor alone, while a listing of the row was `item:15185::::::::20:1488::1:1:12721:1:28:4550`, with +17 Agility and +17 Stamina; the row was put to Pawn as the helm with no stats, and leggings "of the Bandit" far above the worn pair had no arrow, Pawn rating the plain leggings below what was worn. So whoever makes the item also passes the key's suffix and the name the piece has with it (`extraData.keySuffix`, `keyName`: `itemName` of `C_AuctionHouse.GetItemKeyInfo`, which is what the list shows; `NoteKeySuffix` in the auction feature, `PieceSuffixOf` for a tooltip filled by key), and a link of another name is not the piece's; a name that is not known counts as another. A row with a suffix has a cache entry of its own (`|k<suffix>`). No link can be made for such a row, because it is a group and not a piece: the client's own tooltip for the key (`C_TooltipInfo.GetItemKey`) has the name with the suffix, no stat, and the line "Items in this group may vary in stats and appearance. Check the auction's tooltip before buying." Seen in the Forever client (1.60.1.70959): a ring's key had suffix 14330 and its listing the bonus ID 12751 with a modifier of its own (`item:12005::::::::20:1488::1:1:12751:1:28:5241`: +5 Agility, +11 Stamina, +12 Attack Power), and the item with the key's suffix in the link's suffix field or as a bonus ID was the plain ring, with no stats. So a row's verdict is by item level, as in retail, and the real one is on the item's page. Seen in the retail client (12.1.5.70961) before this: a piece of 328 in the list, over a worn 276, had no arrow, because Pawn rated the plain item at 219 below the worn piece, and the comparison called it far worse; the same piece opened from the list has its own link and was an upgrade of Pawn's. Until the addon's own settings took the place of Pawn's "Show item level upgrades" (above), retail's default for that option let item level stand against Pawn's scores, which hid this. Evidence: offline (`scenarios/pawn-scales.lua`). Retail client since (12.1.5.70961, "Also higher item level gear" off): the row of that piece has its level gain and no arrow, the export says "a higher item level alone gets no arrow (the addon's setting)" and that Pawn's scores were not asked, and the comparison has no line. The arrow with that setting on has not been seen. The setting's tooltip names such rows. *Not covered:* a row at its link's own level whose stats are still to be rolled. `C_Item.GetItemStats` names such stats `ITEM_MOD_MODIFIED_CRAFTING_STAT_1` and `_2` (seen in the retail client; `Link Stats (API)` in the export), which a rule for it could go by.
- **The auction house's lists have columns of the addon's own, first in the row, and what a column holds is there with the column and not without it** (`AuctionMixin:WithColumns`, `OnBrowseSetupTableBuilder`, `AskResultsRow`, `OnItemBuySetItemKey`, `CaerdonAuctionCellMixin`; author decisions). The lists are built by the client's table builder from a layout function a list is given (`ItemList:SetTableBuilderLayout`). The addon wraps that function: the client's columns, then its own (`AddUnsortableFixedWidthColumn`), moved to the front of the builder's list of columns. A column is there for a whole list at a time, so when it is there is the feature's to say. A layout the addon made is known for its own (`layoutBases`) and is not wrapped again.
  - *The results have a narrow column for the addon's icon.* The icon is on rows of every kind (a reagent's as much as a helm's). Put in from the first layout on, as the feature hooks the list.
  - *The results have a second one for the upgrade* (the arrow, and the level gain where that is shown) while a gear category is selected and its pieces are ones the toon wears. A gear category by its name, which is the client's own string in both flavors (`AUCTION_CATEGORY_WEAPONS`, `AUCTION_CATEGORY_ARMOR`); not by the detail column a category names, which is the item level in retail and the required level on Forever, and the item level for gems too. Worn: a category's filters name the item class and subclass of each of its parts (`AuctionCategoryMixin:AddFilter`, which hands them up to the parent), so one of several kinds (all armor, one-handed weapons) has rows that are, and one of a single kind (plate, one-handed axes) is asked of the first row the search found (`IsSelectedCategoryWorn`, `CaerdonEquipment:IsKindWorn`): the client does not mark the type as one the toon cannot use (the equip slot line of the item's tooltip, `isValidItemType`; red right text where a client has no such field), and it is not armor of a lower class than what is worn in its slot that Pawn would refuse (`PawnTakesArmorType`). By item level alone a lower class is never a gain, so it is Pawn's to say, and Pawn's answer differs by client (`PawnIsArmorBestTypeForPlayer`, read in Pawn 2.14.4): it refuses the wrong type where the client has armor specializations and the toon is of their level (retail, from 27), and takes cloth and leather for any class that can wear them where there are none. Pawn's library knows WoW Forever for a client of its own (`VgerCore.IsForever`) and gives it the classic rules: no specialization, mail and plate from 40. So a leather category keeps the column for a hunter there, and a cloth one for a druid; without Pawn neither does. The first row has to be of the category's kind, since the results can still be an earlier search's; what cannot be told counts as worn. Read as the client lays the list out anew (`SetupTableBuilder`) and again as results come (`UpdateBrowseResults`), since the client lays the list out before it has them.
  - *An item's listings have one for each listing's percentage,* while a piece of the item opened could be scored for what is worn (`CaerdonEquipment:CanScorePieceForWorn`), as the client's own selling page changes its columns by the item. Not for gear the toon would not wear (plate on a druid, cloth on a leather wearer where Pawn refuses the type, a kind of weapon the scale has no value for or is told not to show), and not where nothing is scored at all (no Pawn, a client without specs). What cannot be told yet, because the client or Pawn has not read the item, counts as it could.
  - *Nothing of the addon's goes on the client's own columns* (author decision). The upgrade columns have a switch (`Auction.UpgradeColumn`, "In the auction house" in the Upgrade block of the Icons page, after "In bags and bank" and under the arrow's, which it needs: `Icon.ShowUpgradeIcon`). *The icon's column has none* (author decision): every icon that can be on a row has a switch for the auction house in its own block (`Icon.ShowLearnable.Auction`, `Icon.ShowLearnableByOther.Auction`, `Icon.ShowOldExpansion.Auction`; `AuctionIcons`), and the column is there while one of them is on. A switch for the column beside those said the same thing twice. With none of them on the results have no icon of the addon's.
