An icon with no switch of its own goes with the star's there (`FOLLOWS_THE_STAR`: owned decor, a conduit that is an upgrade), on a row and on the item's own button, so that the three switches say whether the column would hold anything; in every other window those two are drawn as before, with no switch. The grey gear of something to learn later needs no such word: it is the star's stand-in in every window (Settings). Without the upgrade column, by its switch or because the category has none (another category, a search across everything, gear the toon does not wear), a row has no arrow and no gain, and an item's listings no percentage. Before the columns the icon lay on the price column, and the level gain on the favorite star (seen in the retail client); a first version of the switches put them back there, which was refused.
  - *The results' upgrade column is left out where the player's own settings leave nothing to put in it* (author decision; `CouldResultsHoldUpgrade`). It can hold the level gain where that is shown (`Icon.ShowUpgradeLevelDelta`), and the arrow of a higher item level where that counts (`CaerdonEquipment:CountsItemLevel`: always without Pawn, `Icon.ShowItemLevelUpgrades` with it). With neither, an arrow can only be Pawn's, and Pawn is asked only about a row whose link is the piece's own (a piece known by another link, above): the column is held back until such a row is among the results found. That is known of a row once it has been drawn, since the link is the one the row's item was made with, and it is kept by item key (`NoteRowLink`). The first such row brings the column on the next frame, a row being drawn from inside the list's own layout, and rows drawn in the same frame owe one layout between them. The switch for item level arrows so keeps its one meaning, and a row of the results is no exception to it.
  - *The two switches are for the two lists' columns alone* (author decision). The addon's other icons in the auction house follow the Icons page like any item button: the big icon of an item's page, of a selected auction and of the item being posted, and the rows of the player's own auctions. They are there so that nobody sells what they might still want to learn. The rows of the player's own auctions have the icon at the row's left edge, beside the item's own icon in that list's first column (the item and its name; `AuctionHouseTableBuilder.GetAllAuctionsLayout`), placed by hand before the columns and not measured since.
  - *A row of the results is two buttons to the core,* one for each column (`AskResultsRow`): the row itself for the icon, and a frame of the row's own over the upgrade column for the upgrade. The feature's display rules tell them apart by the request's `auctionPart`: the icon's part has no arrow, badge or level gain (`upgradeIcon`, `levelGain` off), the upgrade's has the upgrade alone (`statusIcon` off, next point). A part whose column is not there is cleared and never asked for. Both are placed from the row's left edge, where the columns are.
  - *A column is headed by an icon, not a word* (author decision): the star of a look to learn for the icon's column, the upgrade arrow for the others, as texture markup in the header's text. The word "Upgrade" made a column as wide as the word, and a column with no header at all leaves the header bar short at its start.
  - *A cell* is a template of the addon's (`CaerdonAuctionCellTemplate`, `CaerdonWardrobe.xml`), which inherits nothing of the auction house's, since that loads on demand. A row calls `OnLineEnter` and `OnLineLeave` on every cell, so the client's plain text template will not do. Cells are pooled, and the builder hands a cell to another row whenever the table is arranged anew: a cell fills itself from its row's data, out of what the feature keeps for the page (`GetListingText`), and nothing is anchored to it from outside.
  - *A refresh* lays the results out anew only where the columns wanted have changed, and then asks for their rows on screen again.
  - *Sorting by it is not done.* The server sorts and pages the results, and a row has no value of the addon's until it is opened. An item's listings could be put in an order of the addon's own by standing in for the list's data provider, which is taking over a part of the client's list.
  - Evidence: offline (`scenarios/pawn-scales.lua`, `scenarios/default-refresh.lua`); the client's side read in both flavors' source. Retail client (12.1.5): the two columns first in the results, headed by the star and the arrow; a row as two parts, the icon in one column and the upgrade in the other; each switch off on its own; no upgrade column under a category of one kind the toon does not wear; the upgrade column left out where the settings leave nothing to put in it; an item's page with its percentages; nothing of the auction house's complained. Not seen there: an item's page of gear the toon would not wear. Forever client (1.60.1.70959, a rogue of 20 with Pawn): the two columns under Armor, headed by the star and the arrow; no upgrade column under Mail, which the rogue cannot use; the upgrade column under Cloth, a lower type than the leather worn (the first row there was for an empty slot, so this does not yet tell Pawn's rule from the slot being empty); an item's page with no percentage column, from before what is worn was scored there (the leading talent tree's scale, above: offline only). Its rows are by item, level and suffix, each named with its suffix, and a row is still a group whose listings vary in stats. After the suffix rule: a ring "of the Bandit" is not put to Pawn and goes by its level.
- **A button can be there for the upgrade alone** (`displayInfo.statusIcon.shouldShow == false`, a window's display rule). The icon of any other status is taken off again before the upgrade is drawn, so an upgrade shows as the upgrade it is (the arrow, with its level gain), whatever the status, and anything else leaves the button without an icon. For a list's own column for upgrades beside another for the icon. Evidence: offline (`scenarios/draw-path.lua`; the drawing compared call by call with the code from before, where only the new cases differ).
- **The listings of one auction house item have a percentage each, and the page's button says what they share** (`AuctionMixin:OnItemBuyInitializedFrame`, `ShowListingGain`, `NoteListing`, `UpdateListingButton`; author decision). The page a row of the results opens (`AuctionHouseFrame.ItemBuyFrame`, the same list template as the results' in both flavors) lists `C_AuctionHouse.GetItemSearchResultInfo` entries, each a piece with a link of its own. They are one item at one level, so the look to learn and the level gain are the same on every row and say nothing there. What differs is what each listing rolled, so a row has what the piece would do for what is worn, against the worn piece it gains most over (`CaerdonEquipment:GetBestReplacementGain`, which is `GetReplacementGains` for each slot): the arrow and "+12%" in the arrow's colour, where it is an upgrade (`Tooltip:GetShortGainText`), and nothing where it is not. Only with the upgrade arrow on (`Icon.ShowUpgradeIcon`).
  - *The percentage is the feature's own text, not the core's,* in a cell of the addon's column (next point), so the feature has a `Refresh()` of its own: the default for the buttons the core drew, then a walk of the listings on screen. The core's icon and level gain were first put on these rows, at the row's left and right edges, and lay on the bid and on the time left (seen in the retail client).
  - *The page's item button* is asked of the core with the best listing seen, so its arrow says that something in here is an upgrade, with that piece's look and level gain; with the first listing while none is an upgrade; and until a listing has come with an item made from the item key, as the row of the results was (`MakeItemForKey`). A listing of another item key starts the page anew. Only the rows that were filled speak for it: the listings further down the page are not read.
  - Evidence: offline (`scenarios/pawn-scales.lua`, `scenarios/default-refresh.lua`). Not seen in a client. *Not done:* a row of the results judged by the listings the client already has for it. Looking inside a row the player has not opened needs a request to the server for each (`C_AuctionHouse.SendSearchQuery`, which its documentation limits to 100 a minute and says is not for querying the whole auction house).
  - *The first setting takes the arrow and nothing else.* A piece that would be the item-level upgrade keeps `upgradeItemLevelDelta` in its transmog info while `isUpgrade` stays nil, so the level gain is drawn by its own setting (`Icon.ShowUpgradeLevelDelta`). The piece is no upgrade and is judged like any other that is none: it can be `sellable`, with the gain beside the sell icon (author decision: a bag is hard to manage when every rule has its exception, and the gain is the hint to look again). A piece Pawn's scores rate below the worn one has no gain and is `sellable` the same way, higher level or not, unless the second setting is on.
  - *Pawn's own "Show item level upgrades" is no longer read for this.* It used to decide whether Pawn's scores or the item level won (on, retail's default: the level; off: the scores), which nobody could see from the addon's page and which differed by client. By default the scores win on both now. It still changes what Pawn's arrow answers (an item level upgrade against the best level Pawn has recorded for the slot, not the worn piece), which is Pawn's verdict and drawn yellow.
  - *The client has no upgrade check of its own* in either flavor (looked up 2026-11-17: `IsContainerItemAnUpgrade` is in neither listing, and Blizzard's `UpgradeIcon` textures are set by nothing). Stat weights of the addon's own were decided against: they go stale with every balance patch, and Pawn is the tool for them.
  - Evidence: offline only (`scenarios/pawn-scales.lua`). The export says which of the two settings decided under `pawnComparison`.
- **`pawnOffTreeUpgrade`:** without specializations Pawn shows one scale per talent tree and answers "an upgrade for any visible scale". When every scale the upgrade is for belongs to a tree other than `GetPrimaryTalentTree()`, the arrow is drawn in the off-spec red, where "Also for gear I am not wearing" is on (`Icon.ShowOtherSpecUpgrades`; its default is on for a client with talent trees in place of specs, which is where these arrows were always shown). Pawn's scale list is the setting: hide a scale in Pawn and its arrows go. Nothing is ignored when no tree leads, the own tree's scale is hidden, or one scale is visible; custom scales count as the toon's own. Pawn's druid numbering is 1 Balance, 2 Feral (Damage), 3 Feral (Tank), 4 Restoration. Confirmed in game for a Beast Mastery hunter; the rest is offline.
  - *With specializations (retail) the same rule goes by the spec being played.* Pawn shows that spec's scale by itself, and a player can make another spec's visible beside it; an upgrade that only such a scale sees is flagged and drawn red as above, unless that spec has a gear set, which then decides in Pawn's place ("Also for gear I am not wearing", above). A scale's `SpecID` is the spec's index there (`GetSpecialization`), read in Pawn's `PawnGetDefaultScale`. A toon with no spec chosen (index 5) ignores nothing. The talent and specialization events refresh the icons when the spec index changed (`OnTalentsChanged`, reason `talents`), and `PLAYER_LOOT_SPEC_UPDATED` when the loot spec did (reason `loot spec`): `matchesLootSpec` is part of a kept verdict. Both are noted as the world comes and compared at each event, so an event with nothing changed costs nothing. Evidence: offline only (`scenarios/pawn-scales.lua`). Not seen in a client: which of those events come at a change of spec or loot spec, and Pawn's scales there.
- **Armor class guard:** the item-level fallback never fires for a lower armor class than the piece equipped (`IsLowerArmorClassThanEquipped`). Pawn may still flag it.
- **Upgrade badge:** an upgrade whose status became something else (a look to learn) keeps `bindingResult.upgradeStatus` (`"upgrade"` / `"upgradeLowSkill"`), taken after the red-text correction. `SetItemButtonStatus` then draws a small ring and arrow on the icon's corner (`UPGRADE_BADGE_SCALE` 65% of the icon, moved `UPGRADE_BADGE_OVERLAP` 40% in), at the corner taken from `icon:GetPoint(1)`. It follows the upgrade arrow's settings, has none of its own, and shows and hides with the icon through its `OnShow` / `OnHide` hooks. Where a window does not show the status' own icon, the item draws as a plain upgrade. Confirmed in game, including Baganator and auction rows.
- **Export:** `pawnComparison`, `pawnUpgradeScales`, `pawnScaleFilter`, `pawnOffTreeUpgrade`, `loadoutsCompared` under `[Transmog (Caerdon Processed)]`; `Talent Trees` under `[Player]`.
- **What the item is asked once.** Its item level, its PVP floor (a tooltip fetched from the client) and its uniqueness are read by the first upgrade helper that needs them and shared by the rest (`NewCandidate` in `types/EquipmentMixin.lua`). Each helper used to read them for itself, three tooltip fetches for one piece. The floor's tooltip is not fetched at all where the status is being computed: `GetCaerdonStatus` asks for the link's tooltip once and hands the answer to `GetTransmogInfo(linkTooltip)`, which reads the floor line from it. That takes a bag copy's floor from its link's tooltip instead of its slot's, which rests on the two carrying the same line (Tooltip Lines in the Export; and `equipment.keptInfo` compares the two infos at every login). A worn piece is still asked by its slot, a row with a level of its own takes no floor, and a caller without the tooltip (the export, the item tooltip) has it fetched as before. A new helper takes the candidate, not the item.
- **Equipment sets are one index, asked by the copy.** `GetEquipmentSets()` looks the item up in a table of every set's pieces in the bags and the bank, instead of walking every set times every slot for each button. A set names where each of its pieces lies (`C_EquipmentSet.GetItemLocations`), so an item that lies in a bag slot is looked up by that slot (`CaerdonEquipment:GetEquipmentSetsOf`): of two copies with one link only the one a set names is in it, and each of two sets' copies is in its own (author decision; by link, a spare copy had its twin's sets). An item known only by its link (the mail, a merchant's row, the bags a bag addon remembers) has no copy to ask about and is in the sets of a piece with its link. The sets are part of the status cache key (`|e` and their names), since they decide the binding text and whether the piece is judged a spare. The game answers the same question for a bag slot itself (`C_Container.GetContainerItemEquipmentSetInfo`), for the bags only and with the names in one string; the addon does not take that string apart, and the contract `equipment.setsByCopy` compares the two for every bag slot. The index is built on first use and kept for as long as `GetTime()` gives the same value, which is taken to be the rest of the frame, so nothing has to tell the index that a piece moved, a set changed or the bank opened. `InvalidateCaches` drops it too, for the recorder. That `GetTime()` stands still within a frame is checked in each client by the contract `client.frameTime`; if it moved, the index would only be built more often. A worn piece is not listed, as before; the list returned belongs to the index.
- **The questions about each source's item are debug only.** The item subtype and minimum level of every source of the look feed `matchedSources` and `lowestLevelFound`, which only the debug details carry, so they are asked for with Enable debug on and not otherwise. A source item's specializations were read and never used, and are no longer asked for.
- **Evidence for the three above:** offline only (`scenarios/equipment-once.lua`: the counts of the client's questions, and that each helper still uses the answers; the fixtures replay to their recorded verdicts). Forever client (same hunter, the same seven pieces before and after): the transmog info went from 9.1 to 5.1 ms and from 1091 to 491 KB per refresh, nearly all of it the second computation that is gone; the toon has no equipment sets, so the index showed nothing there. Retail, where the sets cost most, has not timed it. The floor read from the status's tooltip is offline only (one fetch of the link's tooltip where there were two, none for a bag slot, the same verdicts; faults planted in the hand-over each made the scenario fail). Retail client (12.1.5.70961, a druid with PVP pieces in the bags, 2026-11-18): the bag snapshot of the first reload with the change is what it was before it, 115 items the same and none changed, and `equipment.keptInfo` passes; War Mode was switched on and off in that session. Forever client (1.60.1.70959, a hunter, the same day): the bag snapshot against the evening before is 40 items the same and none changed, and `equipment.keptInfo` passes on six pieces; no item there has shown a floor line, so the change is a fetch less and nothing else. The lookup by the copy is offline only (two helms of one link, each in its slot's sets, one taken out of its set and put back; faults planted in the lookup, the cache key and the contract each made the scenario fail): the contract says nothing on a toon with no set's piece in its bags, which is every Forever toon so far. Retail client (12.1.5.70961, a druid, 2026-11-18): the contract passes on 118 bag slots, 20 of them in sets and one in two (the game's string joins two names with a comma and a space), and the bag snapshot is what it was before the change, 115 items the same and none changed. The case the change is for was then made on that druid: a second copy of a set's piece bought, one of the two put in a set, and only that one showed the set's name in the bags (the author's look, no export).

## Set Bonuses

What wearing a piece would do to the bonuses of the item sets the toon wears pieces of (a tier set and the like, not a saved gear set). `CaerdonSetBonuses.lua`, entry point `CaerdonItemMixin:GetSetBonusChanges()`.

- **It is a line on the piece's tooltip and nothing else** (author decision: a warning, in both directions; the upgrade arrow does not weigh a bonus and is not held back for one). `TooltipMixin:AddSetBonusChanges` adds a line for each bonus the piece would bring ("gains the 2-piece set bonus", green) or end ("loses the 4-piece set bonus", the client's red), with the upgrade lines: after Pawn's own where Pawn is there, into the client's item tooltips where it is not, never into a comparison tooltip and not for a piece that is being worn. A piece that is no upgrade has the line too, and no Pawn is needed. `Tooltip.ShowSetBonusChanges` turns the lines off (the Upgrade block of the Icons page). No status reads any of it.
- **The client has no function for it.** None gives the pieces a bonus begins at or the pieces worn of a set (`C_Item.GetItemSetInfo` is the set's name, `C_Item.GetSetBonusesForSpecializationByItemID` the bonus spells of a spec, `C_LootJournal.GetItemSetItems` the pieces). It is on a piece's tooltip, in lines of no type of their own, written from three format strings and read back through `NS.FormatPattern`:
  - `ITEM_SET_NAME`, "Defias Leather (1/5)": the set and the pieces worn. Only the first number is used: the second, and the list of pieces under the line, changed with what was worn in a retail client (4/5 with four on, 3/8 with three).
  - `ITEM_SET_BONUS_GRAY`, "(2) Set: ...": a bonus the toon does not have, with the pieces it begins at. A greyed-out line (`DisabledLine`).
  - `ITEM_SET_BONUS`, "Set: ...": a bonus it has. No number.
  - The set's line is known by its format and by a piece's line under it (a line that begins with a blank); `ITEM_SET_LEGACY_INACTIVE_BONUS` in the block says the set's bonuses no longer work, and nothing is said of such a set.
- **A gain is read, a loss is remembered.** What a piece would bring is on its own tooltip: the pieces worn, one more, against the number of each bonus not had. What it would end is not, since a bonus the toon has is written without its number. So the number is kept from any tooltip that showed the bonus while the toon did not have it (`CaerdonSetBonusData`, saved for the account: `[set] = { name, bonuses, pieces = { [position] = pieces } }`, the set by its ID where the item info names one, by its name otherwise). Tooltips come from two places: the status code hands over the link's tooltip of every set piece it judges (`NoteTooltip`, from `GetCaerdonStatus`, which has the set's ID from the item info and asks nothing more), and the tooltip line's own readings. A piece taken off lies in the bags with fewer worn, so the number is mostly there before it is needed. Where it is not, the line says "may lose a set bonus".
  - *What is remembered is held against each reading:* a set whose tooltip has another number of bonus lines is begun anew (a spec's bonuses, a patch), and a remembered number above the pieces worn, for a bonus the toon has, is dropped.
- **One thing is taken from the tooltips seen and not from a function:** the bonuses stand in the order of their pieces, fewest first (2, 3, 4, 5 on a Forever set; 2, 4 on retail's). By it a bonus whose number is not remembered needs no more pieces than a later one whose number is, which is what keeps "may lose" off a piece where the last bonus's number is known and an earlier one's is not. A set whose remembered numbers are not ascending is not reasoned about that way. Nothing assumes 2 and 4, or that a bonus needs two pieces.
- **Where a piece can go** is `CaerdonEquipment:GetSlotChoices`: one place for most, two for a ring, a trinket, and a one-hander on a toon that holds two; a two-hander empties both hands, and so does a piece for the off hand while a two-hander is held. A piece that would not do the same in both places has each line say which worn piece it is about ("(in place of ...)", "(in the empty slot)").
- **What is worn alone is looked at,** not a saved gear set that is not on.
- **The last answer is kept** while the same link is asked again (a bag button's tooltip is filled five times a second) and dropped at `PLAYER_EQUIPMENT_CHANGED`. An answer made while a tooltip was not there yet is not kept.
- **Export:** `[Set Bonuses]` ends with what this is made from: what was read off the piece and off each worn piece it would take off, what is remembered of each bonus, and what the line would say or why there is none.
- **Tests.** `scenarios/set-bonuses.lua` (a game that writes a set's lines from what is worn, after the recorded tooltips of both clients; gains, losses, "may lose", two places, weapons, a legacy set, a set without an ID, a tooltip that is not there, the lines and their switch, where they are placed). `scenarios/locale-text.lua` reads the three lines in every locale of both clients. The contract `tooltip.setBonuses` asks a client the premises for every piece worn and carried: a piece whose item info names a set has set lines that are read, the pieces worn by the line are the worn pieces of that set, a bonus line written back with its format is the line, a bonus not had is greyed out and needs more than is worn, and the bonuses not had are in ascending order. It keeps each set's lines as seen, by pieces worn, and counts the pieces with set lines whose item info names no set.
- **Evidence.** Static: the three strings in all eleven locales of both listings; no function for the numbers in either flavor's documentation. Recorded: a Forever client's "Defias Leather (1/5)" with its four bonuses not had, a retail client's set with both bonuses had and no number (fixtures); retail exports (12.1.5.70961) of a set at three and four worn, where the second bonus read "(4) Set:" and then "Set:". Offline: the scenarios; faults planted one at a time each failed them. Forever client (1.60.1.71037, a hunter, an auction's Blackened Defias Gloves with none of the set worn): the item info names the set (161), the export's readings are the tooltip's ("Defias Leather", 0 worn, four bonuses not had at 2, 3, 4 and 5 pieces, each a `DisabledLine`), nothing is said for the first piece of a set, and `C_Item.GetSetBonusesForSpecializationByItemID` answers there too, four spells in the bonuses' order. The set's four numbers were in the saved table after the next reload (`[161]`, 2, 3, 4, 5), the bag snapshot of that session is what it was before (40 items, none changed), and the contract ran as `info`: none of 66 pieces worn and carried is of a set. **No client has shown a line, and the contract has judged no set piece.** Not known: whether a client ever lists two bonuses out of the order of their pieces.

## Sellable Rules

Equipment is `sellable` when it is `collected`, redundant or without use / equip effect / set, and not protected (what counts as a use and as an equip effect: below the list). Never sellable:

- **Fishing poles** (weapon subclass `Fishingpole`).
- **Profession tools:** a Weapon with a met skill requirement line (`bindingResult.skillRequirementMet`; armor with a profession requirement is judged normally), or anything `GetProfessionToolInfo()` names.
- **Equal item level** unless the piece is `identicalToEquipped` (see Upgrades), recraftable items, legendaries and cosmetics with an effect, tabards with a use, teleport items, artifacts.
  - **Recraftable is a fact of the copy, not of the link.** The client is asked by the copy's GUID (`CaerdonItemMixin:IsRecraftable`), so a bare link (the mail, a buyback row, a chat link) always answers no. A verdict is cached per link, and a crafted piece judged first by its link left `sellable` for its copy in the bags until the next refresh, or the other way round. So a crafted piece that lies somewhere has a cache entry of its own (`|c` in the key, `StatusCacheKey` in `CaerdonItem.lua`): its link ends in its maker's GUID, which is how it is told. A soulbound copy was already apart through `|B`. Offline (`scenarios/equipment-once.lua`, which the code from before fails in both orders). Retail client (12.1.5.70783, a druid, 2026-11-16), the code from before: nine crafted pieces in the bags, each recraftable in its slot and not by its link; one read `sellable` by its link and `collected` in its slot. Retail client with the change (the same druid): the bags are drawn as before, 127 items the same and none changed.
  - **A new fact that is asked of the copy belongs in the key the same way,** or copies of one link will take each other's verdict. `CaerdonItemMixin:GetStatusCacheKey()` says which entry a copy uses.

**A use that has to be aimed at an enemy does not keep a piece** (`CaerdonItemMixin:HasHostileUse`, which is `C_Item.IsHarmfulItem`; `keptForUseEffect` in the equipment branch).

- **Why:** a use keeps a learned piece so that nothing fun or special is marked for sale. The client does not say what a use is worth, only what it is aimed at. A use that needs an enemy is a combat effect (Torch of Holy Flame 7344: "Decrease the armor of target by 50"), outgrown like a stat; what is fun or special is mostly cast on oneself, on a friend or on nothing. It is a rule of thumb (author decision, 2026-11-17): a piece it marks wrongly is a reason to add a guard here.
- **Kept whatever the use is aimed at:** a trinket; a piece with a skill requirement line, met or not (`bindingResult.hasSkillRequirement`: an engineer's gadgets are aimed at enemies too); a piece no vendor pays for (a quest's tool: Morbent's Bane 7297, whose use is cast on Morbent Fel). The client is asked last, so none of these asks it.
- **Unchanged:** a teleport, a tabard's use and a legendary's are protected on their own; a set or an equip effect beyond a stat still keeps the piece.
- **Both flavors:** the function is on both clients and the rule is not flavor-specific.
- **Export:** `Use Spell` under `[Caerdon]`: the spell, the client's two answers (`IsHarmfulItem`, `IsHelpfulItem`) and the addon's reading.
- **Evidence.** Offline: `scenarios/hostile-use.lua`, hand-made items. Forever client (1.60.1.70959, a hunter, 2026-11-17): the torch reads `sellable`, which the rule only gives when the client calls its use harmful, and nothing else in the bags was seen to change. Not seen: a trinket, a gadget or a quest's tool in a client, and retail.

**An "Equip:" line that is a plain stat is not an equip effect for this rule** (`IsEquipStatLine` in `CaerdonItem.lua`; `bindingResult.hasEquipEffect` is any "Equip:" line, `hasEquipEffectBeyondStats` one that is more than a stat, and only the second keeps a piece).

- **Why:** Forever writes what classic gear gives beyond the primary stats (spell power, attack power, crit, mana per 5 sec) as green "Equip:" lines. Counting them as effects kept every learned caster piece from `sellable`, on any class. Such a stat is weighed like any other: by item level and Pawn, which decide upgrade, tie and redundancy before this rule is reached.
- **How a stat line is told:** the client builds it from one of its `TOOLTIP_ITEM_STAT_*` strings and hands it out as type `None`; an effect that is a spell (a quiver's attack speed) comes as `ItemSpellTriggerOnEquip` with a spell ID. So: an untyped line that matches one of those strings in full, the number matched loosely. The match is on the client's own strings, so it holds in any locale.
- **Which line is an "Equip:" line at all:** a line typed `ItemSpellTriggerOnEquip`, or an untyped line (type `None`) whose text starts with the client's `ITEM_SPELL_TRIGGER_ONEQUIP` or with the word its stat strings start with where that is another (Text Read Off a Tooltip Line), compared as plain text. The word in the middle of a line, or on a line of another type, is not one. Every line with the word in both flavors' recordings is of one of those two types and starts with it.
- **Still an effect:** a typed line whatever it says; a skill bonus (`TOOLTIP_ITEM_STAT_SKILL_*`, "Increased Fishing +3"), left out on purpose; an untyped line that matches no string; every "Equip:" line on a client without the strings. Retail has none of them, so nothing changes there, its PVP item level line included.
- **Only where a vendor pays for the piece.** Without a vendor price a stat line keeps the piece as before: a quest's piece that is traded in later (Cursed Eye of Paleth 2944) has none, and nothing else says it is still needed.
- **Legendaries, cosmetics and equippable junk** still read `hasEquipEffect`, any line.
- **The names are built at run time** (`CaerdonItem:GetEquipStatStringNames()`), so the static check does not see them; the recorder takes the list from that function. A family the list lacks stays an effect, and `scenarios/equip-stat-lines.lua` fails when the Forever listings have an "Equip:" stat string that is not named.
- **Evidence.** Forever client (1.60.1, the fixtures and two exports of a hunter): the line types and texts of attack power, spell power, healing with damage, shadow and fire damage (type `None`), a quiver's attack speed (`ItemSpellTriggerOnEquip`, with a spell ID) and a fishing pole's skill bonus (`None`). Offline: the rule on those texts, on both flavors (`scenarios/equip-stat-lines.lua`). Forever client with the change (1.60.1.70959, the same hunter, 2026-11-17): an off-hand and a cloak with a spell power line read `sellable`. Not seen: how the client writes a percentage ("1.0%" is what its format says).

Other sources of `sellable`:

- **Equippable junk** (`IsEquippableJunk`): Miscellaneous / Junk that goes in an equipment slot with no use, no equip effect, no appearance, and a vendor price. Built for the trophy fish (15 Pound Mud Snapper 6295). Keep it this narrow.
- **Consumables for another class** (`IsOtherClassConsumable`): a red `UsageRequirement` line of type `RaceClass` (`bindingResult.raceClassRestricted`). Needs a vendor price; account-bound and warbound-until-equip are skipped. Built for mage scrolls on a hunter. Not confirmed in game.
- **A Quest-class item whose quests are all behind the toon** and that has no use, no slot and no equip effect (`IsSpentQuestItem`; Quest Value).
- Recipes, food and drink, bags: their own sections.

## Profession Tools

`CaerdonProfessionTools.lua` says which items are tools of a profession the toon has; `GetProfessionToolInfo()` returns nil or `{ isTool, professions, toolName, source, match }`. A tool is never `sellable` or `auctionGoods`; nothing else changes.

- **Known items** (`KNOWN_TOOLS`): Mining Pick, Skinning Knife, Blacksmith Hammer, Arclight Spanner, Gyromatic Micro-Adjustor. Gathering has no recipe that names its tool. Gated on `C_PaperDollInfo.IsRangedSlotShown()` as a stand-in for "tools are carried in the bags".
- **Recipe tools:** `RecordRecipeTools` in `types/RecipeMixin.lua` saves every `Enum.RecipeRequirementType.Totem` name from `C_TradeSkillUI.GetRecipeRequirements` into `CaerdonRecipeData[realm][toon].tools[professionName] = { skillLine, names }`, for the toon's own profession only. A dropped profession's entry stays and is ignored.
- **Matching:** a known item, a name equal to a tool name, or, for equippable items, a name containing a tool name of 8+ characters ("Keen Skinning Knife"). The last is a guess.
- **Refresh:** new tool names and a changed set of professions (`SKILL_LINES_CHANGED`) call `RefreshItems`.
- **Not covered:** stand-ins under an unrelated name (Finkle's Skinner). Unverified: that `GetRecipeRequirements` names tools by the item's name, and that Forever-only hammers and knives work as tools.
- **Export:** `[Profession Tools]`. **In game:** the author's miner / engineer no longer sees the coin on his tools; a Skinning Knife on a toon without Skinning correctly does.

## Bags for the Bag Slots

`CaerdonBagSlots.lua` holds a bag item against every bag slot it fits; `GetBagSlotInfo()` is nil for anything that is not a bag or quiver (fields are documented above `GetItemInfo`).

- **Verdicts:** `isUpgrade` (a slot it fits is empty, or it beats the bag in one) becomes `own`, or `lowSkill` with red tooltip text. `isSpare` (every slot it fits is filled and it beats none) becomes `sellable` when it has a vendor price. Neither when a comparison cannot be decided.
- **Slots are read, not assumed.** `ReadSlot` classifies the item that is really there.
- **Kinds and the slots they fit** (confirmed in game on Forever unless noted):
  - `bag` (Container / Bag): normal slots, containers 1 to `NUM_BAG_SLOTS`.
  - `reagent` (Container / Reagent): the reagent slot, container 5. Assumed not to fit a normal slot.
  - `profession` (any other Container subclass): normal slots and, on Forever, the reagent slot. Retail's reagent slot is believed not to take them, so it only counts where `IsRangedSlotShown()` is true or once one has been seen in it.
  - `quiver` (item class Quiver; quiver and ammo pouch): normal slots only.
- **Better than the bag in a slot (`Compare`):**
  - Same kind and subclass: more slots. Equal is a spare.
  - Ordinary bag vs. profession or reagent bag: at least as many slots, since it holds anything.
  - Reagent bag vs. profession bag: more slots.
  - Undecidable, so no verdict either way: reagent bag vs. a profession bag with as much room or more; profession bag vs. a reagent bag, another profession's bag, or a smaller ordinary bag. What a reagent bag holds compared with a profession bag is unknown.
  - Quivers are only compared with their own subclass; against any bag the pair is skipped.
- **An empty slot only counts when the toon has a use for the bag (`hasUse`):** a quiver needs a bow or crossbow equipped, an ammo pouch a gun (`ReadAmmoWeapons`); a profession bag needs a profession in `PROFESSION_BAG_USERS` (the one its subclass is named after plus the crafting professions that use the same materials; a soul bag needs a warlock). One of the same kind already equipped also counts.
- **Size of an unequipped bag:** no API; the tooltip's "6 Slot Bag" line is read through the `CONTAINER_SLOTS` format.
- **Refresh:** `BAG_UPDATE_DELAYED`, `BAG_CONTAINER_UPDATE` and `SKILL_LINES_CHANGED` compare a signature of the slots and professions.
- **Settings:** `Icon.ShowBagUpgrades`, `Icon.ShowSpareBags`. **Export:** `[Bag Slots]`, printed for every item.
- **In game:** seen working on Forever (2026-11-14). Not individually confirmed: quivers, the retail gate, a profession bag on a toon without the profession.

## Recipes This Toon Has No Use For

`GetUnneededRecipeStatus()` replaces `collected` on a Recipe the toon knows or cannot learn.

- **`send`:** the recipe can be handed over (not soulbound, not bind on pickup) and another toon on the realm has the profession and the recipe in its recorded list as one not learned, at the rank the item asks for or below it, and has no copy of the item that the ledger knows of (What an Item Teaches, For another toon: the ledger counts every item of class Recipe). No vendor price needed. It is the mail icon, the same status an ingredient for another toon gets (Ingredients); it was `other` / `otherPlus` until the two were made one (author decision, 2026-11-17).
- **`sellable`:** this toon knows it and nobody else wants it, or every toon that does has a copy. Needs a vendor price; never for account-bound or warbound-until-equip.
- **Left on `collected`:** a known recipe when another toon has the profession but its recipes were never recorded, or its recorded list has no recipe of that name and no caps say more.
- **A toon below the rank counts too** (author, 2026-11-22; until then only a toon that could learn it now, his rule of 2026-11-17, so that the same recipe was not bought and sent again while the toon waited: the ledger answers that now). A toon that lacks the recipe is in `missing` when its saved rank meets the item's "Requires Cooking (125)" line (`CaerdonItemMixin:GetSkillRequirement`, the tooltip's skill requirement line with a rank; the rank is the toon's saved one for the skill the line names, so an expansion's line on retail is held against that line) and in `tooLow` below it; both are a reason for the mail icon, and the tooltip says which. A toon with no saved rank for the named skill is in `missing`: nothing says its rank.
- **A toon that has a copy is no reason** (`CaerdonItemMixin:GetRecipeOtherToonInfo` puts `held` and `covered` on each toon of `missing` and `tooLow`, from `CaerdonLedger:GetHeld`): in its bags or its mailbox as they were last seen, or sent to it since.
- **"Not among the recipes it knows" is not "can learn it"** (`IsInRecordedList` in `types/RecipeMixin.lua`). An item of class Recipe may teach something that is no recipe of the profession's list. Forever client: Expert Cookbook 16072 raises Cooking's cap to 225; it is in no toon's list before or after it is learned, so it read as one every other toon with Cooking could learn, a toon that had learned it included. Where a toon's whole list is recorded (the Ingredients recording, `complete`), the recipe's name must be in it; a name that is not puts the toon in `notInList`, which says nothing about it: no mail icon, no tooltip line, and a recipe this toon knows is not called sellable either.
  - *A toon without a recording of its whole list* (its profession was last opened before lists were recorded whole) is judged as before, by the recipes it knows, until its window is next opened.
  - *A book of this kind is judged by caps.* The cap it gives is in its use text and nowhere else ("Use: Teaches you how to be an Expert Cook, allowing a maximum of 225 cooking skill."): the one number of the tooltip's use line (type `ItemSpellTriggerOnUse`), taken as a cap only when it is above the rank the book asks for. A line with no number or more than one gives none, which is also what a colour or plural escape in it comes to. The toon's cap is the one its recording was made at (`maxRank`), only where the profession is one skill line (the recorded line has the profession's name, as on Forever). At or above the book's cap the toon has learned it; below, it lacks it and the rank rule above decides. Without either cap the toon stays in `notInList`.
  - *The toon's cap is as old as its profession window's last opening,* so a toon that learned the book and has not opened the profession since still reads as lacking it.
  - *From one book's export.* That every book of this kind words its use line with one number, and what the other ones give, has not been seen.
  - *A recipe whose item name is not its recipe's name* reads the same way only where the toon's recording has no recipe of the spell the item teaches (the next point).
- **The recipe an item teaches is found by its tooltip's teaching line** (`CaerdonRecipe:FindRecipeID`, `CaerdonItemMixin:GetTaughtSpellID`): the line of type `ItemSpellTriggerLearn` carries a `spellID`, and that is the recipe's ID in the profession's list, the key of a toon's recording. The item's name is asked only where the tooltip has no such line or no list has shown that spell.
  - *Why not the name.* An item and the list may word it differently (Forever: "Recipe: Elixir of Ogre's Strength" teaches "Elixir of Ogre Strength"), which finds nothing. And on Forever a list has pairs of recipes with one name, an old one and one that client added (19 of 471 in a Tailoring list, 1 of 196 in Alchemy): `globalRecipeNameToID` and a toon's `knownRecipes` keep one ID per name, so the name finds whichever was read last and calls the other one learned with it.
  - *Only a spell some list has shown* (a value of `globalRecipeNameToID`, or an ID in a recording of a toon of the realm): the client is no judge of what a recipe is. `C_TradeSkillUI.GetRecipeInfo` answers with a table for every taught spell, a mage's Frost Ward among them, and `C_TradeSkillUI.GetTradeSkillLineForRecipe` answers for skill lines that are no profession ("Companions" for a pet's summon spell, "Engraving" for a rune of the toon's class, a hunter's "Beast Mastery"). So the rule finds no recipe the name's way could not have had a list for.
  - *The table of names is not pruned* (`CaerdonRecipeData.globalRecipeNameToID`; author decision). It is a few tens of kilobytes after long play, and nothing says a name is gone but a full list of recipes, which WoW Forever has no call for; a name taken out wrongly costs a recipe item its verdict until the profession window is opened again. A renamed recipe or a change of the client's language in an export would be the reason to look again.
  - *This toon's "learned"* for a recipe found by its spell is its recording's entry of that ID, or the name filed under that ID among the known (a recipe learned since the recording); the name alone where no recording has the ID. *Another toon's* is asked the same way (`ToonKnowsRecipe`), and a recording that has the ID has the recipe in its list whatever the item calls it.
  - *An item's data made before its tooltip was there* has the name's recipe until the line is; it is looked up again on each ask while it is not found by its spell.
  - *The last teaching line is the recipe's* (`CaerdonItemMixin:GetTaughtSpellID`). A recipe's tooltip holds the lines of the item it makes before its own use line, and the made item may teach something itself: Schematic: Lil' Smoky (11827) has the pet's 15049 "Summon Robot" and then 15633, the recipe; four Engineering schematics on WoW Forever are so.
  - *The item's use spell is not it:* `C_Item.GetItemSpell` of a recipe item is the client's "Learning" in both flavors.
  - *Evidence.* Forever client (test addon, `recipe.taughtSpell`): 21 recipe items of six professions each had the line, on the bag slot's tooltip and the link's alike; all 18 of a profession with a recorded list were in it under that ID, 17 found by name as well with the same ID, one by the spell alone; the tooltip's "Already known" agreed with the list's `learned` on all 18, while `C_TradeSkillUI.GetRecipeInfo(...).learned` was no guide with no profession window open (false for every known one in one session, true for seven of nine in the next). Two patterns whose name two recipes of the list share (Pattern: Crimson Silk Shoulders, Pattern: Earthen Silk Belt) each teach the old recipe where the name found the added one. An export of a carried recipe shows the recipe found by its spell and the icon it had. The recordings of both flavors have the line on every recipe item. Offline, `scenarios/recipe-by-spell.lua`. Not yet seen in a client: on retail, whether a list's IDs are the lines' spells.
  - *Evidence, every item of the classes Consumable and Recipe* (Forever client, test addon, `/cwtest teaches`, 1.60.1.71037, a hunter with five professions): 1991 Consumables and 2731 Recipe items read. No Consumable is taken for a recipe by its name there, and none that teaches (248: runes and two mechanical pets) teaches a profession's recipe. Every profession's recipe item that teaches has a skill requirement line; the 651 that teach without one are all of subclass Book (class spell books). `C_TradeSkillUI.IsRecipeProfessionLearned` was true for exactly the recipes of the toon's professions, with no profession window opened that session, and goes by the spell's profession, which is not always the item's subclass (three patterns of subclass Leatherworking teach and ask for Tailoring, two the other way). 34 recipe items have a skill line and no teaching line (the name stays their only way). Retail not run.
- **A profession the toon has dropped has rank 0** (`ResetMissingProfessionLevel`): the profession's own saved rank as well as its lines'. A rank is saved only ever upwards (`CacheSkillLine`) and the rank read is the highest of the profession's own and its lines' (`FindSkillLine`), so a dropped profession kept its rank while only the lines were reset: a Forever hunter that had left Herbalism at 44 had the grey gear on Blueprint: Greenhouse (273106, "Requires Herbalism (140)"), read from its saved ranks (the profession's own 44 beside its line's 0). The profession's own rank is reset only while the client names a profession at all (`GetProfessions`); with none named, nothing says whether the toon has none or the client has not got them yet. It mends the toon that is played, at its login; what another toon reads of it stays as saved until then. Seen on Forever: after a reload the blueprint has no gear and the saved rank is 0. `scenarios/dropped-profession.lua`. Retail not seen.
- **"Known" for `sellable`** is `bindingResult.alreadyKnown` (the client's tooltip line) only. The name-based `recipeInfo.learned` cannot tell recipe ranks that share a name apart.
- **Data:** `GetRecipeOtherToonInfo()` wraps `CaerdonRecipe:GetOtherToonRecipeInfo(item)`, which returns `{ professionName, recipeName, knownByPlayer, requirement, capTaught, toons, missing, tooLow, unknown, notInList }`. Saved variables: `CaerdonProfessionData[realm][toon]` (ranks) and `CaerdonRecipeData[realm][toon]` with `knownRecipes` and `scannedProfessions`.
- **Recording (`GatherRecipes`):** only the toon's own profession (not linked, guild or NPC). `scannedProfessions[name]` is set only when the list is complete: `GetAllRecipeIDs` exists, or the `GetFilteredRecipeIDs` fallback ran with default filters and no search. Older data has no `scannedProfessions`, so other toons start as `unknown` until they open each profession once.
- **Tooltip (`TooltipMixin:AddRecipeLearners`):** green "Could be learned by: ..." for `missing` toons without a copy; gray "Needs Cooking 125 first: ..." for `tooLow` toons without one; gray "Already has one to learn: ..." for those of either with a copy; gray "Recipes not recorded yet ..." only when the tooltip says "Already known". Wrapped in `xpcall`, because an error would leave `tooltip.caerdonProcessing` set.
- **Settings:** `Icon.ShowRecipesForOtherToons` (the first checkbox of the mail icon's block), `Tooltip.ShowRecipeLearners` (a missing `Tooltip` section reads as on; it also covers the ingredient lines). The icon goes through `otherIcon`, so the per-window toggles of "Learning for another toon" apply.
- **Not modelled:** faction, other realms, deleted toons, a requirement that is not a skill's rank (a specialization, a reputation, a level).
- **Evidence:** offline, `scenarios/ingredients.lua` (the block on other toons; the code from before fails the book's checks, and faults planted one at a time in the rank and cap rules each failed it). Forever client: a recipe for another toon with the mail icon (Ingredients), and the cookbook's export above, from before the rules. Not seen in a client: the cookbook without the icon, a recipe held back by a toon's rank, a book sent by caps; anything on retail. Export: `[Recipe]`, with each other toon's reading and why, what the item asks for, the number in its use line and the cap that is taken for.

## What an Item Teaches

- **Something that teaches this toon a spell it does not know has the star** (`LearnableByTeachingLine` in `CaerdonItem.lua`, asked where the Recipe and the Consumable branch of `GetCaerdonStatus` would say `collected`): a class's spell book (class Recipe, subclass Book), a rune (class Consumable). `own` where the tooltip has a teaching line of the item's own (`ItemSpellTriggerLearn`), does not say "Already known", and no class line of the item's own is unmet; `lowSkill` where its own level line is unmet. Nothing for another class's, which stays what it was (`collected`, and `sellable` for a Consumable by Sellable Rules).
- **Not for an item that asks for a skill.** That is a profession's recipe and has its own rules (Recipes This Toon Has No Use For); a profession's recipe item that teaches has a skill requirement line in every case a client has shown.
- **Whose line it is goes by where it stands.** A tooltip holds the lines of an item it makes before its own teaching line, and the item's own requirements below it: a class's book has "Classes: Mage" and "Requires Level 22" below the line, while Recipe: Rage Potion has the potion's "Classes: Warrior, Druid" and "Requires Level 4" above it. `GetTooltipData` starts the item's own facts anew at each teaching line (`teachesSpell`, `ownClassUnmet`, `ownLevelUnmet`, `ownSkillLine`), so the last such line is the item's and only what follows it counts. The made item's lines are there only while the made item's data is in the client's cache, which is one more reason not to count lines from the top.
- **Met or not is the line's own field** (`usable`), as for every requirement line (Tooltip Lines in the Export).
- **Learned is either word:** the tooltip's "Already known", or `C_SpellBook.IsSpellKnown` of the taught spell. The two agreed on every recipe item of a WoW Forever client (44 known, all others not). A learned book or rune has not been seen there, so neither word is proven for one.
- **Refresh.** The spells such a verdict rests on are kept (`CaerdonItem:IsTaughtSpellJudged`) until the status cache is wiped, and `LEARNED_SPELL_IN_SKILL_LINE` for one of them refreshes; the event comes for every spell a toon gains, and any other spell is passed over. Using the item uses it up, so this is for a second copy. That the event comes for a spell learned from a book has not been seen.
- **The export's `[Teaches]`** has the taught spell and each input with the rule's answer, for any item whose tooltip teaches.
- **Evidence** (Forever client, `/cwtest teaches`, 1.60.1.71037, a hunter of level 36): 651 books that teach and ask for no skill, each with a class line below its teaching line: 647 of another class (unmet), 4 a hunter's (met), all four for level 60 (unmet). 248 Consumables that teach: 246 runes and the like (39 a hunter's, "Classes: Hunter" met, no level line; 204 another class's; 3 with no class line), and two mechanical pets with neither line. None said "Already known" and the client called none of their spells known. Class books are for sale on the auction house there, and one was seen with the rule in place: Book: Gift of the Wild (17682) on a hunter has no icon, its export's `[Teaches]` says the class line and the level line are unmet (both below the teaching line, `usable=false`). A star or a grey gear from this rule has not been seen in a client: no hunter's book or rune was for sale. Retail not run; `scenarios/taught-by-item.lua` holds the rule offline.
- **Mounts, pets and toys** teach too and have their own branches.

### For another toon (`CaerdonLearners.lua`, `CaerdonLedger.lua`)

- **`send` for a book or a rune this toon has no use for** (the rule above says nothing for it: another class's, or learned), that can be handed over (not soulbound, not bind on pickup), where another toon of the realm said it can learn it and has no copy (`CaerdonItemMixin:GetLearnerStatus`; step 5 of the status order, before a Consumable of another class is called `sellable`). One switch for all that another toon could learn, the recipes' (`Icon.ShowRecipesForOtherToons`, "Recipes, spell books and runes they have not learned"; author decision, 2026-11-22: nobody wants the one mailed and not the other).
- **Each toon answers for itself.** Nothing saved says what another toon knows or what class it is, and a class is only a line of a tooltip as that toon's client colours it. So the rule's own verdict is saved per toon and item (`CaerdonLearnerData[realm key][toon].answers`): `own`, `lowSkill`, `known`, or `never` where a class line of the item's own is unmet. No class name is read and no text is parsed.
  - *An item is on the realm's list once any toon has an answer for it,* which is as soon as one comes across it (`GetCaerdonStatus` hands the answer to `NoteOwnAnswer`). A toon judges what the list holds and it has no lasting answer for when it logs in, in the background (ten items a second, from eight seconds after the world is there, not in a fight; the item's data is asked of the client first). `known` and `never` last; `own` and `lowSkill` are asked again at every login (learned or levelled with the addon off) and `lowSkill` at a level gained.
  - *A toon that has not answered says nothing:* it has not been played since some toon first came across the item. So a book seen for the first time has no mail icon until the toon it is for has logged in once.
  - *A spell learned answers at once* (`LEARNED_SPELL_IN_SKILL_LINE` for the spell kept with an `own` / `lowSkill` answer): the item that taught it is used up, so nothing on screen would say so, and the other toons read the saved answer before this one next logs in.
  - *A toon below the item's level counts as one to send to,* since the ledger keeps a second copy from being sent.
- **The ledger: whether that toon already has a copy.** Not a tracker of moves: what was last seen where, for the items of the realm's list and every item of class Recipe (`CaerdonLedgerData[realm key][toon]`). `bags` and `bank`: read whenever the toon is played (login, `BAG_UPDATE_DELAYED`). `mail`: what its mailbox held when it was last open (`MAIL_INBOX_UPDATE`). `sent`: what is on its way to its mailbox, noted when it set out and dropped when the toon next reads its whole mailbox, or after 30 days: a mail from another toon of the player's, and what the toon bought at the auction house. A toon with any of them above zero is covered and is no reason for the icon. A sighting replaces what was there, which is how time with the addon off is mended.
  - *The bank is counted with it closed, by the client's count* (`C_Item.GetItemCount(id, true, false, true)` less the bags). Forever client: a recipe put in the bank reads 0 in the bags and 1 with the bank at the auction house after a reload, the bank window closed. The client says how many of an item, not what the bank holds, so the items asked about are kept per toon (`asked`): what the ledger counts that was seen in its bags or mailbox, what it said it could learn (`NoteOwnAnswer`), and a recipe another toon found it lacking (`NoteWanted`, written into its record by that toon's session and asked when it is next played). And whatever the toon comes across itself and could learn is asked and written down then and there (`GetOwnCopies`, from the status), on the copy it holds as on any other. That last one is from a Forever client: a recipe lay in each of two toons' banks from before the ledger counted banks, each toon's own second copy lost its star by the client's count, and each still took the other to have none, because nothing had put the item among those to ask about; the saved ledger showed the count there only after the other toon had looked at the item and the first had been played again. With both counts saved, neither toon had an icon on the recipe after a reload. A recipe in a bank that neither the toon nor another has come across since is still not known. Retail not seen.
  - *An auction bought* is the `C_AuctionHouse.PlaceBid` call (hooked; the auction's item by `GetAuctionInfoByID` while the auction is there) with `AUCTION_HOUSE_PURCHASE_COMPLETED` for that auction after it; a bid that is no buyout brings no such event. A commodity by `ConfirmCommoditiesPurchase` and `COMMODITY_PURCHASE_SUCCEEDED`. Both flavors' auction house buys through these calls (read in the mirrors). Forever client, the author's word: a recipe bought there lost its star on the other auctions at once.
- **One copy is enough for the toon itself** (author decision, 2026-11-22). A recipe, book or rune it could learn (`own`, `lowSkill`) and already has a copy of is `collected` on a copy it does not hold (an auction, a vendor's, loot): no star, no gear, and the later steps may still make it one to send to another toon. The copy it holds keeps its icon. `CaerdonLedger:GetOwnCopies` is asked for it: the bags and the bank by the client's count, the mailbox as last read, and what is on its way. `bindingResult.ownCopies` has the counts.
  - *A copy the toon holds* (`CaerdonItemMixin:IsOwnCopy`) lies in a slot of its own, or a window says so (`extraData.ownCopy`: the mail feature's items). The two kinds do not share a cache entry (`|f` on the key of a recipe or consumable that is not held).
  - *Refresh:* the ledger compares how many of each counted item the toon has after each reading and each purchase, and refreshes (`"copies held"`) when it has one where it had none, or none where it had one; a copy moved from the bags to the bank changes nothing. A changed count refreshes only for an item a verdict counted (`NoteCountJudged`: enough to send, below). *A copy taken out of a mail is counted as on its way into the bags* (`landing`) until the client's count of the bags has risen, ten seconds at most, after which a mail that went with its copy is found out. Two simpler rules failed in a retail client: holding back the mailbox's comparison at the event that saw fewer (the event came again and compared), and holding it back until the bags were next read (that reading came before the copy and had none; the one after had it). The export says how many copies landed and how long after at the latest.
  - *Enough to send:* a copy the toon does not hold of a recipe, book or rune that its other toons could learn has no mail icon once the toon has a copy for each of them that has none, beside the one it keeps to learn itself (`CaerdonItemMixin:HasEnoughToSend`; the copies it holds keep the icon, and the other copy is left as it is, never called a spare to sell). Its tooltip says how many the toon has to send (`bindingResult.spareCopies`). Evidence: offline (`scenarios/learners.lua`, `scenarios/ingredients.lua`); asked for from a retail client where a recipe bought for the one other toon left the mail icon on every other listing.
  - *Seen on Forever* (one export, a recipe the toon is below the rank for, a copy in its bank, the bank closed): `bindingResult.ownCopies` has the bank's 1, the star is held back, and the status is `send` for the other toon, which the ledger took to have none (above).
  - *Tooltip (`TooltipMixin:AddOwnCopies`):* gray "You already have one to learn (in your bags / in your bank / in your mailbox / on its way to your mailbox)" on the copy without the icon. Not where the tooltip's data names a copy of the toon's own (`guid`). Hidden by `Tooltip.ShowRecipeLearners` with the lines that name toons. Whether an item in the mailbox has a `guid` on its tooltip has not been seen.
  - *A send is the client's `SendMail` call* (hooked: the recipient as typed, held against the realm's toons with a record, without the realm suffix and whatever its case; the attachments by `GetSendMailItem`) *and counts when `MAIL_SEND_SUCCESS` follows;* `MAIL_FAILED` drops it. Blizzard's mail frame of both flavors sends through that call. It refreshes (`"sent to another toon"`), since a second copy's verdict said "send it".
  - *A mailbox that shows a part of what it holds* (`GetInboxNumItems`' second value above its first) keeps the notes of what was sent.
  - *The export's `[Ledger]` section,* which every item has: whether the ledger counts the item, what it holds of it for this toon, the copies the toon has now and whether a star was held back for one, whether this is a copy it holds, the ledger's state this session, whether the bank window is open, and the client's own counts.
- **A spell book or a rune's tooltip (`TooltipMixin:AddTaughtLearners`)** has a line a toon, in the learner info's order: green "Could be learned by: ..." (now, no copy), gray "Could be learned at a later level by: ... (level 20)" with the level the toon had when last played, gray "... already has one (in its bags / in its mailbox / on its way to it)". Nothing for a toon of another class, one that has learned it or one that has not answered. Five toons at most, then "and N more of your toons" (author decision). Only for an item on the realm's list, so no tooltip is read for an item nobody answered for. `Tooltip.ShowRecipeLearners` hides it with the recipe and ingredient lines (one switch, author decision).
- **The recorder's seam** is `CaerdonLearners:GetSavedAnswers` (the other toons' answers with the ledger's counts): saved variables, which a recording of a client's answers does not hold.
- **The export's `[Teaches]`** has this toon's answer, whether the item can be handed over, each other toon's answer with what the ledger has of it, the toons that have not answered, the mail icon's verdict with its reason, and what the background judging and the ledger did this session.
- **Not modelled:** faction, other realms, deleted toons (as for recipes); a copy that left the toon in a way the ledger does not see until its next sighting; the bank.
- **Evidence:** offline, `scenarios/learners.lua` (two toons played in turn with the saved tables handed over) and the ledger's block of `scenarios/ingredients.lua`. Forever client, recipes only (the author's report and one export, 1.60.1.71037): a recipe that lay in another toon's mailbox when that toon last opened it has no mail icon at the auction house and the tooltip says the toon has one; recipes that toon lacks have the icon, the toon being below their rank (Cooking 5 against 75 in the export, whose `[Recipe]` has the ledger's line for it: nothing in its bags or mailbox, nothing sent). Not seen in a client: a book or a rune with the icon, a toon answering from the realm's list at login, a mail noted at the send, anything on retail. That a mail to one's own toon goes through `SendMail` and `MAIL_SEND_SUCCESS` is read from both flavors' mail frame; how long a mail lives is from memory.

## Outgrown Food and Drink

`CaerdonFoodDrink.lua` marks food or drink `sellable` when the toon carries a better one of the same kind it can use now; `GetFoodDrinkInfo()` is nil for anything else.

- **Compared with what is carried (bags only),** so the last stack is never marked.
- **Candidates:** Consumable / Food & Drink (subclass 5). The gate matters: a healing potion would otherwise read as food.
- **Food vs. drink:** no API; the tooltip line with a number and the client's word for health or mana decides (`both` is left alone; which words: Text Read Off a Tooltip Line). The amount is the number before that word, and the line's first number where none stands before it. More numbers than "amount, duration" means buff food, which only competes with other buff food. The trailing cooldown note and `|4` plural escapes are stripped before counting.
- **Better:** higher required level, or the same level and a larger amount.
- **Never marked:** crafting reagents, items not usable yet, anything with quest value.
- **Hunters, where the client says what the pet eats** (Forever: `C_PetInfo.CanPetEatItem(itemID)`, which Blizzard's bags use to light up what Feed Pet takes; retail has no such function): the food kept for the pet is chosen from everything in the bags that it eats, and food kept that way is never outdated. Any other food is judged as for another class.
  - **What is kept (`ChoosePetFood`):** the best 4 pieces (`PET_FOOD_PIECES`) that fit in one or two kinds, a piece counting for the square of its item level. Two kinds at most, so that pet food does not fill the bags a few pieces at a time (author decision, 2026-11-17), and the second only while the best is down to two or three pieces (author decision, 2026-11-25): with four of one kind nothing is kept beside it. The number was 20 before, and a stack of 14 then kept a second kind for the six places left. By item level because higher food is good for longer; by pieces so that one or two pieces of the best food do not cost a stack its place. Squared so that the level outweighs the count: counted plainly, ten pieces of level 14 took the place of four of level 30 in the author's bags. One kind wins over two that give no more.
  - **A kind to send to another toon is never the second kind** (author decision, 2026-11-25): the pet's best food stays its food whatever another toon's recipes take, but what only fills it up goes to the toon that gets skill from it, and the next kind takes the place. The food module asks the item's own rule with the pet left aside (`GetIngredientStatus(petAside)`), of an item made from the kind's first stack, once per kind and only for a kind that would otherwise be the second. The mail icon came and went with where the stack lay otherwise: in the bank it is not carried and read `send`, taken out to be mailed it became the pet's second kind and had no icon.
  - **A kind, not a slot.** A verdict belongs to an item, so every stack of a kept kind is kept, however many there are.
  - **Raw meat counts** (Trade Goods / Cooking: the pet eats it). It can take one of the two places and so make a cooked food outdated; it gets no verdict from this module itself.
  - **The answers are remembered while the pet is away** (`petEats`: dismissed, dead, the toon mounted), so its food is not marked and unmarked each time. A pet without a diet is not asked: "eats nothing" would be taken for an answer.
  - **Refresh:** the kept kinds are part of the carried signature, compared on `BAG_UPDATE_DELAYED` and `UNIT_PET`.
  - **Export:** `[Pet Food]` on a hunter, for every item: the pet, its diet and happiness, the client's answer for the item, and every carried kind the pet eats with the kept ones marked.
  - **Evidence.** Offline: `scenarios/pet-food.lua`, a hand-made game. Forever client (1.60.1.70959, a hunter with a level 32 pet that eats meat and fish, 2026-11-17): the client answers true for raw meats and cooked meat from 2 to 27 item levels below the pet, and three trade goods of the Cooking subclass in the bags are not among what it eats (Gooey Spider Leg, Murloc Eye, Spider Ichor); a kabob with better food carried for the toon and for the pet reads `sellable`, and of nine kinds the pet eats the four pieces of level 30 and the six of level 23 are the kept ones (with 20 pieces kept; with 4 the six are not). Forever client (1.60.1.71091, 20 pieces kept): Clam Meat that another toon's Cooking takes read `send` in the bank, with 14 pieces of level 35 carried. Not seen in a client: the rule with 4 pieces, and a kind passed over as the second one. Not seen: the answer for bread, the answer with no pet out, and whether it turns false for food too far below the pet (the client has an error string for that).
  - **The three food fixtures were recorded before the rule** and replay without `C_PetInfo`, so they show the level gap's verdict. They ask for a new recording.
- **Hunters, where it cannot say** (retail, or no pet seen yet this session): food must be more than 20 item levels below the toon (`HUNTER_FOOD_LEVEL_GAP`) to be outdated.
- **Setting:** `Icon.ShowOutdatedFood`. **Export:** `[Food and Drink]`, with the words looked for; an item of the class whose line was not read has the section too and says so. **In game:** one buff food classified correctly; an "outdated" verdict is offline only.
- **Raw ingredients are not this module's:** a raw meat is a trade good and gets no verdict here, kept for the pet or not. What it is still good for in the toon's recipes is the next section.

## Ingredients

`CaerdonIngredients.lua` says what an item is still good for as an ingredient of a recipe, this toon's or another toon's of the realm; `GetIngredientInfo()` is nil when no recorded recipe takes it. Two statuses come of it (`CaerdonItemMixin:GetIngredientStatus`): `send` and `sellable`.

- **The rule it is for** (author decisions, 2026-11-17): an ingredient has a use while a recipe that takes it still gives skill. A green recipe counts, and so does a recipe the toon has not learned that would give skill. An ingredient whose recipes are all gray is the one to mark: the sell coin, or the auction icon where that pays. It comes from Forever, where bag space is short and the trade goods in a toon's bags had no verdict from any rule (the auction rule marks one only when it clears its profit bar).
- **Recorded when a profession window opens** (`RecordRecipes` in `types/RecipeMixin.lua`, beside the known-recipe scan on `TRADE_SKILL_DATA_SOURCE_CHANGED`; the toon's own profession only, not a linked one, the guild's or an NPC's): `CaerdonRecipeData[realm][toon].recipes[professionName]` holds per recipe its name, `learned`, the client's `relativeDifficulty`, `canSkillUp`, `numSkillUps` and `maxTrivialLevel`, and `reagents = { [itemID] = quantity }`; per profession the base skill line, the rank the difficulties were said at, whether the list was the whole one, and the client build.
  - **Why it is written down:** the client answers for one profession at a time, the one whose window is open or was opened last (which of the two is not on record). For a recipe of any other, `C_TradeSkillUI.GetRecipeInfo` knows the recipe and not what it is to the toon: `learned` false, `relativeDifficulty` 0, `canSkillUp` false, `numSkillUps` 0, `maxTrivialLevel` 0 (Forever: a known First Aid recipe after Cooking was opened; retail: a recipe of a profession the druid does not have). So what was said is kept, with the rank it was said at.
  - **The rank is the open child line's where it has one,** otherwise the base profession's: on Forever a secondary profession has no rank on its base line (WoW Forever Client, Professions).
  - **Reagents are what the recipe cannot be made without:** a slot of type `Basic` or one marked `required`. An optional slot (retail's modifying and finishing reagents) is left out; a slot that takes one of several items (retail's quality tiers) names each.
  - **Reagents are read once per client build.** A schematic per recipe is what a recording costs, and only the client's data changes it; difficulty and `learned` are read at every opening, from the recipe infos the scan already asks for.
  - **A filtered list only adds to a recording; the whole list replaces it.** `GetAllRecipeIDs` is the whole list; the `GetFilteredRecipeIDs` fallback is one only with default filters.
  - **A recipe learned from an item** (`NEW_RECIPE_LEARNED`) is marked learned in the recording at once; its difficulty stays as it was said.
- **Read by the toon's rank against the rank the recipe is gray from** (`ReadRecipe`): `maxTrivialLevel` is that rank, so a recipe is a use while the rank is below it, learned or not, and the recording does not age as the rank rises. The rank is the live one (`GetProfessionInfo`); a point gained refreshes through the skills watch, which empties the index.
  - **Only where a profession is one skill line** (the recorded line has the profession's name, as on Forever). Retail has a line per expansion, the profession's rank is not the rank of a recipe's line, and there the recorded colour decides: orange, yellow and green are a use, gray is not. That colour is as old as the window's last opening.
  - **A recipe with neither** (no gray rank, no difficulty) is counted neither way (`undecided`).
  - **`canSkillUp` is recorded and not read.** It is false for every recipe of a profession at its rank cap, the orange and yellow ones included, and the cap is raised by training.
  - **Every recipe of the list counts, also one this toon may never learn.** An unlearned recipe that would give skill is a use whatever it takes to get it (author decision, 2026-11-17, after the exports below). The list is the profession's whole one and nothing in a recipe's info says whether the toon can get it (another race's or faction's, a rare drop), so the rule marks little: only what every recipe of the profession has outgrown.
- **Only what was recorded is known.** "No recipe takes it" is said about the professions that were opened and no other; the export names the ones that were not. A dropped profession keeps its recording and is ignored (matched by name or base skill line, as the tools are).
- **Saved per toon,** which is how another toon's use is known. Its rank is the one its last session saved (`CaerdonProfessionData`, `CaerdonRecipe:GetToonRank`), its recipes those of its last recording; a profession it dropped reads rank 0 and counts for nothing. The other toons' index is built once a session: their saved data does not change while this toon is played.
- **The index is kept through a refresh about what is worn or learned** (`CaerdonIngredients:InvalidateCaches`): it follows from the recordings, which drop it themselves, and from the toon's professions.
- **Export:** `[Ingredients]`, for every item: the three settings, the status the rule gives, whether the auction icon is kept off, how many vendors sell it and whether that makes it a common vendor good (or why that is not known), whether it has a use, each recipe of this toon's that takes it with what was recorded and what the client answers at that moment, the recorded professions with their rank then and now and what the session's recording cost, the professions not recorded, and the other toons whose recipes take it with each recipe's reading.
- **What a Forever client answers** (1.60.1.70959, a hunter with five professions, three exports, 2026-11-17):
  - **The list holds the recipes the toon has not learned.** `GetAllRecipeIDs` gives the profession's whole list although Blizzard's Forever window hides the unlearned ones (`Professions.GetDefaultShowUnlearned` is false in `Blizzard_ProfessionsTemplates/Camelot/Blizzard_Professions.lua`, and its filter menu has no entry for them): Cooking 132 recipes of which 27 known, Tailoring 471 and 57, Alchemy 196 and 24.
  - **An unlearned recipe has a real difficulty and gray rank,** not a filler: one read gray (3, gray from 120 at rank 150), two orange (gray from 205 and 230).
  - **`maxTrivialLevel` is the rank a recipe is gray from.** Seven recipes of two professions: the three gray ones had it at 85, 120 and 140 with the rank at 150, the four others above the rank (160 at rank 119; 180, 205, 230 at 150). No recipe was seen with the rank exactly at it; the code takes the recipe as gray from that rank on, as classic had it.
  - **Reagents are recorded,** so a classic recipe's slots are typed `Basic` or marked `required`. Which of the two is not on record.
  - **A recording is cheap there:** 471 recipes with their schematics in 7.6 ms, 132 in 1.4 ms.
- **Not known:**
  - What the client says about where an unlearned recipe comes from. Chunk of Boar Meat's known recipe is gray and it has a use only through a recipe that is not learned, orange until 230, whose quest the same export skips as another race's. `C_TradeSkillUI.GetRecipeSourceText` is what Blizzard's recipe form shows for an unlearned recipe (both listings; undocumented); the export prints it and the recipe's `sourceType`, asked while the profession is the open one. The rule does not need them; they are there should it ever be narrowed.
  - On retail, what the client says about a recipe of an expansion's skill line the toon never trained. The export prints each recipe's line and the toon's rank in it (`GetProfessionInfoByRecipeID`). It is read by whatever colour the client gave it, like any other recipe there; if that turns out to be a filler, such a recipe should count neither way.
  - Whether a rank at the gray rank is gray (above).
  - What a recording costs on retail's lists, which hold every expansion's recipes, and what the recorded colour is worth there.
- **The verdict** (`GetIngredientStatus`; a step after outgrown food and before quest value, replacing an empty status only):
  - *Kept, no icon:* a recipe of this toon's still gives skill for it, or one of them could not be read (`undecided`). Its own use comes before any other toon's.
  - *`send`:* otherwise, when another toon's recipes take it and it can be handed over (not soulbound, not bind on pickup). Which recipes count is the setting: one that still gives that toon skill (`skillFor`), or also one it knows that no longer does (`usedBy`). It is sent rather than sold. An item none of this toon's recorded recipes takes qualifies: the ore a hunter loots for a blacksmith.
  - *Not what vendors everywhere sell* (`CaerdonVendorGoods.lua`, `GetVendorInfo()`): thread, spices, a vial, wood for a campfire. The other toon buys it where it stands, so it gets no mail icon and no tooltip line, and outgrown here it is one to sell. Forever client: Star Wood 11291 read `send` for a toon whose unlearned campfire recipe takes one.
    - **The answer is QuestieDB's.** The client says who sells an item only in a vendor's own window. QuestieDB's item data names the NPCs that sell it (`LibQuestieDB.Item.Get(itemID, "vendors")`). Without QuestieDB nothing is known, `GetVendorInfo()` is nil and the rule is as it was; retail has no QuestieDB.
    - **Common is 20 vendors or more** (`COMMON_VENDOR_COUNT`). The data does not say whether a vendor's stock is limited, and a material a few vendors sell a handful of is worth sending. Of the 74 items QuestieDB's Forever data (1.1.2, read offline from its TOC) classes as trade goods and gives a vendor, 33 have 1 to 7 (leather, herbs, an engineer's parts), three have 11 or 12 (enchanting dust and essence) and 38 have 21 or more (flour, dyes, flux, thread, vials); Star Wood, a reagent in that data, has 75. Nothing lies between 12 and 21, and the number is in that gap. Whether any of the 38 is limited in stock is not known (Bronze Tube has 23 vendors, Gyrochronatom 21); such a one reads as common.
    - **Asked last, and not kept by the module:** one read of the library for an item that would otherwise be sent, kept with the item's status. The recorder needs every run to ask again. QuestieDB loads after the addon; a verdict made before it is there is made again when the quest module's index is built (its `quest data` refresh), and the ingredient rule asks the quest module before it gets here.
