    - **In a recording** it is an ordinary answer (`LibQuestieDB.Item.Get`, named in `ALSO_NAMES`). A fixture from before reads the library's item data as a name it does not have, and its verdict stands.
  - *`sellable`:* otherwise, when this toon's own recorded recipes take it, every one is gray, and a vendor pays. The auction step turns it into `auction` where that pays.
  - *Only a material:* a trade good (item class `Tradegoods`) or what the client itself calls a crafting reagent (`GetIsCraftingReagent`). A recipe can take anything, and an item of another class has a use of its own that no recipe says anything about. Forever client: a hunter's Razor Arrows 3030 (class Projectile, not a crafting reagent to the client) read `send` for a toon whose unlearned Explosive Arrow takes a hundred of them. The tooltip line follows the status, so it names nobody there either.
  - *A quest of this toon's comes before another toon* (author, 2026-11-17): an item a quest takes, one in the log or one still to come, is neither sent nor sold. The quest module is asked itself (`isActive`, `isUpcoming`), not the status logic's "has quest value", which follows the quest icon's two settings: with that icon off the item has no icon at all. A recipe to send is in the quest step's list of what it replaces.
  - *Never:* a profession tool, a kind of food kept for a hunter's pet (`CaerdonFoodDrink:IsKeptForPet`; one to send is kept only as the pet's best kind, never as its second: Outgrown Food and Drink), and, for `sellable`, an item no recorded recipe of this toon's takes: nothing says what else it is good for.
  - *A profession this toon never opened is not known,* so an ingredient of it can read `send` for another toon until the window has been opened once.
- **The auction icon on trade goods leaves a kept ingredient alone** (`IsIngredientKeptFromAuction`, the setting `Icon.AuctionTradeGoods`): `all` (none kept, as before the rule), `skill` (one a recipe still gives skill for; the default), `professions` (any ingredient of a recorded recipe), `none` (no trade good is marked for its price alone).
- **Settings** (author's design for the mail block, 2026-11-17):
  - *The mail icon's block, "For another toon to use":* recipes they have not learned (`Icon.ShowRecipesForOtherToons`); ingredients this toon has no use for (`Icon.ShowIngredientsForOtherToons`) with what the other toon must be able to do with them (`Icon.IngredientsForOtherToons`: `skill` or `any`). The icon shows where the icon for another toon does (`otherIcon`), so it has no window grid of its own.
  - *Sellable block:* ingredients my recipes have outgrown (`Icon.ShowOutgrownIngredients`). It starts on on Forever and off on retail (`GetDefaultConfig`): a starting value picked by flavor, which is Claude's reading of the author's "sounds good" and his to overrule. On retail every recipe of a finished expansion is gray and its reagents are kept all the same.
  - *Auction block:* the choice above.
  - *Why not one ladder of what is kept,* as first proposed: for the sell icon every rung above the first is the same as off, and a ladder had no rung for what the auction icon did before. So the sell icon is a checkbox, the auction icon has the ladder, and the other toons are the mail block.
- **Tooltip** (`TooltipMixin:AddIngredientUsers`): on an ingredient this toon has no use for, green "Gives skill to: Dorn (Cooking 120)", and with the setting on `any` a gray "Used in recipes of: ..." for the toons that only know a gray recipe. Off with `Tooltip.ShowRecipeLearners`.
- **Refresh:** a rank gained and a profession learned or dropped come through the skills watch. A recording asks for one itself when what it says about an item differs from the recording before (`OnRecipesRecorded`, refresh `recipes recorded`), and only when the index was read since the last refresh. *The index is kept over a refresh that changed nothing it is made from* (`GetIndex`): it follows from the recordings, every write of which tells the module, and from the toon's professions, skill lines and ranks, which are a handful of calls to read again; so after a full refresh those are read and compared, and the index is built anew only where they differ. Building it walks every recorded recipe: with four professions and some 700 recipes it was one call of 4.5 ms in each full refresh, on whichever ingredient was asked first (retail 12.1.5.70961). The export's `[Ingredients]` says how often it was made and how often kept. Evidence: offline (`scenarios/ingredients.lua`; a rank ignored, an index never kept, and an unread index compared at a recording each fail it; that the saved table itself was replaced is checked and not under test). Timed in both clients since (`/cwtest timing bank`, the function's slowest call of a refresh, which was the building): retail 12.1.5.70961 0.65 ms where the runs before had 4.2 to 4.5 ms, and Forever 1.60.1.71037 0.46 ms where three earlier runs had 6.4 to 9.5 ms. A rank gained with the index in hand has been seen in neither. The kinds kept for the pet come through the food module's signature, which now also runs with only the ingredient options on.
- **Recorded for the harness as a seam:** the recordings are saved variables, which no recording of a client's answers holds. Everything read from them goes through `CaerdonIngredients:GetRecordedUses(itemID)`, plain facts with nothing judged, which the recorder keeps and a replay puts back (`RecordKeys.lua`, `SEAMS`).
- **The mail icon:** the atlas `ui-hud-minimap-mail-up` (both flavors' XML use it for the minimap's mail), else `Interface\MailFrame\Mail-Icon`. Drawn like the auction icon, without a ring and at its smaller size (not prominent): the envelope fills its square where a ring icon has a margin, and at the full size it read as larger than the icons beside it (Forever client). Whether it is the right picture is the author's to say.
- **Still open:** on retail, whether the reading follows a rank gained without the window being opened again (it reads the recorded colour there); a recipe of an expansion's line the toon never trained.
- **Evidence:** offline (`scenarios/ingredients.lua`, a game hand-made from the client's documentation, both flavors; `scenarios/pet-food.lua` for the kept kinds; faults planted one at a time each failed one of them). Static: the recipe info, the schematic and the two enumerations are documented alike in both flavors, and every function is in both listings. Forever client: the recording and the reading by colour, as above. Forever client with the rule (1.60.1.70959, two toons and their professions, 2026-11-17, the author): the mail icon where he expected it and the tooltip line. Then his arrows with the mail icon, which the material rule above answers, and Star Wood with it, which the vendor rule answers (offline: the scenario, which the code from before fails; the vendor counts are QuestieDB's data read from its TOC, not a client's answer). Not on record from a client: the arrows without the icon, Star Wood without it and the export's two vendor lines, which items the first sighting was, the sell icon on an outgrown ingredient, the quest coming first, the settings page, the icon at its smaller size; retail.

## Quest Value

`CaerdonQuestItems.lua` gives the `quest` status to items of any class that an unfinished quest takes; `GetQuestItemInfo()` returns nil or `{ hasQuestValue, isActive, isUpcoming, isOver, starts, quests }`.

- **No client API maps an item to the quests that take it.** `C_QuestLog.GetQuestObjectives` has no item ID.
- **The client does say which quest an item in a bag slot starts** (`C_Container.GetContainerItemQuestInfo`: `isQuestItem`, `questID`, `isActive`; in both listings, and Blizzard's bags draw their quest border and bang from it in both trees). `CaerdonQuestItems:GetSlotQuestInfo` hands the answer on, and the quest it names is one of the item's quests (`GetItemQuestInfo`'s third argument, which the item's `GetQuestItemInfo` fills for a copy in a bag slot): that is how a copy is known to start a quest on retail, where no QuestieDB is, and for a quest neither source ties to the item. `isQuestItem` and `isActive` are read for the export (`[Quest Value]`, "The Client, For Its Bag Slot") and the contract alone; whether the quest is in the log or done is asked of the quest log as for any quest. Forever client (1.60.1.71037, a hunter): one starter seen, a Craftsman's Writ whose quest was in the log: no quest item for the client (class 0), the quest's ID kept with `isActive` true. Not seen: what a client answers for a starter whose quest is done or not for the toon, so nothing rests on that (the completed flag and the quest data say it).
- **An item that starts a quest the toon has neither done nor taken is one to act on** (author decision): the star, or the grey gear while the quest or the item asks for a level the toon has not, with the quest icon as the small mark on it. `starts` in the quest info (`{ questID, requiredLevel, belowLevel }`); `questStarter` and `starterMark` in `GetCaerdonStatus`, where quest value is applied; `own` or `lowSkill` with `quest` as the status's sixth value.
  - *Where it holds:* every quest that is not behind the toon is one the item starts and that is not in the log. Once the quest is in the log, or where another quest takes the item, it is a quest's item as any other, with the quest icon alone. The quest that takes the item back is usually the one it starts (the index has both roles, "objective, starts quest"), which is one quest and no other.
  - *For a copy the toon does not hold, the quest data says whether the quest can be started* (`startable` on the quest's verdict): neither in the log nor done, and kept from the toon by nothing, or by nothing but that it can be done again. "Repeatable" keeps a quest from being one still to come, since what it takes would be wanted for ever; an item that starts one is used once (a treasure map, a writ). Whether the toon has the item that starts the quest is asked of the quest's objectives and is no question for the item itself. An event's quest, one hidden by Questie, a profession not learned, another faction's: none to start, and the item is as it was before the rule.
  - *For the copy in a bag slot the client's word stands* (`byClient` in `GetItemQuestInfo`, `canStart` on the quest's entry): the quest it names is one to start unless it is in the log, flagged as done, or kept out by the player's options, whatever the quest data says of who can take it or of when it is offered. **Not against the item itself:** where the item's own tooltip has its class or race line unmet (`bindingResult.raceClassRestricted`), `GetCaerdonStatus` gives no star, since the client names the quest a copy starts whoever holds the copy. Seen on retail (12.1.5.70961, a druid): Essence of the Iron Protector 120210 and of the Iron Conqueror in the warband bank, "This Item Begins a Quest", the class line red with `usable` false, the client naming quest 37548 for the slot, had the star; the export says why it has none now. An unmet faction line ("Horde Only" on a toon of the Alliance) is not read for this: no export has shown one unmet. A first version let the data's "behind the toon" overrule the client. Seen on Forever (1.60.1.71037, a dwarf hunter of 37, QuestieDB 1.1.4): Craftsman's Writ: Elixir of Lesser Agility 264016 in the bank, the client naming quest 94216 for the slot and not in the log, had no icon, its quest "other race (behind the toon)". Read in QuestieDB's data: 94216 has the Horde's races (`0x2000000B2`); of WoW Forever's 150 writ quests 144 have no races, five the Horde's and one the Alliance's, and every one of the six rewards both factions' reputation (2586 and 2587); the same toon had 94216 in its log a week before (a fixture). So the data's word on who can take a quest is not to be held against the client's for a copy the toon holds. A copy of such a writ that the toon does not hold (an auction, the mail) still goes by the data and has no star; nothing in either client says whether a toon can take a quest.
  - *QuestieDB's faction masks on WoW Forever are not the bare classic ones:* `0x10000004D` (77 with bit 32) and `0x2000000B2` (178 with bit 33), on 1,899 and 1,659 of its 6,080 quests (1.1.4); the bare 77 and 178 that `CLASSIC_FACTION_MASK` knows are on none. A classic race is matched by its own bit all the same; what that table was for (the two races WoW Forever added, whose bits are 32 and 33) rests on `raceMaskById` having those bits, which no toon of such a race has shown.
  - *WoW Forever's writs in QuestieDB:* level 60, asking for level 1, none flagged as one to do again. Whether the game lets a writ's quest be done again is not known; the client's completed flag decides either way.
  - *The player's options hold* (the range of levels, gray quests), in the bags as anywhere, as they do for the quest icon a class gives: a starter whose quest is beyond the range or gray has no icon. Within the range a quest that asks for a level the toon has not is the grey gear. Not under the option for quests not picked up yet, which is about what a quest will ask for; with the quest icon switched off nothing is told of a quest and the item is no starter.
  - *The level* is the one the quest data has for the quest (`requiredLevel`, `lvl`), and the item's own (`GetItemInfo`'s fifth value): the tooltip of a writ says "Requires Level 10". Of several quests an item starts, one the toon is of a level for comes first.
  - *A state of its own where answers are compared* (`StateOf`, "to be started"): a quest that can be done again, or that the client named for a copy in the bags (`namedByClient`, kept until the caches are dropped), is neither "upcoming" nor "in the log" before it is taken, and can be taken and done within one run of quest events, which would compare as no change and leave the star on a second copy.
  - *The cache key carries the client's word* (`|q<quest>`): a copy the client names a quest for does not share an entry with the bare link, which the mail or an auction asks about. So every copy in a bag slot is asked `GetContainerItemQuestInfo` as its key is made, on every call.
  - *A copy that can be read keeps the star,* as a quest's item keeps the quest icon (`GetCopyStatus`), and its one small mark is the quest's. The vendor, the journal and crafting orders leave it alone as they leave `quest` (`ProcessItem`).
  - *Drawing* (`SetItemButtonStatus`, before the animation is chosen): the quest icon as the plain small badge on the star or the gear (`SetSmallMark`), where the window shows both icons; the quest icon alone, standing still, where it shows no star; the star alone where it shows no quest icon. An upgrade's arrow comes first in the badge's place. The Icons page says so in a line under the quest icon's options.
  - *Export:* `[Quest Value]` has `Starts A Quest Not Taken Yet`; a quest's line says "by the client's word for its bag slot" and, where it is skipped as one still to come, "(the item can still start it)", or that the quest data has it behind the toon and the client names it for this copy. `[Caerdon]` has the small mark.
  - *The test addon:* the contract `quest.slotInfo` (version 2) counts the bags' starters by what they are drawn as (one to start, the quest icon, neither) and fails where the addon calls a quest one to start that the client says is in the log or done; the bag states have a case `starter`; the verdict tests retire such an item as they do one a quest takes.
  - Evidence: offline (`scenarios/quest-starters.lua` with a hand-made quest database, `slot-quest-info.lua` with none, `quest-source-att.lua` for AllTheThings' quests, `draw-path.lua` for the mark, with the drawing compared call by call with the code from before: only the two new sets differ; faults planted one at a time each failed one of them). A fixture of a copy in the bags recorded before this has no answer for the slot's question and replays with it unanswered until it is recorded again. Forever client (1.60.1.71037, a hunter of 37): Cuergo's Treasure Map 9254 on an auction had the grey gear with the quest icon as its small mark (quest 2882, "repeatable" in QuestieDB, asks for level 40, three above the toon; the map itself asks for 40); the bags' snapshot across the reload that brought the rule had every item the same; the writ in the bank (above) had the star with the quest icon once the client's word stood, by the author's eyes. Not seen in a client: the change when a starter's quest is taken; retail.
- **Quest log** (always on): item objectives matched by item **name**, parsed for both "Name: 0/1" and "0/1 Name" (which Forever uses is unverified). An objective's text names its item only while the client has that item's data, so: a scan that finds an objective without its text takes the name it had this session (`objectiveNames`, by quest and objective; a quest that leaves the log takes its names with it), and one that has never had a name is asked about again, on `QUEST_LOG_UPDATE` and by the clock (`ScheduleRecheck`). Before, a scan forgot a name whose text was gone, which took the item's quest icon away, and the name's return was a change with a refresh of everything; and the event that brought a name could come within the two seconds of the last check, which let it pass, with no other event to follow while the toon stood still. Seen (retail 12.1.5.70961, five timing runs): two objectives of one quest named 0.2 s into the first forced refresh of a session, and every window drawn a second time 0.5 s later. Both happened in one session once they could be told apart (the same client, 2026-11-21): the clock's recheck found the two names 1.5 s after the first scan, and half a minute later a full refresh found their texts gone again and kept the names, with no refresh for it. The export's `[Quest Value]` has a line for it (`Quest Log Names`). Evidence: offline (`scenarios/quest-compare.lua`, with faults planted). Quests in the log always count, finished objectives included.
- **QuestieDB** (`LibQuestieDB`, optional, contract 2+): quests not accepted yet. Its `Item.relatedQuests` holds about 13% of the pairs, so the module builds its own index from `objectives[3]`, `objectives[6]`, `requiredSourceItems`, `sourceItemId` and `startedBy[3]`, in a coroutine at 3 ms a frame.
  - **`sourceItemId`** is the item a quest hands over on accept and takes back at turn-in (a letter, a kit, the Cursed Eye of Paleth for quests 292 and 293). It is often the only field that names the item, and it is not an item objective either, so neither the index nor the quest log found it without this field. In the Forever data it adds about 800 items, nearly all of class Quest or Miscellaneous. Offline: `scenarios/quest-items.lua`. Forever client (1.60.1.70938, a hunter): the eye in the bags went from no quest icon to the quest icon. Retail not seen.
- **QuestieDB data shape:** baked into the TOC as base64 CBOR (grep finds nothing; use `LibQuestieDB.Quest.Get(id, "field")`). `objectives` is sparse, race masks exceed 2^32 (use arithmetic, not `bit.band`), and a bare classic faction mask (77 / 178) is compared with the toon's faction.
- **AllTheThings is a second source of quest data** (`CaerdonQuestSourceATT.lua`, `NS.QuestSourceATT`; author decision): for retail, where there is no QuestieDB, and beside it on WoW Forever, so that either addon gives the upcoming quests. The retail client has no API from an item to its quests, and its own data files hold item objectives for a fraction of the quests only (most quest data comes from the server, quest by quest).
  - *What is read, while AllTheThings is loaded:* its global table's `GetRawField`, `GetRawFieldContainer` and `GetRelativeValue` (5.4.16, `src/Cache.lua`, `src/base.lua`). These are its internals and no promised interface: every read is protected, a function that is gone leaves the source `unsupported`, and the export's `[Quest Value]` has its state, its version and the functions not found. `GetRawField` is the lookup that creates nothing; `SearchForField` leaves an empty table behind for every miss.
  - *An item's quests:* the caches `itemIDAsCost` (a quest's `cost` of type `"i"`, what is handed in; and the items that start a quest: `providers` of type `"i"`, `qss`) and `qItemID` (`qis`, what a quest has collected; `qss`). A hit is a quest where its `key` is `"questID"`: vendors' wares and recipes cost items too, and many other kinds of entry carry a `questID`. On WoW Forever's data a collected item sits on the quest's objective (`key` `"objectiveID"`), whose parent is the quest. Copies under its hidden lists (hidden triggers, unsorted, never implemented) are left out. *What is listed under an item is read once and kept until AllTheThings' ready call comes again* (`itemQuests`), not dropped at a refresh as the rest is: Mark of Honor is the cost of about 9,900 things in the retail data, and walking them was one call of 6.7 ms in every full refresh of a window that shows it (retail 12.1.5.70961, AllTheThings 5.4.17; the counters named the item). Whether a quest found there is hidden is asked at each call, since AllTheThings changes that as the session goes. Evidence: offline (`scenarios/quest-source-att.lua`; the code from before fails it), and timed in that client after it: the quest lookups of a refresh of 518 items took 15.8 ms for 23.7, the worst of them 0.76 ms.
  - *Whether the toon can take a quest* (`Source:Blocker`), from the quest's own table and the headers above it: removed from the game (`u` 2), another faction's (`r`), race's (`races`) or class's (`c`), locked by a finished quest (`altQuests`), a breadcrumb whose next quest is taken, outleveled (`lvl` as a pair): behind the toon. An event's (`e`), repeatable, a profession not learned (`requireSkill`), a level not reached: out of reach for now. The level is the bound, as for QuestieDB's quests (author decision): a quest within the player's range counts whether its chain is begun or not. A first version kept every later quest of a chain not begun out, on the thought that retail's scaling quests have no level to bound them; that was a guess about retail and treated one source's quests differently from the other's. What the range lets through on a retail toon at the level cap is to be looked at there. *Beyond the range, a later quest of a chain the toon is on is still to come, however many steps away* (`SourcesBegun`, `Begun`): the quests before it are followed back (`sourceQuests`, as many of them as `sqreq` asks), and one that is done or in the log anywhere back there means the chain is begun. Fields are read raw where the quest's own are meant: a missing field runs AllTheThings' class code, and its `name` asks the client to load the quest.
  - *Beside QuestieDB:* what AllTheThings names for an item is added to what the index has (`GetIndexedQuests`). A quest QuestieDB has is judged from QuestieDB, also where only AllTheThings ties the item to it; one it lacks is judged from AllTheThings (`provider.known`; the export's quest line ends in "judged from AllTheThings"). AllTheThings has no item a quest hands over, so the rule for those (below) rests on QuestieDB.
  - *Its data comes at login,* in one go or over many frames (its own setting), and it calls the handlers registered before it was through (`AddEventHandler("OnReady")`). It is an optional dependency so that it loads first and the call can be listened for; where the call was missed, its data being there is looked for a few times after the world is entered. Either way what was answered without it is dropped and the icons are drawn again (`RefreshItems("quest data", "AllTheThings is ready")`).
  - *Its data is not copied into the addon:* its folder has two licence files that disagree.
  - Evidence: offline (`scenarios/quest-source-att.lua`, against a stand-in written from AllTheThings' source). Retail client (12.1.5.70961, AllTheThings 5.4.17, a druid of 90): the source is ready and its ready call was heard; for the bags, the bank and auction lists it was asked about 509 items and named 221 quests; quests came out as removed from the game (9714, 9715 for Sanguine Hibiscus 24246), an event's (29433), repeatable (29692), each "judged from AllTheThings", with no error. A bag refresh of 117 items has `GetQuestItemInfo` at 8.6 ms of 124 ms, one call of them 6.6 ms: which item that is and what it walks is not looked at yet. WoW Forever: ready beside QuestieDB, its ready call heard. Not seen: an item it names as starting a quest, a chain.
  - *It does not tie every quest's item to its quest.* Sacred Mojo 36758, a Quest-class drop, is in its retail data once, as a plain item under a zone with the note "Only used for a given quest, can otherwise be vendored.", and in no quest's `cost`, `qis` or `providers`. No quest is known for such an item, so nothing can say its quest is trivial, and it keeps the quest icon its class gives (below), whatever the option for gray quests says. Seen on retail: the item on an auction with the quest icon and "Quests: none". The quest is 12068, Voices From the Dust, and the retail client does not tie the item to it either (`/cwtest quest 12068 36758 36757`, 12.1.5.70961): its one objective is "0/1 Drakkari Tablets" (item 36757, which AllTheThings has as the quest's `qis`), level 30, trivial for a toon of 90; the five Sacred Mojo the quest's text asks for are no objective. So neither source reachable on retail names it, and what would is AllTheThings listing the item on the quest.
  - *The retail client gives the objectives of a quest that is not in the log,* once its data is loaded, with the item's name in the text (the same probe). Nothing reads them yet: they could name further items of a quest that is being loaded anyway, and no quest for an item that no source ties to one.
- **How far ahead a quest counts is the player's choice** (`Icon.UpcomingQuestLevels`, a dropdown of "2", "5", "10", "20", "any", default "5"; `Icon.IncludeTrivialQuests`, default off; both in the quest icon's block on the Icons page, under the option for quests not picked up yet; `UpcomingSettings`, read into the toon's context, so a change of settings judges every quest again). Someone who follows a guide that has items bought early wants a wider range, someone short of bag slots a narrower one, and someone who does every quest wants the trivial ones too. They hold for both sources.
- **A quest's level and whether it is trivial are the client's to say, for both sources** (`CaerdonQuestClient.lua`, `NS.QuestClient:GetFacts`; the author's question: why an addon's data where the quest has its own). QuestieDB's level can be behind the game (the quests WoW Forever added), and AllTheThings has no quest level at all. The level a quest asks for stays the quest data's: no function of either client gives it.
  - *The client has no word on a quest whose data it does not hold, and answers all the same* (WoW Forever 1.60.1.71037, typed in game): for quests that were not in the log `C_QuestLog.GetQuestDifficultyLevel` gave 0 (93685, 2521) and `HaveQuestData(93685)` false; `IsQuestTrivial` gave false for 93685 and for a quest ID that does not exist (999999), and true for 116, a quest of level 15 whose data the client had. So false is what it says without data, and nothing is read of a quest while `HaveQuestData` says no.
  - *It is asked for the data* (`C_QuestLog.RequestLoadQuestByID`, answered by `QUEST_DATA_LOAD_RESULT` with the quest's ID and whether it has it; typed: two seconds after the request `HaveQuestData(93685)` was true and the level 55). Twenty-five quests at a time, from a timer and never from inside a draw, through the addon's own listener, so the counters' ledger has the requests with every other load, under `CaerdonQuestClient.lua`. A request without an answer is given up on after five seconds (the longest answer seen took 1.9 s); a quest the client refused or never answered for is not asked for again that session. It was ten at a time and ten seconds while nothing waited for an answer; an icon can wait now (below), and asking costs about 0.02 ms a quest.
  - *Only where its word can change the verdict:* never for a quest in the log or done, not for one that is behind the toon whatever its level (another faction's, a chain gone past it), not for one the level it is known to ask for keeps out, and with trivial quests wanted only for a quest with no level it asks for. For a toon in the thirties that leaves out most of what a bag names.
  - *Where the quest data has no level, the item's verdict waits for the answer* (author decision, after retail showed icons drawn and taken away). A quest judged from AllTheThings has nothing that says whether it is trivial until the client does, and at the level cap nearly every older quest is. So while the client's answer for such a quest is on its way, the quest's verdict is `pending` and not kept, the item names the quest (`waitingFor` in the quest info), and `GetCaerdonStatus` is not ready for "the client's word on a quest" with the key `quest:<ID>`. The quest client tells the queue when that quest has its answer, of whatever kind: data, a refusal, or the request given up on (`RetryWaitingFor`), and only the buttons that waited are drawn, with no refresh. A wait ends by itself six seconds after the quest was first asked for, which is below what the queue's own retries of a button add up to; the quest is then judged without the client, as a refused one is. An item a quest in the log takes does not wait (it has the quest icon whatever the answer), nor does anything with the quest icon switched off. A quest judged from QuestieDB never waits: that data has a level, and on WoW Forever the first verdict was right every time. The counters' late draws have the wait under its name. Offline: `scenarios/quest-client.lua`, with faults planted one at a time each failing it. Retail client (12.1.5.70961, the druid of 90, auction lists over two minutes): 35 draws waited for the client's word on a quest, 0.50 s on average and 2.15 s at the longest; 57 requests, 54 answered after 0.37 s on average; three runs of answers changed no icon and refreshed nothing, where the session before had five such refreshes. WoW Forever (1.60.1.71037, the hunter of 37, bags and an auction list) ran the code with nothing to wait for: no draw waited and no quest was asked for, since the client had the data of the two quests it was needed for (with gray quests included, only a quest with no level it asks for needs the client at all).
  - *The quest data judges meanwhile, and an answer is matched to its quest* (`CaerdonQuestItems:CompareWithClient`): the quests whose data came, where they were answered since the caches were last dropped, are judged again, and the icons are drawn again only where one of them means something else for an icon than before (`RefreshItems("quest data", "the client's word: quest 93685: kept out by the options -> upcoming")`). The answers of a run are one check: half a second after one came, and while more are on their way three seconds at most.
  - *What changes in a verdict.* A quest judged from QuestieDB: its level is the client's (for the stand-in below), and trivial is the client's `IsQuestTrivial` in place of the level against the toon's trivial range. One judged from AllTheThings: trivial, which nothing said before unless the data happened to be there, and a quest with no level it asks for (`lvl`) goes by the client's level for it, the rule QuestieDB's quests have (below).
  - *What the client said stands while the toon is of the level it said it for* (`kept`): the client drops data again, and a word that was there for one refresh and gone for the next would turn a verdict back and forth, with a request and a refresh each time (the quest log's objective names are kept for the same reason). The test addon's recorder forgets it with the other caches (`ForgetClientAnswers`), since a replay starts a session of its own.
  - *"Kept out by the options" is a state of its own where answers are compared* (`StateOf`), for a quest event as for the client's word: where that is all that is left for an item, its class gives it no quest icon, so it is not the same as "not wanted".
  - *Export:* `[Quest Value]` has `The Client's Quest Data` (how many quests of the last refresh were judged with its word, with what it said earlier and without; what was asked this session, what came and after how long, refused, not answered) and `The Client's Answers Compared`; a quest's line says "level 55 by the client", or why the client had no word.
  - *What it costs is measured where it is spent, since none of it is inside a draw's figure.* Always on, in the export's `The Client's Quest Data Cost`: the time spent putting requests (in all, and in one frame at the most) and the time spent judging quests again when their answers came. In the counters' ledger: the requests under `CaerdonQuestClient.lua` with the other loads, and every refresh an answer caused under `quest data` with "the client's word" in its detail, which is what a player sees of this as an icon that changes after the window is drawn. With the profile on: `GetFacts` (the part that is inside a verdict: three questions to the client for a quest it has the data of), `Pump` and `CompareWithClient`. A timing run names a refresh that came from an answer during it (`REFRESHED AGAIN BY`). What decides whether this is worth its price: the refreshes by the client's word in a session (each is one redraw of every window; once a quest's word is in, it is kept for the toon's level), the longest frame of asking, and a refresh's time with and without the data being there.
  - Evidence: offline only (`scenarios/quest-client.lua`: a hand-made client that answers a request after a moment, at once from inside the call, with a refusal, or never; faults planted one at a time each failed it). Forever client (1.60.1.71037, a hunter of 37 with QuestieDB and AllTheThings, BetterBags' bags of 50 items and an auction list, one session of two minutes):
    - *The requests:* 21 in the session. 18 were answered, after 0.27 s on average and 0.31 s at the longest; 3 were refused (quests 9268, 7943, 7929), and a refusal is the same event with `success` false, after about as long; none went unanswered. The client had most quests' data without being asked: 54 quests of a refresh were judged with its word.
    - *The cost:* putting the requests 0.55 ms in all over 9 frames, 0.12 ms in one at the most; judging 11 quests again for their answers 0.36 ms; `GetFacts` 27 calls and 0.15 ms of a bag refresh of 32 ms, with no memory. A reload came to the world and to idle as fast as the day's reloads before it (2.9 s, 1.2 s).
    - *What a player saw of it:* nothing. Three runs of answers changed no icon, so no refresh came of them, and the bags' snapshot across the reload that brought this had 40 items the same and none changed.
    - *A verdict:* Flawless Draenethyst Sphere 8244 on an auction, quests 93685 and 93686 "level 55 by the client, requires 0", kept out at a range of 5, as QuestieDB's level had them. Bottle of Moonshine 1942 in the bags, quest 116 "level 15 by the client, requires 12", trivial and with no icon; the fixture recorded then holds the client's two answers and replays to that.
    - *The client keeps quest data across a reload:* the session after it asked for one quest (the refused 9268 again) and judged 26 with the client's word. The asking is a first session's cost.
    - *The figures do not say* what a bag refresh cost before, item for item: the day's earlier record is of 65 items and another set (38.4 ms against 32.3 and 32.9). Both timing runs were made after the answers had come, so a refresh while the data is not there is not timed; the asking it adds is the figure above.
  - Retail client (12.1.5.70961, a druid of 90 with AllTheThings 5.4.17 and no QuestieDB, BetterBags' bags of 117 items, the bank and auction lists, a session of five minutes):
    - *The requests:* 96 in the session, 77 answered after 0.62 s on average and 1.91 s at the longest, 19 refused (as slowly), none unanswered. Asking cost 1.96 ms in all over 27 frames, 0.15 ms in one at the most; judging 56 quests again 1.71 ms; `GetFacts` 2 calls of a bag refresh of 124 ms (128 ms the day before on 115 items).
    - *The level of a scaling quest is the level it has for the toon:* 90 for a Darkmoon quest with no `lvl` (29433), 30 for an Outland quest that asks for 10 (29691), which the client calls trivial. So the stand-in keeps out no quest the toon has reached.
    - *Answers change icons there, and it is seen.* Five of eight runs of answers refreshed, the first for 18 quests as the bank opened. AllTheThings has no quest level, so until the client answers nothing says a quest is trivial, and at the level cap nearly every older quest is: its item is drawn with the quest icon and loses it about a second later ("upcoming -> kept out by the options"). On an auction list the author saw the icons go. With QuestieDB's levels (Forever) the first verdict was right every time. That is what the waiting verdict above is for.
  - *A cache of the addon's own for what the client said is not kept between sessions* (the author's question). The client has one: `Cache/WDB/<locale>/questcache.wdb` in each flavor's folder (490 KB on the retail install looked at), written as the client exits, and WoW Forever answered for 26 quests after a reload with one request. A second one would be small (a level and a flag for a few hundred quests a toon) and would have to be dropped with every level and every build, since trivial is said for a level. It is worth building only if a login shows the client asking for the same quests again; the export's `The Client's Quest Data` line of a fresh login says that.
  - *Retail asked again after a reload:* 57 requests in the session after one, 96 in the one before. Whether they were the same quests is not on record (the windows opened were not the same), so whether the client keeps a quest's data across a reload there is still open.
  - **Not seen in a client:** a request left unanswered; whether the client drops a quest's data again within a session; whether a request sets off `QUEST_LOG_UPDATE` for others; how many quests a fresh login asks for again.
- **A later quest of a chain the toon is on always counts, whatever its level** (a fixed rule and no setting, author decision: being on the chain says more than a level range, and nobody would switch it off; the page says so in a line under the two options). For QuestieDB's quests (`PrerequisitesBegun`, `ChainQuestBegun`): a quest the level range would keep out is still to come where the quests before it are done, in the log, or themselves further along a begun chain (every one of `preQuestGroup`, one of `preQuestSingle`, followed back with a budget). The two fields are asked only for a quest beyond the range, so a recording made before this replays as it did unless its quest is one of those. Within the range QuestieDB's quests are not asked about their chain, as before. For AllTheThings' quests the same lifts the level (above).
  - *A quest whose data has no level it asks for goes by its own level,* the client's where it has the quest's data (`requiredLevel` 0 in QuestieDB: 113 quests of 6080 in its Forever data, 106 of them with IDs from 90000 on, all of quest level 50 and up; no `lvl` in AllTheThings). Seen on Forever (1.60.1.71037, a hunter of 37 with the range at 2): Flawless Draenethyst Sphere 8244 on an auction had the quest icon for quests 93685 and 93686, "level 55, requires 0", while their classic twins 2521 and 2522 ask for 45. The quest's level is never below the level it asks for, so the stand-in keeps a quest out a little longer than its true level would, and never lets one in early.
  - Evidence: offline (`scenarios/spent-quest-items.lua` for QuestieDB's quests and the settings, `scenarios/quest-source-att.lua` for AllTheThings'; `scenarios/character-settings.lua` builds the page). No client has seen the options or the rule.
- **A commodity opened from the auction results has a big icon of its own** (`AuctionHouseFrame.CommoditiesBuyFrame.BuyDisplay.ItemDisplay.ItemButton`; the client picks the view by the key info's `isCommodity`, in both clients). Only the other view's icon was asked for (`OnSelectBrowseResult`), which showed once a quest's item could have an icon in the auction house: Sacred Mojo 36758 had the quest icon in its row and none on the page it opened (retail 12.1.5.70961). It is asked for with the other view's size and offsets, which is the same template and has not been measured. Offline: `scenarios/default-refresh.lua`, which the code from before fails. Retail client (12.1.5.70961): the commodity's big icon had the quest icon ("asked for by Auction; on it now: icon yes"). WoW Forever (1.60.1.71037): the commodity's big icon is asked for (Flawless Draenethyst Sphere 8244, "asked for by Auction; on it now: icon no", which is its verdict there).
- **The quest icon shows on auction house listings** (`Icon.ShowQuestItemsInAuction`, on by default; "Show in the auction house" in the quest icon's block, as the star, the icons for another toon and the older expansion's mark have; author decision). The window had the icon switched off outright, from when it only said "a quest item, or one in the log"; an item a quest still to come takes is one to buy there. It is the fourth icon that brings the list's icon column (`AuctionIcons`, `WantsIconColumn` in `features/Auction.lua`), and it needs the quest icon itself switched on. On by default, so a player who had the other three off gets the column back until this one is off too. Evidence: offline (`scenarios/pawn-scales.lua`, the column and the display rule). Not seen in a client.
- **Upcoming quests (`UpcomingBlocker`):** not completed or hidden by Questie, not a holiday sort, right race and class, not repeatable, profession learned, no exclusive / later-chain quest done, takeable within `UPCOMING_LEVEL_WINDOW` (5) levels, not trivial, and an item-started quest needs that item. Without the level and trivial rules a level 23 toon gets the icon on about 400 items instead of 70.
- **What the quest icon replaces** (`QUEST_REPLACES` in `CaerdonItem.lua`): no status, `collected`, `sellable`, `send`, `oldexpansion`, and everything to learn (`own`, `other`, their Plus forms, `otherNoLoot`, `lowSkill`, `lowSkillPlus`). The piece has to be in the bags at the turn-in, and the learnable icon is read as "sell it, use it or send it": on Forever selling is how a look is learned, and a pet, mount or recipe is used up by learning it.
  - *An upgrade keeps its arrow* (author decision): gear a quest names may be gear the quest means the toon to wear. One that was a look to learn as well gets the quest icon with the arrow as its badge (`bindingResult.upgradeStatus`, as on the learnable icon).
  - *With the quest icon switched off* the status logic is told of no quest value, and the look to learn is what shows.
  - *The vendor, the journal and crafting orders* change a learnable status after the verdict (`ProcessItem`); they leave `quest` alone.
  - *The test addon's verdict tests* retire an item a quest takes (`questTakes` in its facts), since it shows the quest icon whatever the shape's rule says.
  - Offline: `scenarios/quest-items.lua`, which the code from before fails. Forever client (1.60.1.70959, a hunter 33): Hillman's Cloak 3719, an objective of a quest still to come, went from the star to the quest icon in the bags. Not seen: an upgrade a quest takes, the badge on the quest icon; retail.
  - **Unverified on Forever:** whether a piece worn to learn its look still counts at the turn-in once it is back in the bags, and whether handing it in teaches the look.
- **A Quest-class item has the quest icon by its class, until every quest known for it is behind the toon** (`isOver`; `questsOver` in `GetCaerdonStatus`). Before, the class alone gave the icon and the item's quests were never asked about, so a quest's item kept it after the turn-in.
  - *The player's options hold for the class's icon too* (`keptOutByOptions` in the quest info, `optedOut` on each quest; author decision): where every quest that is not behind the toon is beyond the range of levels chosen, or trivial with those left out, the item has no quest icon by its class, in the bags as on an auction, and is not one to get rid of either. A quest kept out for another reason (it can be done again, an event's, a profession not learned, hidden by Questie) is not the player's choice, and its item keeps the icon. Seen on Forever (1.60.1.71037, a hunter of 37 with the range at 2): The Greatest Race of Hunters 18361 on an auction house listing had the quest icon while its one quest, 7503, was "needs level 54"; the range had been obeyed and the class gave the icon. A first answer gave the class's icon to the copy the toon holds alone; one rule for every copy replaced it. Offline: `scenarios/spent-quest-items.lua`.
  - *Behind the toon* (`verdict.over`): a quest that is done and cannot be done again, or one the toon will never take: another faction's, race's or class's, one a finished quest excludes, one its chain has gone past, one whose parent is done or that is no longer offered, one it has outleveled. *Not behind it:* in the log, still to come, done but repeatable, hidden by Questie, an event's, a profession not learned, a level not reached, trivial (it can still be done), a starter item not owned, no quest data.
  - *Every rule that can say "for good" is asked, whichever blocker is found first* (`UpcomingBlocker`). The reason shown is the first one; were the answer the first one's too, a quest its chain had gone past would stop being behind the toon when a quest it is exclusive with is accepted, and the item's icon would come back.
  - *A quest that can be done again does not count where a quest that handed the item over is behind the toon* (`setAside`, in `GetItemQuestInfo`). Library Scrip 3898 on Forever: quest 578 hands it over (`sourceItemId`) and is done; quest 579 takes it for the book 578 speaks of, is repeatable and has no `parentQuest`, `preQuestSingle` or other tie to 578 in QuestieDB, so by its own verdict it is never behind the toon. An item handed over was that quest's own, and no more of it come. The repeatable quest in the log counts as any quest in the log does. An item a repeatable quest takes and no quest hands over (a turn-in gathered from mobs) keeps the icon. For this the index names both roles of a quest that hands an item over and takes it as an objective ("objective, provided").
  - *Such an item keeps the quest icon, with what is left of it as the small mark* (`repeatableLeft` in the quest info; `questRepeats` and `questMark` in `GetCaerdonStatus`; author decision). The repeatable quest can still take the item, so the quest icon is true of it, and that nothing else is to it is said beside: the item is judged as any spent quest item is, through the auction step, and where that ends as `sellable` or `auction` the status is `quest` with that as its small mark. One with a use or an equip effect has the quest icon alone. The mark is part of the verdict and kept with it. With the quest icon switched off the item is `sellable` or `auction` itself.
  - *An item no quest is known for goes by the level it asks for itself* (`CaerdonItemMixin:IsQuestItemBeyondRange`): beyond the player's range it has no quest icon by its class. Seen on Forever (1.60.1.71037, a hunter of 37 with the range at 2): Top Half of Advanced Armorsmithing: Volume I 18780 on an auction, a Quest-class item that asks for level 50 and that neither QuestieDB nor AllTheThings names a quest for, had the icon. An item that asks for no level keeps it.
  - *An item no quest is known for keeps the icon:* nothing says it is done with. That is every Quest-class item the log does not name where QuestieDB is not loaded (retail), and a quest's reward that no quest takes (QuestieDB has no rewards).
  - *What is left of it* (`CaerdonItemMixin:IsSpentQuestItem`): `sellable` where it has no use, goes in no equipment slot and has no equip effect; unmarked otherwise, since what can be used or worn may be kept for the fun of it. No vendor price is asked for: most such items have none and can only be destroyed, and the icon says that nothing is left to do with the item. The auction step may still make it `auction`. Asked with the quest icon switched off too.
  - *The risk:* an item that a finished quest took and that something the quest data does not name still needs. The export's `[Quest Value]` lists the quests and which of them are behind the toon; such an item is a reason for a guard here.
  - Offline: `scenarios/spent-quest-items.lua`, which the code from before fails. No client has seen it.
- **The quest icon stays on a copy that can be read** (`GetCopyStatus`): `readable` replaces every status but `quest`. The readable icon is added per bag slot after the verdict and stood in the quest icon's place: the pages of a book a quest in the log asked for all showed it (Forever client 1.60.1.71037, a hunter, Green Hills of Stranglethorn - Page 25 with Chapter IV in the log). That an item belongs to a quest says more than that it has text, and what can be read is what players sell or delete unread, so the icon stays where it comes from the class alone too (no quest known for the item, which is every such item on retail). The readable icon is back once every quest known for the item is behind the toon, and shows with the quest icon switched off. The test addon's bag states have a case for each (`readable`, `readableForQuest`). Offline only.
  - *The small mark on the quest icon* (`smallMark`, the sixth value of `GetCaerdonStatus`; `markBadge` and `SetSmallMark` in `SetItemButtonStatus`): what a quest's item is beside that. (The other way round, the quest icon is the small mark on a starter's star: above.) `readable` for a copy that can be read (the second value of `GetCopyStatus`), `sellable` or `auction` from the verdict (above), which comes first where both hold. That a copy can be read belongs to the copy, so it is not kept with the verdict and not put on the shared binding result: `ProcessItem` hands the mark to the drawing. The mark is the other status's own icon in the badge's place and at its size, with no ring, as the second mark of a piece kept for a look is, and it goes by the window's rule for that icon (no sell icon, no mark; no auction icon, the sell icon's). An upgrade's arrow comes first in that place. Where a window draws no quest icon (`displayInfo.questIcon`), or there is no window, the item is drawn as the other thing it is. The export's `[Caerdon]` has `Small Mark On The Icon` when there is one. Evidence: offline (`scenarios/draw-path.lua`, and the drawing compared call by call with the code from before: only the sets of a copy that can be read differ; `scenarios/spent-quest-items.lua` for the value). Forever client (1.60.1.71037): the readable mark on a quest's page was looked at and found to read well, with no export. Not seen in a client: the sell and the auction mark.
- **Refresh:** a quest accepted, removed or turned in is compared before anything is refreshed (`CaerdonQuestItems:CompareWithAnswers`; Refresh below). "Behind the toon" is a state of its own there, apart from "not wanted": a Quest-class item keeps its icon through the second and loses it with the first. The module refreshes by itself when its data comes: the index built or Questie ready (`quest data`), the client's word on a quest it was asked to load where that changes an icon (`quest data`, "the client's word: ..."), the names of the log's objectives arriving on a `QUEST_LOG_UPDATE` (`quest log`). A level gained is the core's (`level`).
- **Settings:** `Icon.ShowQuestItems` (every window), `Icon.ShowUpcomingQuestItems`. **Export:** `[Quest Value]`.
- **In game:** one drink showed the icon in bags and at the BetterBags vendor; the rest is offline. Unverified: the objective text layout and the index build time.
- **Parked idea:** status icons on Questie's map pins. QuestieDB has no item rewards, and Questie has no public hook for pins.

## Auction Value

`CaerdonAuctionValue.lua`; `GetAuctionValueInfo()` always returns the full breakdown.

- **Statuses:** `auction` replaces `sellable`; `auctionGoods` marks trade goods with no status. Soulbound, bind-on-pickup, account-bound and quest items are never candidates.
  - **The module's verdict is not the icon.** `GetAuctionValueInfo()` says `shouldAuction` for anything that clears the profit bar, and the export prints that. The icon is only given to what is `sellable` and to an item of class `Tradegoods` or one the client calls a crafting reagent. So a quiver of arrows, which clears the bar by its count alone (a Forever hunter: 2472 Razor Arrows, "Should Auction: true"), gets none: class Projectile, no vendor price. Offline: `scenarios/always-on.lua`.
- **Rule:** `profit = quantity * (price * 0.95 - vendorPrice) - deposit * depositRisk`; `required = clamp(vendorValue * margin, floor, 1g)`. Floor: `Icon.AuctionMinProfit`, `"auto"` (25c per level, minimum 1s) or copper as a string.
- **Rule sets by max stack size:** single item `depositRisk = 2`, `margin = 1`; stackable `depositRisk = 1`, `margin = 0.25`, and quantity is everything owned including the bank. `BAG_UPDATE_DELAYED` re-judges stackables and refreshes only when a verdict flips.
  - **The stacks watched for that go with the verdicts at a full refresh** (`CaerdonAuctionValue:InvalidateCaches`, called from `RefreshItems`). A stack that is drawn again is judged and watched again. One that is not needs no watching: its button is redrawn or owed a draw anyway, and that draw judges it on what is owned then (the owned count is in the status cache key). Kept for the session, the list only grew, items since sold included, and every change in the bags asked the client about each. Offline (`scenarios/always-on.lua`, which the code from before fails). Forever client (1.60.1.70938, a rogue under BetterBags, 2026-11-16): no icon in the bags or the bank changed, the auction icons among them, and a change in the bags cost 0.04 ms where it had cost 0.15, on three calls.
- **Price:** Auctionator's public API (`GetAuctionPriceByItemLink`, `GetAuctionAgeByItemLink`, `RegisterForDBUpdate`); older than 7 days is ignored. No sales data exists, so the price used is the lower of the last seen price and the 14-day median daily low (3+ days; from internal `Auctionator.Database:GetPriceHistory`, pcall'd).
- **Forever price keys:** gear below item level 168 is keyed by item ID alone, so random suffixes share one price.
- **Deposit:** unknown formula; starts at the larger of 1s or 15% of vendor price. `features/Auction.lua` records a sample when an item goes into the sell slot (`CaerdonAuctionData`).
- **Icon:** only in bags and bank (`displayInfo.auctionIcon`); elsewhere `auction` draws as `sellable` and `auctionGoods` draws nothing. `Auctioneer` atlas, else the gold coin.
- **Settings:** `Icon.ShowAuctionValue`, `Icon.AuctionMinProfit`. **Export:** `[Auction Value]`. **In game:** untested.

## Housing

Retail only: `types/HousingMixin.lua` does nothing below interface 110207 or without `Enum.ItemClass.Housing`, and Forever has neither.

- **The catalog since 12.0.5 has one entry per thing,** owned or not, with the totals over every variant of it: `totalNumStored`, `totalNumPlaced`, `remainingRedeemable`, beside `recordID` and `entryType`. An entry no longer comes in an "owned" and an "unowned" kind, and the lookups take no "try owned" argument. `Blizzard_Deprecated/Mainline/Deprecated_12_0_5.lua` describes the change and, while the CVar `loadDeprecationFallbacks` is on, wraps the catalog's functions to put the old fields back (`entryID`, `quantity`, `numPlaced`, `showQuantity`), with every entry called unowned. The addon reads none of those.
- **Owned** is what is placed plus the larger of stored and still to redeem: the client reports the same copies under both. A copy in the bags is not owned decor (it has not been redeemed), except for a service item. The item's stack size caps the counts and is the limit at which another copy gets no icon.
- **The lookup by record is a guess that was kept.** When the item's entry says nothing is owned, or there is none, the catalog is asked for a record whose ID is the item's ID, and an answer replaces the entry. Whether an item's ID is ever its record's ID is not known. The export's `[Housing]` section says which was used: "Record ID From Item" and "Found By Item ID As Record".
- **Dyes get no icon,** and nothing is asked about a dye's color.
- **The catalog has no entry for a service item or a dye,** so neither is ever "still on its way". An item with no entry, no counts in its tooltip and no copy in the bags is asked for again up to six times, each time with a full refresh; for these two kinds that wait never ended in anything (retail client: a Harvesting Hatchet in the warband bank, six full refreshes a session, and it was not drawn at all while they ran).
- **A service item is judged by its tooltip, not by counts.** The Harvesting Hatchet teaches an ability; its tooltip carries the "Already known" requirement line once the toon has it (retail client, 12.1.5.70783), and then it gets no icon (author decision: neutral; the item is not needed once the ability is learned). Counts cannot say that: nothing is stored or placed, and `C_Item.GetItemCount` as it is asked does not count the warband bank, so the known hatchet read as not owned and got the star. A service item whose tooltip does not say so keeps the count rule (no client has shown one). Seen in a retail client: the hatchet in the warband bank went from the star to no icon and nothing else in bank or bags changed, and a session with bags, bank and auction house open made no refresh from the housing code where the one before made twelve.
- **Evidence:** offline, `scenarios/housing.lua` on hand-made entries in the new shape, and, once, the code from before the change under Blizzard's own wrapper against the changed code without it: the same answer for 1792 combinations of counts, item kinds and bag copies, each asked three times. Retail client (12.1.5.70783, a druid): the bank and bags show what they showed before for every housing item the catalog knows, and an unowned decor, an owned one at its stack limit and a dye export as the scenario has them; the lookup by record answered for none of the three.

## Refresh

- `RefreshItems` (cache wipe, generation bump, debounced feature refresh) runs on `TRANSMOG_COLLECTION_UPDATED`, `TRANSMOG_COLLECTION_SOURCE_ADDED`, `..._SOURCE_REMOVED`, `TRANSMOG_COSMETIC_COLLECTION_SOURCE_ADDED`, on a mount, toy, pet or recipe learned, and on equipment, equipment set and expansion changes. Each of those events is a change. The ones that also come when nothing changed go through the watcher (next point), and the signal modules ask for it when their own signature changes.
  - **The windows draw a tenth of a second after the last refresh of a run,** and every refresh starts that wait over. The caches are emptied at once. The events of one change come together (a quest turned in with three looks learned was four refreshes within 0.03 s on a Forever client), and the wait makes them one redraw. It stays at 0.1 s: shorter gains nothing that is seen, longer is an icon that lags behind a piece put on, and the events that came with nothing changed are compared by the watcher before they get here. That is reasoning; no timing has been made of another value.
  - **So refreshes and redraws are two counts.** A refresh is a call of `RefreshItems`; a redraw is one round of every feature's `Refresh()`. A refresh in a fight is no redraw until the fight's end, which is one for all of them. The counters keep both (Counters).
- **An event that also comes when nothing changed is compared first** (`CaerdonRefresh.lua`, `NS.Refresh:Watch`). A watch names its events, reads what they stand for into a flat table, and asks for a refresh only when that reading differs from the last one.
  - *Skills* (`SKILL_LINES_CHANGED`, `TRADE_SKILL_DATA_SOURCE_CHANGED`): the first event comes for every skill point and in a run at every login. The reading is the toon's professions with their ranks, on a client that has a skill list (`C_SkillInfo`, Forever) every line of it with its rank, and the ranks the recipe code has saved. The profession tools and bag slot modules no longer listen for the event themselves.
    - **A profession is read with its cap.** A trainer or a book raises the cap with the rank as it was, and the book's tooltip says "Already known" from then on. Forever client: Expert Cookbook 16072, learned at Cooking 150 of 150, kept its star at the vendor, since the verdict from before stood and no rank had changed. The cap is read from both answers that hold one (`GetProfessionInfo`'s fourth value, and `maxRank` of the profession's line in the skill list): which of them has it for a secondary profession on Forever is not on record. No other line's cap is read: a weapon skill's rises with the level, which refreshes by itself. That the skill line event comes when a cap rises is from Blizzard's Forever skills frame, which shows rank and cap and listens for nothing else; not seen in a client.
    - **The second event is the profession window opening on a profession.** The recipe code saves that profession's ranks then, a recipe's verdict reads them, and no skill line event need follow. A rank the client does not answer for yet when the event comes is found by the check after the burst. The known recipes it also saves then are not in the reading: a recipe's "already known" comes from its tooltip first, and the saved list is what other toons read at their next login.
    - **The recipe code saves the ranks as the watch asks for them** (`CaerdonRecipe:AddSkillReading`), where the client answers for them (`IsTradeSkillReady`). The watcher's frame hears an event before the recipe code's, so a reading taken first held the toon's new rank beside the rank saved before the point, and the saved rank was a second change when the burst was over: two refreshes and two redraws for every profession point (Forever client, a session of crafting: 60 of its 106 refreshes). Where the client does not answer, a point is the toon's rank alone and the saved rank follows at the window's event. Evidence: offline (`scenarios/ingredients.lua`, with the real recipe code and watcher; it fails on the code from before). Forever client, a First Aid point with the window open: one refresh and one redraw, its detail naming the toon's rank and the saved one together (`profession 129: 119 -> 120; saved First Aid > First Aid: 119 -> 120; skill 129: 119 -> 120`). Not seen: a point with the window closed, retail.
    - **A weapon skill and Defense are read as there or not, without the rank.** They go up by use, a point with every few swings, and each point was a refresh of every icon: on a Forever client a two-handed sword's skill went from 29 to 49 in eighteen minutes, 24 of that session's 40 refreshes. Which lines they are comes from Blizzard's Forever skills frame (`Blizzard_UIPanels_Game/Camelot/SkillsFrame.lua`: `WEAPON_SKILL_LINES`, `DEFENSE_SKILL_ID`), copied into `RAISED_BY_USE` because the table there is a local.
    - **Unless an item asked for a rank of it.** Whether any Forever item does is a server's rule and not known, and no export can show that none does. So the tooltip scan tells the watcher the skill of every requirement line with a rank (`UsageRequirement` of type `Skill`, "Requires First Aid (50)"; `NS.Refresh:NoteSkillRequirement`), and a line named that way is read by rank for the rest of the session. The next skill event after the first naming is one refresh (`skill 43: present -> 98`). The export lists the names under `[Transmog Collection Events]`, "Skill Ranks A Tooltip Asked For".
    - **A lock's requirement is not such a line:** "Requires Pick Lock (1)" on a lockbox is an error line (type 41; Forever, a hunter's lockbox), so it is not noted. Whether that name is a line of a rogue's skill list has not been looked at; it would matter only if that line were ever read without its rank.
  - *PVP* (`PLAYER_FLAGS_CHANGED` for the player, `PLAYER_ENTERING_WORLD`): the flags event was listened to for War Mode and came for AFK, DND and every unit in sight. The reading is the one answer the equipment code uses (`CaerdonEquipment:ReadPvpContext`: a battleground, an arena, or War Mode).
  - *Housing* (retail): no reading. One change sends several events and the catalog may not be through with it when they come, so there is one refresh a tenth of a second after the last, where there were two per event.
  - *Level* (`PLAYER_LEVEL_UP`): no reading, since every level is a change (level requirements, outgrown food and drink, the quests still to come, the auction floor). Half a second after the event, for a client that still answers with the old level while it sends it.
  - *Reputation* (`UPDATE_FACTION`, `MAJOR_FACTION_RENOWN_LEVEL_CHANGED`, `MAJOR_FACTION_UNLOCKED`, `PLAYER_ENTERING_WORLD`): the first event comes for every point gained and for a click in the reputation window. A verdict reads reputation through the tooltip, as a requirement line in red that a standing meets (WoW Forever Client, "A reputation a piece asks for": on retail the line carries `factionID` and `reactionLevel`). So what is compared is where the toon stands with every faction of the client's list (`C_Reputation.GetFactionDataByIndex`), not its points: `reaction`, for a friendship its rank's name, for a renown faction its level, told apart the way Blizzard's reputation window does it. A refresh names the faction by ID and name (`faction 576 (Timbermaw Hold): 3 -> 4`).
    - **The standings are remembered between sessions, per toon** (`CaerdonRefreshData[realm][toon].standings`, by faction ID), and the watch compares with those itself (`compare`). The client's list is not the same from one moment to the next with the standings as they were. At a login it is empty when the faction event comes while the client loads, and filled about two seconds after the world is there (Forever; at a reload it is whole from the start). And it is the reputation window's list: a faction under a collapsed header is not in it, and on retail neither is one the window's filter hides. So a faction that is not in the list keeps what was last read of it, and one that comes into the list is compared with that: the login's late list and an opened header are a change only where a standing differs, and a collapsed header is none. Only a faction never read before is new (`new, 4`): one the toon has just met, and every faction in a toon's first session with this. A memory that lasted one session could not tell the login's late list from a faction met; both are factions it has not read.
    - **No first reading.** What was judged before a session's first check was judged from the standings the last session left, unless they differ, and then that is the change: a standing reached on another machine or with the addon off is found at the first check. A toon the client has no name for yet (`UnitName` nil or `UNKNOWNOBJECT`) has nothing remembered to be compared with and nothing is saved for it: a refresh too many, not one too few. The saved table is looked up at every check and not kept, since the client's saved variables replace a table made before they loaded.
    - **What it does not see.** A standing reached below a collapsed header refreshes nothing until the header is opened, in that session or a later one; opening the headers to read them would change the player's window, so it is not done. And a verdict made in the seconds before the login's list came is not made again for it: whether a tooltip's requirement is wrong while the list lacks its faction is not known.
    - **The world's arrival is among the events** for a client that sends no faction event while it loads: a standing that is not the one remembered is then found as the world comes. Forever sends the faction event while it loads, before the world is there, at a login and at a reload. On retail the list of the one login whose times were read came 2.5 s after the world; when its faction events come has not been read off.
    - **The two renown events** are the ones Blizzard's reputation window listens for besides the first (both flavors' source; Forever has no such faction). `FACTION_STANDING_CHANGED` is in both listings with a faction and a standing as payload, and no code of Blizzard's listens for it in either flavor. On Forever it is a gain's event, not a standing's: it comes more than once for a quest turned in and never in a session without reputation gained, a loading screen included, while the watch reads no standing as changed. The watch does not use it, and the test addon counts it beside `UPDATE_FACTION` in every session's record.
    - **The watch is made only where the client has the list's two functions** (both do), so the offline scenarios, whose client has none, have no watch and save nothing.
    - Evidence: offline (`standalone/refresh-watcher.lua`, both flavors' mock, sessions that follow one another on what was saved; the code from before fails it, and faults planted one at a time each failed it). Static: the events are in both listings, and Forever's own reputation window (`Blizzard_UIPanels_Game/Camelot/ReputationFrame.lua`, 1.60.1.70938) calls every function the reading uses. Forever client (1.60.1.70959, 2026-11-17). With the watch as it was before the standings were remembered, each reading compared with the session's last, over a day of logins and reloads on two toons: a reload was five faction events while loading, the list whole at the first; a login's list came after the world (eleven factions, all the toon has), and that was a refresh and a draw of its own, which is what the memory is for; nine quests turned in for reputation brought the event some twenty-five times more and no refresh. With the standings remembered, one toon: the first session one refresh of `new` lines; the reload and the login after it no refresh for reputation, a login drawing as often as a reload; a `/reload` was enough for the client to keep the new saved variable. A faction first met there was one refresh (`faction 2740 (Kirin Tor): new, 4`). Retail client (12.1.5.70961, a druid): the first session one refresh of 86 `new` lines 2.5 s after the world and one of seven more at a quest turned in; after it a login and nineteen reloads with no refresh for reputation. Not seen in a client: a standing reached, so whether the client answers with the new one when the event comes; a header opened over a changed standing; on retail what a check costs on its longer list, a friendship, a renown level.
  - *Quests* (`QUEST_ACCEPTED`, `QUEST_REMOVED`, `QUEST_TURNED_IN`), the quest module's own watch: most quests take no item that is carried or shown, and their events change no icon. An icon follows from two things there: whether a quest it was asked about is in the log, still to come, behind the toon or none of those, and which items the log's objectives name. So the module keeps every verdict it handed out since the caches were last dropped, the ones it does not cache included, and after a run of events judges each of those quests again and reads the log's objectives again. A difference is a refresh, named by the quest whose answer turned (`quest 293: upcoming -> in the log`, `quest 292: in the log -> behind it`) or the item (`objective Boar Tusk: new, 8007`).
    - **Every answered quest is judged again, not the quest of the event.** A quest decides about others: the one before it in a chain, one it excludes, one offered only until it is done. A quest with no item of its own can take an icon away that way, and the event names only the quest it is about.
    - **The compare is with what was answered, not with the last reading.** The answers are forgotten with every full refresh and grow again as items are drawn, so two readings taken at two events hold different quests. A watch of this kind passes `compare` in place of `read` and has no first reading. Nothing drawn since the last refresh means nothing answered, and then no event refreshes.
    - **One check, when the run of events is over** (half a second after the last): the completion flags settle a moment after `QUEST_TURNED_IN`. An answer first given inside that half second is among the answered ones and is compared with the rest.
    - **`CaerdonQuestItems:InvalidateCaches` forgets the answers too,** so it goes with a redraw of every icon. The recorder empties the caches behind the icons on screen and therefore ends a run of recordings with one full refresh. A refresh about what is worn or about something learned is the exception: it leaves the module as it is (below, "A refresh can say what changed").
    - **What the refresh on every quest event also did, and no longer does:** it redrew the icons after a turn-in whatever the turn-in changed. Reputation is the one thing found that no other trigger covers: a requirement in red (`foundRedRequirements`) that a turn-in's reputation meets kept its icon until the next refresh, as it always did for reputation gained any other way. The reputation watch (above) is for both.
  - **A check as a burst starts and one when it is over** (`leading`, `delay`): the first is for a client that already has the new state when it sends the event, the second for one that has not. Which of the two a client does is not known for these events, so both are read.
  - **A watch's first reading counts as a change.** What was judged before it was judged from a state nobody read.
  - **A new watch** is for an event measured to be noise (the counters name it). An event that is always a change stays a plain handler.
  - Evidence: offline only (`standalone/refresh-watcher.lua`, with faults planted one at a time each failing it; `standalone/counters.lua` for the watcher with the real core and counters). What the old code did, from the test addon's totals: on a Forever client 247 of some 790 refreshes of the addon's own were the flags event and 176 the skill line event, over 878 minutes; on retail 70 of some 230 were the skill line event, 18 of the 23 of one login. Forever client (1.60.1.70938, a hunter, eight minutes of play, 2026-11-16): 23 skill line events were read four times and were one change, the first reading; 11 flag events came and none was the player's; the session made 14 refreshes where the code from before would have made 46. A later session there had a skill that changes: a weapon skill, each point its own event and its own refresh, 24 in 18 minutes. Not seen in that session: a flag change of the player's own, War Mode. The quest compare and the level watch: offline (`scenarios/quest-compare.lua`, which the code from before fails, with faults planted one at a time each failing it or the watcher's test). What the old code did, from the same totals: 209 of the 790 refreshes on Forever and 26 of the 230 on retail were the quest module's. The quest compare on the same Forever client (ten minutes, five quests accepted and five turned in): 15 quest events were one check per run, 10, and 2 changes, both an accept, one named by the quest and one by its objective; the quest icons were right by eye. Not seen: a turn-in that changes an answer, a level gained. The weapon skills read without their rank: offline (`standalone/refresh-watcher.lua`, and `scenarios/skill-requirement.lua` with the item code's own tooltip scan, faults planted one at a time each failing one of the two), and the same Forever client in a fight of three minutes: 22 skill line events after the login's, each read and none a change, where the session before had a refresh for every such event. Not seen: an item that asks for a rank of a weapon skill.
  - Measured over play, 2026-11-17 (refreshes of the addon's own, the test addon's forced ones left out). Forever client (1.60.1.70959, a hunter and a rogue, fourteen sessions of five minutes and more, 315 minutes): 0.5 refreshes and 0.3 redraws a minute, where the code from before made 0.9 refreshes a minute over its 878; by the sessions' own event counts it would have made about twice as many of them, most of the difference the flags event. Retail client (12.1.5.70961, a druid, one session of 75 minutes of play): 0.8 refreshes and 0.7 redraws a minute against about 2.2 refreshes in the session before the watcher; a login was 7 refreshes against 23; 159 skill line events were 4 changes, 45 quest events 33 checks and 5 changes, and War Mode switched four times was four refreshes about what is worn. **A session spent in the settings, in Pawn's options or putting gear sets on is not lower than before:** each click there is a refresh, a reload has one skill line event for the watcher to take out, and a set put on is a refresh for each slot (two sets in two minutes: 32 refreshes, ten redraws in the whole session).
- **Pawn has no event for a change of its scales, so two of its functions are hooked** (`types/EquipmentMixin.lua`, refresh `pawn`). The upgrade verdict, the arrow's colour and the level gain are kept with the status since they are made once per refresh, so a weight edited in Pawn showed only at the next refresh of any kind. Pawn throws its calculated values away with `PawnResetTooltips`, which every function that edits, adds, removes, imports or shows a scale calls, and its record of the best items with `PawnInvalidateBestItems`, which a change of spec and a reset of its upgrade tracking call (read in Pawn 2.14.3, the same file in both clients).
  - *Pawn's "Show item level upgrades" checkbox calls neither:* it sets `PawnCommon.ShowItemLevelUpgrades` and calls `PawnResetBags` (read in Pawn 2.14.4), which Pawn also calls after every change of the worn gear. So that function is hooked too and refreshes only when the value differs from the one last seen (noted by the first verdict that reads Pawn, or the first call). Evidence: a retail client where the arrow did not follow the checkbox until a reload; offline `scenarios/pawn-change.lua`. Not seen in a client since.
  - **One refresh half a moment after the first call** (0.2 s): an import or a reset calls them several times.
  - **Some of Pawn's option clicks call the reset with nothing changed for a verdict** (tooltip alignment, border colour, item IDs): each is a refresh nobody needed, made by a click in Pawn's options.
  - **Not covered:** Pawn's "Show item level upgrades" option, which the verdict reads (`PawnPrefersEquipped`) and whose click calls neither function.
  - **At login** Pawn calls the second from its initialization; whether that comes before or after this addon's file is loaded decides whether a login has one `pawn` refresh. A Forever client's login had none, and a retail client's reloads have none; the one retail login looked at had one in a session of two minutes, which the record cannot tell from a click in Pawn's options.
  - Evidence: offline only (`scenarios/pawn-change.lua`, two functions of the scenario's own standing in for Pawn's; the code from before fails it, and faults planted one at a time each failed it). That Pawn calls them is reading. Forever client (1.60.1.70938, a hunter, Pawn 2.14.3, 2026-11-16): 14 refreshes named `pawn` in a session of 54 minutes, all from clicks in Pawn's options, and none in the 38 minutes of play after them, which had a level gained and three pieces put on; no error.
