Retail client (12.1.5.70961, Pawn 2.14.4, 2026-11-18): scales hidden and shown in Pawn's options were refreshes named `pawn`, 25 over three sessions, and the arrows looked right to the author afterwards.
- **A refresh says why** (`RefreshItems(reason, detail)`): the watcher passes its watch's name and what differed (`skill 43: 97 -> 98`), a signal module a name of its own (`quests`, `quest data`, `bag slots`, `auction prices`). The counters file the refresh under it. Without a reason it is filed under the event the core's frame is handling, which is right for a plain handler.
- **A refresh can say what changed, and then keeps what does not follow from it** (`RefreshItems(reason, detail, changed)`). Every verdict is made again whatever it says: the status cache is always emptied and every window draws. What can stay is what a module holds behind its verdicts. The names are the core's (`REFRESH_ABOUT` in `CaerdonWardrobe.lua`); a name it does not know, or none, is a refresh about everything, which is what every refresh was before.
  - *`worn`* (a piece put on or taken off, an equipment set changed or put on, the refresh a second after a binding piece is put on, the PVP watch): the equipment code reads the worn gear, the sets and the PVP context again and keeps what the client said about the sources of every look (`sourceInfoCache`, `appearanceSourcesCache`). A source's answer is about the collection and about what the toon is, and a change of clothes is neither. The quest module keeps everything.
  - *`collection`* (the four transmog collection events, a mount, toy, pet or recipe learned): the equipment code keeps nothing, as before. The quest module keeps everything: its verdicts, the answers a quest event is compared with, and its reading of the log.
  - **A piece learned by being put on relies on the collection event.** Kept past an equipment change, a source's "not collected" would be stale if the client learned it and sent no `TRANSMOG_COLLECTION_*` event. On both clients the test addon's journal has `TRANSMOG_COLLECTION_SOURCE_ADDED` for pieces learned by equipping, by selling and by a quest; it records events, so it cannot show a learning that sent none. The sign would be a piece that stays a look to learn after another piece of the same look was put on, until the next refresh about everything.
  - **What a quest's answer follows from** is the log, the completion flags, the quest data, the client's word on the quest's level and on trivial, and the toon's level, race, class, faction and professions (`JudgeQuest`, `UpcomingBlocker`). A new input there that hangs on what is worn or learned takes the two names out of `CaerdonQuestItems:InvalidateCaches`.
  - **The answers then outlive the icons they were given for.** After such a refresh the quest module still holds answers about items that may not be drawn again, and a quest event judges those too. At worst that is a refresh nobody needed.
  - **A watch's first reading is about everything,** whatever the watch says its changes are about: nothing says what differs from a state nobody read.
  - **A refresh put off by a fight** keeps what the ones put off were about, when they were all about the same thing; otherwise it is about everything.
  - **A module that keeps something says so where it is told** (the comment on its `InvalidateCaches`), with what its answers follow from. The other four signal modules are not told and empty what they hold on every refresh: what they hold is a reading of the bags or the professions that costs little to take again, and nothing has measured otherwise.
  - **Measuring it:** `/cwtest timing <label> worn` and `... collection` force refreshes that say so; the same windows timed without the word are the comparison. The export's and the session record's refresh lines say how many refreshes were about one thing only.
  - Evidence: offline (`scenarios/scoped-refresh.lua`: which questions the client is asked again after each kind of refresh, with the game changed each time and the icon read; the code from before fails it, and faults planted one at a time each failed it). Forever client (1.60.1.70938, a hunter under BetterBags, 59 bag buttons of which five are pieces with a look): the icons were as before after the reload that loaded it, the upgrade arrow followed a piece put on and taken off, and a quest turned in with four looks learned sent its five collection refreshes and one of the quest watch. **What it bought there is memory, not time:** a refresh about either thing made about 125 KB less garbage than a plain one (of some 1550 KB), the quest answers that were not made again; in time the three kinds lay within the spread of two plain timings (28 and 33 ms). Keeping the sources showed nothing with five pieces to judge. Not seen: a bank or a toon with many pieces, where the sources are most of a refresh; a look learned by putting a piece on while another piece of that look is carried; retail.
- The export's `[Transmog Collection Events]` section lists the last 25 such events with the refresh generation. Use it for "the appearance was learned but the icon did not change".
- For a mount or a pet the export's `[Mount]` and `[Pet]` sections hold the journal's answer (`Is Collected`; `Num Collected` and `Limit`). A learnable icon on one the journal calls collected is the addon's doing; one the journal still calls uncollected after a reload is the client's. A pet can also be learnable through the tooltip scan (`canLearn`), which the section does not show.
- **What belongs to one copy is added after the cache lookup.** Openable, locked, readable, refundable and the combine statuses are facts about one copy of an item in its bag slot, and the status cache is per link. `GetCaerdonStatus` therefore caches the verdict as it stands before them and calls `GetCopyStatus` after the lookup, on every call, for an item in a bag slot. Cached together, a verdict first asked for outside the bags stood for the copy in the bags: a lockbox taken from the mail had no icon until the next refresh (seen on Forever), and two copies in different states shared one verdict. What an item's use spell says about combining follows from the link and is kept per link (`copySpellCache`), so a cache hit costs the slot's container and purchase info and no more. It replaces any status but one: `quest` stays on a copy that can be read, and that it can be read is handed back beside the status (`smallMark`; Quest Value).
- **What a window says about a row beyond its link is part of the cache key.** An auction row passes its item level (`extraData.overrideItemLevel`, since the row's link does not carry it), a set or dressing room entry its source (`extraData.appearanceSourceID`). Both decide the transmog info, so the key gets `|l<level>` and `|s<source>`. Without them two rows of one link shared the verdict of whichever was drawn first: offline, a row listed above the worn piece's level read `sellable` after a row of the same link below it. No client has shown the case.
- **The Debug Frame's export is of the piece as its window gave it** (`CaerdonWardrobe:GetAskedOf`, `AskedUnderPointer` in `features/DebugFrame.lua`). The frame takes its item from the tooltip's link, and a link alone is another piece where the window passed more: an auction row's export described the plain item, at another level than the row, with a verdict the row never had. So where the tooltip's owner is a button the addon decorates, or a part of one (a cell of a row; its parents are asked, four levels up), and the item it was last asked to show is the tooltip's item, the export is made from that item's link and `extraData`: the same cache entry the icon was drawn from. `[Item]` then has `Passed By The Window` (the level, beside the link's own; a key's suffix with the name the piece has with it, beside the link's name; the source), for a piece named by item key a section `[Tooltip Lines (Item Key)]` with what `C_TooltipInfo.GetItemKey` says for the key and the link that tooltip names, and, for any decorated button, `Its Button` (the window, and whether an icon, an upgrade badge, a level gain and text are on it now). `upgradeSettings` under `[Transmog (Caerdon Processed)]` names the settings that decide whether an upgrade is drawn. What is kept is taken as the item is, and used again when the frame refreshes it: the pointer has left by then. The tooltip lines in such an export are still the link's (`C_TooltipInfo.GetHyperlink`), not the window's own. Evidence: offline (`scenarios/default-refresh.lua`, `scenarios/export.lua`). Retail client (12.1.5.70961): an auction row's export named the row's level beside its link's, the window, and what was on the button, and its verdict was the row's.
- **A box's lock is read from its slot** (`IsBagSlotLocked`, `C_TooltipInfo.GetBagItem`), with the text compared on every line. The tooltip of the bare link says "Locked" for a lockbox whether or not this copy has been opened, and the line's type depends on the toon: `ErrorLine` (41) for a toon that cannot pick locks, a plain line (0) for a rogue. The general tooltip scan reads only the plain one, so it called every box openable on the first toon and locked, opened or not, on the second. Seen on Forever: locked on a hunter and a rogue, openable on the rogue once picked, the icon changing as the lock was picked. Retail not seen.
- **The combine icon waits for its spell's text.** `canCombine` and `readyToCombine` are read off the description of the item's use spell, and the client hands out an empty description for a spell whose data it has not loaded. Kept, that empty answer left the icon off until the next full refresh: after a login it was missing on whichever items' spells had not been loaded, and there after a reload (retail client, bank items, two sessions with the same code). So an empty description of a spell that is not cached (`C_Spell.IsSpellDataCached`) is not kept: the data is asked for through `CaerdonSpellEventListener`, once a session per spell, and `RefreshItems` runs when a text comes. No refresh for a spell that turns out to have no text (a recipe's use spell) or no data. A cached spell is never asked for: the client can answer such a request from inside the call, which runs Blizzard's own listeners in the addon's call. Evidence: offline, `scenarios/combine.lua`.
- **An empty `Refresh()` leaves the stale icon** while the Debug Frame shows the right status. No feature has one: a feature with no `Refresh()` of its own is asked again for what its buttons last showed (Processing Pipeline, "A window with no `Refresh()` of its own"), and `scenarios/default-refresh.lua` fails a feature file that defines an empty one.
  - **A window redraws on its own data, and a refresh is about something else.** Blizzard's mail redraws on `MAIL_INBOX_UPDATE` and `MAIL_SEND_INFO_UPDATE`, the guild bank on its slot and tab events, a roll frame when it is shown, the black market on `BLACK_MARKET_ITEM_UPDATE`, an auction list on its results and when it is shown (both flavors' source, where none of those four addons names a collection, equipment, skill line, level or quest turn-in event). A refresh comes from a look learned, a piece put on, a skill point, a quest, a level or a setting. So a window whose feature said nothing kept its icons until its own next update. That was sixteen features: these five windows with the profession, customer order and mission windows, and eight integrations.
  - **A `Refresh()` of its own is for a window that has to be walked:** BaudBag keeps the buttons it was asked to draw, with their slot, and draws them again; Inventorian draws every button its two windows hold (both seen on Forever in the rotation: no button drawn again before, every one after; bags only, the bank not looked at). The test addon's rotation fails a bag feature whose buttons a full refresh does not draw again (notes: Test Addon).
  - **LiteBag also redraws by itself on `TRANSMOG_COLLECTION_UPDATED`,** which the integration asks it to (`LiteBag_AddPluginEvent`); the default covers the refreshes that are about anything else.
  - **Sorted's sort order is not redrawn:** the status a row is sorted by (`caerdonStatus`) is written when Sorted asks for it and stays as it was after a refresh; only the icons follow.
  - Evidence: offline (`scenarios/default-refresh.lua`). Forever client (1.60.1.70938, a hunter, 2026-11-16): a session of 54 minutes with 54 refreshes, the auction house used and settings changed, the icons right by eye and no error of the addon's; no icon in the bags or the bank changed, which says the remembered requests cost the windows that walk their own buttons nothing. Which of the sixteen windows were looked at is not on record. Not seen: retail. Auctionator, Angrier World Quests and Venture Plan are installed; cargBags Nivaya, LiteBag, Sorted and World Quest Tracker are not, so for those it is the core's behaviour that is proven and nothing about the addon.

## Bag Addon Integrations

- **BetterBags** only sends `item/Updated` when a button's draw inputs change, so the integration tracks its buttons (`trackedBagItems`, weak keys, dropped on `item/Clearing`) and `Refresh()` re-runs them, skipping slots with no item. A button that is not on screen (a closed bag, a collapsed section) is not re-run: it is owed a draw for when it is (Processing Pipeline, `AskWhenOnScreen`).
- **BetterBags vendor** (`next/vendor.lua`): BetterBags hides Blizzard's `MerchantFrame`, so the `BetterBagsVendor` feature decorates BetterBags' cards. Each button's `SetBuy` / `SetBuyback` / `SetRepair` / `Release` is wrapped (pcall'd). `GetStatusRules()` answers with the Merchant feature's rules so the vendor's exceptions apply (Processing Pipeline, "A window that offers items"), and `GetDisplayRules()` with its display rules; any feature that redraws another feature's content should do the same. Buy tab confirmed in game; buyback is offline only.
- **BetterBags guild bank** (`next/guildbank.lua`, `next/guildbutton.lua`): Blizzard's `GuildBankFrame` stays hidden while it is open, so the `BetterBagsGuildBank` feature decorates BetterBags' buttons, with the GuildBank rules and display settings. BetterBags sends `guild/Updated` after every draw, for an item and for a free slot; a released button sends nothing and is hidden. The button's `tab` and `data.slotid` are the item's real tab and slot, also in the view of all tabs, so the link and the tooltip are read the way the GuildBank feature reads them. Its `Refresh()` asks for every button that is shown, not only those on screen: BetterBags lets go of every button when the visit ends (`Wipe`), so none is of a closed guild bank, and a panel that a hidden parent took off screen is not drawn again when it comes back (`panelProto:Watch`, BetterBags 0.9.5), so skipping it would leave its icons as they were. Icons confirmed in game (retail); the refresh path and a late-loading item are offline only.
- **Baganator places, this addon sizes.** Three corner widgets (`caerdon_wardrobe_status`, `..._binding`, `..._level_gain`) are the overlay's own regions, which Baganator re-parents, anchors and shows.
  - `options.hostPlacesParts = true`: the drawing code only fills and sizes the parts; it never anchors, shows or hides them.
  - `onUpdate` answers true, false, or nil for "not known yet". `BaganatorMixin:Ask` requests one draw per item and answers nil until `OnButtonDrawn` has the result (`CaerdonWardrobe:GetDrawnParts`). An item not drawn within `PENDING_LIMIT` (3 s) is answered false; a later draw calls `RequestItemButtonsRefresh`.
  - `feature:OnButtonDrawn(button, item, locationInfo, options)` is an optional hook called after every draw.
  - The upgrade arrow is not hidden for Baganator's own Pawn widget; both may show.
  - `SetItemButtonMogStatusFilter` is skipped for host-placed parts: the icon is hidden at that moment and would be left at alpha 0.
  - Confirmed in game: icon, level gain and binding text following their corners and the layout sizes. Unverified: a widget removed from every corner, the first bag open while answers are pending.

## Integration Report

An integration hooks functions of another addon, and that addon can rename or remove them in any update. The feature then draws nothing and raises no error. The report says, per integration, whether the places it attaches to were there.

- **Declared and noted.** A feature that decorates another addon's frames has `Mixin.integration = { addon, hookPoints, optionalHookPoints }` and calls `self:NoteHookPoint(label, owner, key)` on the line before it attaches, with the table and key the hook itself uses (`FeatureMixin.lua`). `CaerdonIntegrations.lua` reads the two: a point is found, missing (looked for, not a function), not reached (the code has not come to it) or undeclared.
- **Verdict per integration:** `problem` when a needed point is missing, the feature's `Init` did not finish, or a note has no declaration; `waiting` while a needed point has not been reached; `ok` otherwise. The Debug Frame export prints one line per integration under `[Integrations]`, with the other addon's version, so a user's report carries it.
- **Note where the code attaches, not where it looks things up.** A lookup that raises (`GetAddon`, `GetModule`) shows as an `Init` that did not finish. A point behind a window (the Narcissus set frame, the buttons of Baganator and of BetterBags' vendor, Inventorian's `OnEnable`) is noted when it is reached, and reads "not reached yet" until that window has been opened.
- **The note changes nothing.** It never raises, whatever the other addon hands over, and it does not replace the guard or the hook beside it: an `Init` that raised an error before still raises it. The core contains that error (Processing Pipeline): the features after it start as usual, and this one reads "its Init did not finish".
- **A message is only half proven.** For a subscription (`RegisterMessage`) the note shows that the registering function exists, not that the other addon still sends that message. A draw shows that; the test addon counts draws per feature.
- **A callback the other addon calls by name** has nothing to look for beforehand: `NoteHookCalled(label)` records the call arriving (Venture Plan's `VPEX_OnUIObjectCreated`).
- **Listed:** only integrations whose addon was loaded before this one; the others never register a feature.
- **KkthnxUI and NDui** report their hook point as missing while that UI's own bags are switched off: there is nothing to decorate.
- **Evidence:** every integration was run offline against a stub of its addon, and installs the same hooks as before the notes were added. The declared names were found in the installed source of BetterBags 0.9.3, Auctionator 347, Dialogue UI 1.0.7, Narcissus 1.8.7, Angrier World Quests and Venture Plan 8.1.6. Forever client (1.60.1.70938): the export's section and the records show every point found for Auctionator, the three BetterBags features and Dialogue UI, with draws for BetterBags' bags and vendor. Narcissus finds its set frame and slot buttons at login and reaches the set buttons only once a set is shown. Retail has not been seen, and the other integrations cannot be run here.

## Skillet

- **What it draws** (`external/Skillet/Core.Skillet.lua`): the status of what a recipe makes on each row of Skillet's recipe list, and on the icon of the selected recipe in its detail pane, by the rules of the game's own profession window (`features/TradeSkill.lua`; `otherIcon` by that window's setting).
- **Where it attaches.** `Skillet:AddPreButtonShowCallback` is Skillet's documented way in: the function is called just before a row is shown, with the row's button and the recipe's ID, and with no ID for a header's row. What it returns is taken as a button to show in the row's place, so the feature's function returns nothing. The rows are a fixed set filled again at every scroll, so a header's row and a hidden one are cleared (`AddPreButtonHideCallback`, optional). The detail pane has no callback: `Skillet.UpdateDetailWindow` is hooked (optional) and the selected recipe read off `Skillet.currentRecipeInfo`.
- **What a recipe makes** (`GetRecipeOutput`): the client's own word first (`C_TradeSkillUI.GetRecipeInfo(...).hyperlink`, as the game's window), then the item of Skillet's record of the recipe (`Skillet:GetRecipe`), which it also has for a recipe the client says nothing of.
- **An error of the feature's is caught in its own function** and handed to the error handler: the function runs inside Skillet's pass over its rows, which an error would end.
- **Placement.** A row is a line of text 16 high with an arrow or a star at its left, a level, the name, and counts at its right edge; the icon is put right behind the name's text and held before the counts where the name is long (`GetRowOptions`). Seen so in the retail client. On four rows of a glossary group ("Appendix I - Terms") the icon lay further right than the name's end; why is not known.
- **A row has no binding text** (`displayRules`): its right edge is Skillet's counts, and the text was drawn across them. The selected recipe's icon has it (`GetDisplayRules`, by `locationInfo.isSelectedRecipe`).
- **A number that goes with the icon stands right behind it** (a piece's level gain, a decor's house XP, which is drawn in the level gain's place and is not held back by the `levelGain` rule): `options.levelGainAnchor = { point, x, y }`, which the core takes for a window that fits the number by hand (`UpdateUpgradeDeltaPosition`). A hand-fitted window's number otherwise goes to the bottom right of its button, which on a row is Skillet's counts: the house XP of a decor's recipe was drawn across them. Seen in the retail client (12.1.5.70961, Skillet 5.68); `scenarios/draw-path.lua` is as before, since no other window names the option.
- **Only an ID the client calls an item is asked for.** Where the client knows the recipe and names nothing made, nothing is shown; Skillet's record is for a recipe the client has no word of, and it holds the recipe's own ID in the item's place for a recipe that makes no item, which was asked of the client at every row shown and failed each time.
- **Evidence.** Skillet's own source (b-morgan/Skillet, 2026-11-23; its TOC names interface 16001 and retail's): the callbacks, their arguments and the fields read are there. Offline (`scenarios/skillet.lua`, both flavors, against a stand-in in those shapes; seven faults planted one at a time each fail it). Retail client (12.1.5.70961, Skillet 5.68, an inscription list): the icons are on the rows and on the selected recipe, the export has the hook point found; that sight is what showed the two texts across the counts and the failed loads, and a later one had none of them (no failed load in the export, no text on the counts). Forever client (1.60.1.71037, Skillet 5.68): the rows have their icons, and the test addon's record has the hook point found and the rows' draws in both sessions it was loaded in.
- **Before this** the file registered a function with no body, so nothing was drawn in Skillet's window.

## World Quest Tab

- **What it draws** (`external/WorldQuestTab/Core.WorldQuestTab.lua`, retail: the addon's TOC names no other client): the status of a world quest's item rewards on the reward icons of its list, and of a quest's best reward on its map pin. The quest icon by its setting, no level gain (a reward icon is 32 wide with the addon's own amount, a piece's item level, at its foot).
- **Where it attaches** (World Quest Tab 12.1.05). Its list is a scroll box: a row is taken when the scroll box has set it up (`ScrollUtil.AddInitializedFrameCallback` on `WQT_ListContainer:GetQuestScrollBox()`), with the row's quest in the element's data. A pin set up comes through its callback registry (`WQT_CallbackRegistry`, `"WQT.MapPinProvider.PinInitialized"`). The mixins of its rows and pins are private to it; the frames are read (`row:GetRewardsFrame().rewardFrames`, `pin:GetButton()`). The callbacks an earlier version had on its frame (`ListButtonUpdate`, `MapPinPlaced`) are gone, which is why the integration had been set aside.
- **A reward's link is found by its item:** World Quest Tab sorts a quest's rewards, so its index is not the quest's own, and the link (which carries the piece's level and look) is the quest's (`GetQuestLogItemLink`) for the reward with that item. Only its item kinds of reward are looked up.
- **A row or a pin that shows another quest by the time its item has loaded is not drawn** (`IsSameItem`, by the owner kept in `locationInfo`).
- **Its tooltip is its own** (`WQT_GameTooltip`, with the reward's item tooltip set into it as `WQT_GameTooltipTooltip`), and Pawn does not fill it: the upgrade lines, which go on with Pawn's own, never did (241 items shown there, Pawn came 0 times; the export's `[Item Tooltips Seen]`, which keeps this per tooltip frame). An item's tooltip set into another (the frame that holds it names it as its `Tooltip`) that has been shown an item with Pawn not coming gets the lines from the addon itself from then on (`OweUpgradeLines`), while the client still fills it. Retail client (12.1.5.70961): the lines are on the reward's tooltip there.
- **Its comparison tooltips are its own too** (`WQT_ShoppingTooltip1` and `2`, named by its tooltips as their `shoppingTooltips`). The line of a comparison tooltip went on the client's four alone, which are noted at load. The comparison tooltips of any tooltip are the ones it names in that field (the client's comparison manager fills those and makes the tooltip their owner), so they are noted from the first item the tooltip is shown (`NoteNamedComparisonTooltips`), which is before the client builds them for it. Offline (`scenarios/pawn-scales.lua`), and seen in the retail client (12.1.5.70961, World Quest Tab 12.1.05).
- **Evidence.** The addon's installed source. Offline (`scenarios/world-quest-tab.lua`, against a stand-in in those shapes; faults planted one at a time fail it). Retail client (12.1.5.70961, World Quest Tab 12.1.05): the icons are on its list, the export has its hook point found and no failed load.

## Dialogue UI

`external/DialogueUI/Core.DialogueUI.lua`. Dialogue UI shows a quest at an NPC in its own window (`DUIQuestFrame`) in place of Blizzard's quest frame; the quest log and the map's quest details stay with the QuestLog feature.

- **Hooks:** `hooksecurefunc` on the global `DUIDialogItemButtonMixin`, for `SetItem` (every item draw) and `OnRelease` (back to the pool). The button template copies the mixin when a button is created, and the first button is created for the first quest shown, so the table hook reaches all of them. That needs Dialogue UI loaded first: it is in `OptionalDeps`.
- **What a button shows** is in Dialogue UI's own fields: `objectType` ("item", "currency", "spell", ...), `type` ("reward", "choice", "required") and `index`. The link is `GetQuestItemLink(type, index)`, the tooltip `C_TooltipInfo.GetQuestItem`.
- **Drawn:** reward and choice items. Not drawn: required items (handed in, not received) and currencies (only retail renown currencies have a status, and that path is unverified).
- **Tracking (`trackedButtons`):** what was last sent to the core per button: the link, plus whether Dialogue UI's own upgrade arrow was on the button. Dialogue UI sets a button again every 0.05 s while it waits for the item's name, so an unchanged draw is skipped; `Refresh()` forgets the links and re-runs every tracked button. A released button also loses its button state (`ClearButtonState`), so a request still on its way for it is dropped instead of drawn on a pooled button.
- **Display rules** are the QuestLog feature's (`GetDisplayRules` answers with its declaration), so "Quest rewards" on the Icons page covers both.
- **Placement (author decision, fixed layout):** Dialogue UI has a marker of its own for three corners of the icon: its upgrade arrow at the bottom left, the highest sell price coin at the top left (its option, off by default), and a red "!" at the top right for an item the toon cannot use (part of its `ItemOverlay` texture, no option, about 15 px, centered 6.8 / 7 px in from the top right corner of `AreaDefinitionItemBorder`). The overlay sits on that 36 px frame and the icon goes exactly over the "!" (`overrideStatusPosition` TOPRIGHT, `ALERT_INSET_X` / `_Y`), for every status: an icon only shows on an unusable item when there is still something to learn, which says more than "cannot use", and two markers side by side read as a disagreement. Size is `GetSlotStatusSize`; the ring of an important icon covers the "!", a minor icon (0.8 of it) may leave a rim. Text is fixed at the bottom, the level gain at the bottom right. The player's layout does not apply (`GetLayoutNote` says so in the layout editor).
- **One upgrade arrow (author decision):** where Dialogue UI has put its arrow on a button (`button:DoesButtonHaveMarker("upgrade")`; its "Evaluate Item Rewards" option, Pawn's verdict where Pawn is installed), `GetDisplayInfo` switches `upgradeIcon` and `levelGain` off, so this addon draws no arrow, no badge and no level gain there. Any other icon stays. The arrow can arrive 0.33 s after the item, so `ShowUpgradeIcon` is hooked too and draws the button again. An upgrade only this addon sees keeps its arrow and gain. `displayInfo.levelGain` exists for this; no other feature sets it.
- **Light background:** parchment shows beside the icon, so the buttons pass `roundStatusIcon` (see Icon Size and Placement).
- **Planned sell icon:** a sell icon on the best paying choice would mean what Dialogue UI's coin means, so it has to yield to the coin the way the arrow does. The coin sets no marker on the button; its frame would have to be found among the button's children.
- **Setting (author decision):** `Icon.ShowOnDialogueUI`, "Show icons on its quest rewards" in a Dialogue UI section of the Display page, only offered while Dialogue UI is loaded. Off clears every reward button and draws nothing there; the buttons stay tracked (`SWITCHED_OFF`), so `Refresh()` draws them again when it is switched back on. It is a whole-window switch because the window is another addon's, with markers of its own; which icons show while it is on still follows "Quest rewards" on the Icons page. Checked in game.
- **Debug keybind:** the button carries `caerdonDebugItemLink`; Dialogue UI's own tooltip is not one `GetHoveredItemContext` knows.
- **Tooltip line:** only with Dialogue UI's "Use Blizzard Tooltip" on; its own tooltip gets none.
- **Not covered:** the reward popup after a turn-in (`QuestFlyout`) and the looted quest item popup.
- **In game:** confirmed for reward choices: the icons, the circle clip and the upgrade arrow standing down. Not seen yet: the fixed layout over the "!", the level gain standing down, text on a reward, a minor icon over the "!".

## Icon Size and Placement

- **Reference look (author decision):** the default Blizzard slot. Important icon 20/37 of the short side of the frame it sits on, minor icon 0.8 of that, center a fifth of the icon's size in from the corner. `GetSlotStatusSize(width, height, noMaximum)` returns it, clamped to 13..24 px. Bag addons whose slot size the player sets pass `noMaximum`.
- **Windows opt in** by passing `statusProminentSize`; the core does not apply the rule by itself. Moved: BetterBags, quest rewards, Dialogue UI's reward buttons, Auctionator, dressing room outfit rows and set buttons, the item conversion output slot, the dungeon journal's search preview. Already at the value: the auction sell slot, crafting order browse rows. Deliberately not moved: the round output button of the profession window and of the crafting order form (fixed 20 px), Narcissus' round dressing room slots, world quest pins and the dungeon journal's lists and suggested reward (below).
- **Not measured, so still on their old values:** loot rolls, and what needs retail content: the dungeon journal's item sets (the tab was disabled in 12.1.5), mission rewards (covenant table, landing page report), Great Vault, Black Market (rows, hot deal). They follow the player's layout; the mission inset was made for the top left corner. Blizzard's guild bank is a 37 px slot with no options of its own, so it is the reference look; only its room has not been measured (BetterBags hides that window).
- **Measured (retail 12.1.5, default icon layout):**
  - World quest pin: a 20 px button whose art (`NormalTexture`) is a 32 px circle around it. The 15 px icon, 10 px in, lies inside the circle. The slot look would put the icon's center outside it, so the inset stays.
  - Dungeon journal loot row: a 42 px icon 2 px in from the list's clipping edge. The 8 / 7 inset lets a 20 px icon hang out by exactly those 2 px, and on a right-hand anchor keeps it off the item's name; the slot look would hang 6.8 px. The rows pass `keepInsideWhenClipped` for an icon the player has made bigger: at 100% the icon stays where it was (seen in game), the move inside has not been seen.
  - Dungeon journal search result row: a 36 x 35 px icon with 11 px of room, so the slot look would fit. It shares the loot rows' inset so both lists of the journal look alike (open: author's call).
  - Dungeon journal search preview: a 19 x 18 px icon, nothing clipping it. It had 13 px at 2 / 2 and now gets the slot look, which is the same size 0.6 px further in.
  - Dungeon journal suggested reward: a 30 px icon in a ring (`adventureguide-rewardring`), no mask reported, taken as round. The 8 / 7 inset keeps the 20 px icon's center on it.
  - Dressing room set button: a 24 px icon in a 32 px row with 16 px of room. It had 13 px at 1 / 1 and now gets the slot look, the same size 1.6 px further in.
  - Crafting order form output: 47 px with a circle mask, the profession window's output button. Left on the 20 px default like that one.
  - Item conversion output: a 48 px square slot, unmasked, nothing clipping it. It had the 20 px default and now gets 24.
  - Crafting order browse row: 20 px tall, 13 px icon anchored LEFT hanging 3.5 px into 5 px of room.
- **Measured (Forever, button units, important / minor):** Blizzard bags, bank, vendor, mail and loot are 37 px slots with a 20 / 16 px icon that hangs 6 / 4.8 px outside the corner. Auction and profession rows are 20 px tall with a 13 px icon and no icon region. Every slot window had at least 10 px of room around it; only auction rows have none.
- **Clip-aware placement (`options.keepInsideWhenClipped`; BetterBags, Auctionator, dungeon journal lists):** `RunClipPass` (0.1 s after the draw) finds the nearest clipping parent and, when its room on the anchor's side is less than the overhang, re-places every icon in that frame inside the corner at `CLIP_INSIDE_SCALE` (0.8). The answer is per clipping frame and looked up on every draw, because pooled buttons move between grids. Only horizontal room decides. The full size is kept on the icon (`caerdonBaseSize`) so it never shrinks twice. Confirmed in game for BetterBags' two designs.
- **Button geometry recorder (`CaerdonButtonGeometry.lua`):** with **Enable debug** on, `ProcessItem` notes every button it draws and one per window and option set is measured 0.2 s later (up to 6 shapes per window, session-wide; a button whose width follows its text makes a shape per width). A button with no layout is looked at again three times, a second apart, and the export then says why a shape was never measured. The export's `[Button Geometry]` section prints the player's layout (and whether it is the default: it is part of every number in a window that is not hand-fitted), then per shape options, size, what the overlay sits on, masks, the icon's size and overhang, text, stack count reach, and the nearest clipping parent with its room. `/cdebug geometry` shows only that section.
- **Ring icons on a light background (`options.roundStatusIcon`; Dialogue UI):** the store's category icons (`SetRingIcon`: star, "?", bag, key, ...) are a ring in the middle half of a 64 px texture with a dark shadow around it. The crop to the ring's square keeps the shadow in the corners: lost on a dark slot, a square that turns with the animation on parchment. The option clips the icon to the ring (`TempPortraitAlphaMask` on `mogStatus`, `RING_ICON_CLIP` = 0.9 of its size; the ring measures 0.88, and `SetStatusIconPosition` sizes the mask). No rim of shadow may be left: it is a drop shadow, heavier below, so a rim swings around as the icon turns. Opt-in because the mask's fit is only seen in game and Baganator re-parents the icon. Blizzard's quest frame is parchment too and has not been looked at.
- **Rectangles are not shapes.** The round profession button read as a 47 px slot. A mask does not mean round either: the two masks of `ItemButtonTemplate.xml` are named in the export (`TempPortraitAlphaMask` is the circle, `common-iconmask` every standard slot).
- The copy dialog is a child of the Debug Frame, so anything that shows it opens the Debug Frame first. Its edit box must not use `SetSpacing`; that broke the selection highlight.

## Settings

Two sub-pages under "Caerdon Wardrobe": **Icons** (`config/Icons.lua`) and **Display** (`config/Display.lua`), built with the helpers in `config/ConfigPanel.lua`.

- **Icons page:** one block per icon: the real texture, what it means, where it shows, then its rules. A line the addon adds to a tooltip explains an icon, so its switch is in that icon's block (the upgrade lines under Upgrade, the toon's name under For another toon to use): someone who sees the line and wants it gone looks beside the icon's own switches. Seen: with the switch on Display, the upgrade arrow and the level gain were switched off in search of it. A place one icon shows in has its switch in that icon's block too (the auction house's upgrade column under Upgrade), and a place has no switch of its own beside its icons' (the auction house's icon column). Everything not about an icon (corner, animation, text, vendor gray-out, debug) is on Display.
- **Learnable by this toon (`ownIcon`):** toggles only for bags and bank, guild bank, vendor and auction house (`Icon.ShowLearnable.*`). Every other window always shows it.
- **The grey gear is the star's stand-in** (`lowSkill`, `lowSkillPlus`; author decision): something this toon can learn once it has the level or the skill it asks for (a recipe, an ensemble, a bag for a bag slot), the green one where the star would be green. So it is drawn where the star is and nowhere else (`displayInfo.ownIcon`, in `SetItemButtonStatus`), and has a block on the Icons page that says what it is and no switch. It was drawn in every window whatever was switched on. An upgrade to come has the same mark in the arrow's place and goes by the arrow's settings (Upgrades). Evidence: offline (`scenarios/draw-path.lua`, and the drawing compared call by call with the code from before: it differs where a window shows no learnable icon, in the gear's two statuses, and in when the badge's two textures are first made). Forever client (1.60.1.70959): looked at after the change and found working, with no export.
- **Learning for another toon (`otherIcon`):** one key per window in `Icon.ShowLearnableByOther`, read with `~= false`. World map, missions, Great Vault and item conversion have no option. Windows a client does not have are left out (`IsWindowAddOnAvailable`, with `Blizzard_GuildBankUI` as a canary).
- **Quest icon:** `Icon.ShowQuestItems` is one switch for every window.
- **Helpers:** `AddTitle`, `AddIconHeader`, `AddSection`, `AddLabel`, `AddCheckbox`, `AddCheckboxGrid`, `AddAllNoneButtons`, `AddDropdown`, `AddDropdownList`, `AddButton`, `FinishLayout`. Widths are fixed pixels (the canvas is about 640 wide). An option is `{ key, text, tooltip, configSection, configSubsection, configValue }`, with `perCharacter` and `default` where it is the toon's own; `key` becomes the global `CaerdonWardrobe<key>` and must be unique across both pages. `dependsOn` grays a control out.
- **Values** are resolved from `CaerdonWardrobeConfig` on every read and write: the table is replaced on a version bump, a missing section is created on write, nil reads as the default. Pages set `frame.OnRefresh`, which the settings window calls on every display; a page's first display is not an `OnShow`.
- **A list that changes** (`AddDropdownList`: a row per gear set of the toon). A page is laid out once, top down, at fixed places, and such a list is not known until the page shows and changes while the game runs. So the list is a frame whose height is set at every refresh, and everything added after it is laid out on a frame that hangs from its lower edge (`self.flow`; `nextPoint` then counts from that frame's top, `heightAbove` keeps what was above). `UpdateHeight` sums it for the scroll child at every refresh. The list is never without a height (1 where it has nothing to show): a frame with none has no place, and what hangs below it would go with it. A page gives the list four functions (rows, the line above them, a row's choices, a click) and writes the click with `SetOption`. A choice can be greyed (`disabled`): it has no click and keeps its tooltip, which is where it says why (`tooltipWhileDisabled`, in both clients' `UIDropDownMenu`). A row can have a switch at its head (`spec.check`: `get` answers nil for a row whose switch is not to be clicked) and a line of text in the dropdown's place (`note`). `dependsOn` is for the switches alone. A choice can change the rows themselves, so the page's height is summed again after one. A second, labelled switch can stand at a row's end (`spec.mark`), which hangs on nothing and is hidden for a row that has none.
- **The toon's own settings** (`CaerdonWardrobeCharacterConfig`, `## SavedVariablesPerCharacter`): for a setting that describes the toon and not the player's taste. Today four, all of the one list under "Also for gear I am not wearing": `Icon.OtherSpecs` and `Icon.OtherSets`, which of the toon's specs and gear sets that option is for, `Icon.GearSetScales`, the Pawn scale each of its gear sets is scored on (set ID to scale name; Upgrades), and `Icon.PvpSets`, the sets scored at PVP levels.
  - *What belongs there:* a fact about the character that would be wrong on another one (the specs it plays). A matter of taste (where an icon shows, what counts as an upgrade) stays in `CaerdonWardrobeConfig`, so that a player sets it once. No profiles: nothing has asked for them.
  - *On a page:* `perCharacter = true` on the option, and a label that says the choice is kept per character and names the toon. `GetValue` / `SetValue` in `config/ConfigPanel.lua` pick the table by that flag.
  - *No defaults, no version, no migrations:* the table is made by the first choice on a page, a missing table or key reads as the option's `default` (a function where the default follows the game, asked each time the page shows), and a reader checks the type at each level (`IsOtherSpecWanted`), since saved data can be anything.
  - *Read the global at each use,* as with `CaerdonWardrobeConfig`: the client puts the saved table in place of whatever was there before it loaded.
  - *Tools:* a recording carries it (`characterConfig`, only where the toon has one) and a replay sets it; a snapshot records it and lists what differs with the prefix `this character:`.
  - *Carried over once:* `Icon.OtherSpecs` was kept by class in `CaerdonWardrobeConfig` before, in no release. `NS:LoadCharacterConfig` gives the first toon of a class to log in what its class had and removes it there.
  - *Seen on retail (12.1.5, one toon):* the table is written to the toon's own saved variables file and comes back after a login and a reload; the carry-over. *Unverified:* a second toon beside it, and whether a `/reload` alone is enough for the new TOC line.
- **A change on a page refreshes the icons at once** (`Changed` in `config/ConfigPanel.lua`, once per click, reason `settings`), so the windows open behind the settings show it. The settings window's own commit, which the main page still answers with a refresh, comes only as the window closes. Offline, `scenarios/character-settings.lua` builds the Icons page against the harness's frames and clicks its checkboxes (which table a click writes to, the one refresh, `dependsOn`); the Display page, the dropdowns and anything about looks are not in the harness.
- **Main page** (`config/Config.lua`, `Config.xml`): a short description and the support box, no options. The box sizes itself from its text (`FitCard`), because the sentence wraps with the page's width.
- **`/caerdon`** (`NS:OpenSettings`) opens the main page, as does the dialog's button (author decision). It does nothing in combat.
- **Category ID:** the main category keeps the numeric ID it was created with and is kept in `NS.SettingsCategory`, which the sub-pages register under. Do not set `category.ID` to the addon's name (the old way to make `Settings.GetCategory(name)` work): `Settings.OpenToCategory` hands the ID to `C_SettingsUtil.OpenSettingsPanel`, which raises an error for anything but a number (seen on Forever).
- **Dialog text:** a slash command at the end of a line is wrapped after the slash (seen on Forever), so the footer starts with it.
- **One-time message dialog** (`MESSAGES` in `config/Config.lua`): a `StaticPopup` with **Open Settings** (`NS:OpenSettings`) and **Close**. `CaerdonWardrobeConfig.MessageSeen` is the number of the last entry shown.
  - Settings saved before the field existed read as 0 and get the latest entry. A fresh install starts at the latest number and gets the welcome text; settings reset by a `Version` bump get the reset text.
  - Only the latest entry is shown, once per account. It comes up a few seconds after login, waits for the end of combat (the button cannot open the settings during it), and counts as seen once it is on screen. Add an entry at the end and never renumber.
  - A dialog interrupts, so add an entry only when players will see different icons or need to revisit their settings (author decision): a sentence or two, not release notes.
- **In game:** the pages were built against mocked frames; real text widths and wrapping are unverified. The main page's box and the dialog (its look, its timing after login) are offline only.

### Layout settings

Where the icon, the binding text, a gear set's name and the level gain sit, and how big they are.

- **Keys:** `Icon.Position / Scale / OffsetX / OffsetY`; `Binding.*`; `Binding.GearSet.*`; `Icon.UpgradeDelta.*`. Positions are the nine anchor names, scales are percentages, offsets a nudge right / up in pixels.
- **Relative to the window's own values:** size is a factor on `statusProminentSize` / `bindingScale`, the nudge is added to the window's inset. `GetStatusBaseSize(options)` is the one place the icon size comes from.
- **Hand-fitted windows** (`overrideStatusPosition` / `overrideBindingPosition`, or `fixedLayout = true`) get no size factor and no nudge. `fixedLayout` rows still follow the player's anchor.
- **Inset** only applies along an axis where the anchor is on an edge.
- **Scaled text:** `SetPoint` offsets of a region with `SetScale` are in its own units, so default spacing is divided by the player's scale.
- **Text and the level gain on one row (author decision):** a part goes where the player puts it and the gain never moves. Text that would run into the gain moves away by the gain's height, as it does for a stack count (`bindingTextMovedFor` says which). The gain's inset is `LEVEL_GAIN_INSET` (4 px).
- **Samples measure text at normal scale** (`options.sample`), because a string's width in its own units depends on the scale it is drawn at.
- **Defaults reproduce the previous look.** The binding text's default position stays Bottom (author decision).

## Layout Editor

`config/LayoutEditor.lua` (`CaerdonWardrobeLayoutEditor`, `/caerdonlayout`), opened from the Display page.

- **The examples are real** `ItemButton`s decorated by the drawing code with a made-up status (item: Hearthstone 6948), so they cannot drift from it. The large slot is a 37 px slot in a frame with `SetScale(4)`.
- **The editor mirrors the settings (author decision):** a part switched off everywhere is not drawn and has no button; an example only about such a part is left out (`EnabledParts()`, `TEMPLATES[...].needs`). Parts are switched on and off on the Icons page, never in the editor.
- **A gear set's name is a part of its own** with a full layout, independent of the binding text's.
- **Controls:** pick a part, nine anchor squares, a size stepper (5% steps, 50 to 300), a one-pixel nudge cross (up to 40), "Reset this part", "Reset everything", "Undo changes" (back to the snapshot taken on open).
- **Changes are written live.** Done keeps. Any other close with changes shows `CAERDON_LAYOUT_KEEP_CHANGES` (Keep, Discard, Keep editing); only a click on Discard restores the snapshot.
- **Samples cut to "B...":** a slot drawn while its page is hidden has no width yet, so `RedrawSamples` queues one more redraw 0.05 s later.
- **Addons that place things themselves:** a feature may define `GetLayoutNote()` returning a sentence that starts with the addon's name and a colon, plus an optional button. They show as a callout at the top of the editor; a note leads with what does not work there. Baganator's button opens its settings on the Icons tab (`OpenBaganatorIconSettings`: its `ShowCustomise` event toggles the window and always selects Theme, so the tab is set on the dialog itself).
- **Not built:** samples on the player's real windows; a stand-in for the player's own bag addon's slot.
- **In game:** the callout and Baganator's button are confirmed; the window's overall layout was built against mocked frames.

## Processing Pipeline

- **One failure stays alone.** The core runs each feature's `Init`, `Refresh` and event handlers, and the draw of each queued item, through `Contained` (`xpcall` with the game's error handler): the error is reported like any other and the loop goes on. Without it an error in one `Init` kept every feature after it from starting, one in a `Refresh` or a handler cut off the features after it, and one in a draw ended the queue's coroutine, so the rest of its batch was never drawn. A feature whose `Init` raised gets no events (`initFinished` is false, which the integration report shows); its `Refresh` is still called, contained. A button whose draw raised is cleared: no icon rather than a stale one. The counters are told (`Counters:Unwound`), because the failed call's wrappers never returned. New code that calls into features or draws items from a loop goes through `Contained` too. Evidence: offline only (`standalone/counters.lua`; each protection taken out in turn fails it). No client has been made to raise such an error.
- **A window whose addon the client loads on demand is waited for with `self:WhenAddOnLoaded(addon, method)`** from the feature's `Init` (`FeatureMixin.lua`, on `EventUtil.ContinueOnAddOnLoaded`, which both clients have). The method runs once: at once where the addon is loaded already, else at its `ADDON_LOADED`, and through `Contained` like the feature's other handlers. An `ADDON_LOADED` handler that compares names is not enough: the client loads addons in the order of their names and any of them may ask for a window's addon, so the event can be past before this addon's features begin, and the window then has no icons for the session. Offline: `scenarios/addon-loaded.lua` (the harness stands in for `EventUtil`).
- **A window that offers items declares what it does with a look** (`statusRules` on the feature's mixin; `CaerdonWardrobeFeatureMixin:ApplyStatusRules` in `FeatureMixin.lua`, which `ProcessItem` calls at each draw). The verdict is one per item link for every window, and three windows show some looks otherwise: the journal and crafting orders mark a look this toon needs, on a piece the loot spec chosen does not bring, as another spec's (`otherSpec`); all three mark a look only another toon could learn as not to be had where this toon cannot collect the source (`noLoot`), and the journal also where the drop binds when picked up (`noLootBindOnPickup`), while a vendor's or an order's such piece stays learnable for the collection. An item a quest takes and one that starts a quest are left as they are. The core knew these windows by name before; a new window with such a rule declares it, and one with a rule of another kind adds a field there, never a name check in the core. A feature that draws another feature's content in its own frames answers with that feature's rules (`GetStatusRules`, as BetterBags' vendor does with the Merchant's), so the two cannot drift apart. The answer is kept nowhere: it is per window, and the status cache is per link. Offline: `scenarios/status-rules.lua` (the four shipped declarations case by case, and every combination of what the rules read against the rules as the core had them by name).
- **A window declares what it shows of a draw** (`displayRules` on the feature's mixin, laid over the core's own answers by `GetDisplayInfoInternal` in `FeatureMixin.lua`). A draw's rules are the binding text, the star (`ownIcon`, which the grey gear follows), the icons for another toon, the quest icon, the older expansion's mark and the sell icon, and beside them the upgrade arrow, the auction icon, the level gain and the status icon. A rule's value is `true`, `false`, or the window's key among that rule's own settings, read at each draw: `otherIcon = "Mail"` is `Icon.ShowLearnableByOther.Mail`. Four rules have settings by window (the binding text, the star, the icons for another toon, the sell icon); a key named for any other raises.
  - **What a window does not name is as the core has it:** the quest icon by its setting, the older expansion's mark shown, and the rest shown, but for an item in the bags or the bank by the settings for those. So a window that shows what the toon holds declares nothing, and a window that offers items names what it shows whatever the bags' settings say.
  - **A window whose rules depend on the place or the item has a declaration for each case and chooses among them** (`GetDisplayRules`, which is handed the draw). The vendor: its own settings, and nothing to learn on an item of which a copy is already carried. The bag addons with more than the toon's open bags (ArkInventory, Bagnon, Bagnonium): a view kept of bags that are not open goes by the settings for the bags, a guild bank by the guild bank's, and the toon's own bags are the core's.
  - **A function is for what a declaration cannot say** (`GetDisplayInfo`, whose answer is laid over the declaration): the auction house (its own switches, the two parts of a row, a status that follows the star) and Dialogue UI (its own arrow). The Debug Frame answers for itself alone (`GetDisplayInfoInternal`).
  - **A feature that draws another feature's content answers with that feature's rules,** handing the draw on to its `GetDisplayRules`: BetterBags' vendor and guild bank, Dialogue UI with the quest rewards'. Neither the vendor nor the guild bank has a `GetDisplayInfo`; one that gained it would need it passed on in BetterBags' file too, as Dialogue UI passes the quest rewards' on beside its own.
  - **A key the saved settings lack reads as off.** Every key of `ShowLearnableByOther` is filled when the settings load, so none is missing there; a new key among the other three needs its nil-migration, as any setting does.
  - **One spelling shows what differs between windows.** Each window had a function that built its answers at every draw, and the same answer was written many ways.
  - **The quest icon follows its setting in every window that shows it (author decision).** Six integrations had it on whatever "quest items" said: the world quest lists of Venture Plan, World Quests List, World Quest Tracker, Zygor and Angrier World Quests, and Sorted's bags. No reason for it was in the code, and the world map and the mission table went by the setting. No window declares `questIcon = true`; three never show the icon (crafting orders, the item conversion slot, the profession window). Offline only: none of the six addons is installed in a client here.
  - Evidence, offline: the answer of every registered window, from the code before and with the declarations, compared over each setting alone, the settings together in groups, an item in the bags and elsewhere, each binding text and each kind of place, in both flavors: the same throughout (before the quest icon's change above, which then showed in those six windows alone, with quest items switched off). `scenarios/display-rules.lua` keeps that comparison for the windows that declare, against their functions' answers written out, and checks each shipped declaration against the default settings (a rule a draw reads, a key the settings have); faults planted one at a time in the core, the declarations and the features that pass rules on each failed it. The draw transcript is unchanged. WoW Forever has run it: the mail, quest rewards and the profession window as before, no icon changed in the bags, no error. Not looked at there: the loot window, the guild bank. Retail has not run it. The vendor's and the three bag addons' declarations came after that look and rest on the same comparison, offline; none of the three bag addons is installed in a client here.
- **A window hands a button over with `Decorate`** (`CaerdonWardrobe:Decorate(feature, button, source, locationInfo, options)`, `self:Decorate(...)` on a feature): `source` is an item link, an item ID, an item the window made, or nothing, which clears the button; the item is handed back. The item's data is waited for there before `UpdateButton`, as the rule asks (an item handed to the queue without it can circle in the queue's container and never be drawn, which the vendor window met with old recipes). A window that waited itself and then asked had no guard, so a row drew its old item over what it had come to show: a vendor's page turned while its items loaded, or turned and turned back.
  - **The wait itself is `CaerdonWardrobe:WhenItemLoaded(button, item, callback)`**, which a window calls where the answer does more than ask for one button: a row of the auction house's results is two of the core's buttons, the dressing room and Narcissus tell the Debug Frame of the row with the ask, a listing's row is the feature's own text. The wait belongs to `button` as it was at the call: every `UpdateButton` and `ClearButton` a window makes, and every new wait, gives the button a new serial, and an answer that comes late runs only if the serial is still its own. No feature waits for a button with `ContinueOnItemLoad` itself.
  - **The core's own word does not end a wait.** A refresh that asks again for what a button had (`AskAgain`), a retry of a verdict that was not ready, the queue clearing a request it drops or a draw that failed: none of them says the row shows anything else, and the item that loads is still the row's. They ask and clear through `AskAsCore` and `ClearAsCore`, which put the serial back. A new place in the core that asks or clears on its own account does the same.
  - **Every answer of one wait is its own.** `ContinueOnItemLoad` answers for a piece of equipment at once and again when the other items of its look have loaded (LoadSources), and a window has always asked again with each answer. So what the callback itself says about the button does not end the wait; what the window says does. Whether the later ask still changes an icon, now that a verdict is kept by its link, is not known: nothing measures it.
  - **A guard of the window's own stays where it says more than the serial can:** another addon's fields (BetterBags' card, Narcissus' button), a slot whose content changes with no draw (the guild bank), a page of listings (a commodity's page has a button of its own, so the other view's button hears nothing of it), a list that can give a row another item with no word to the feature (the dressing room's sets). Dialogue UI's said no more than the serial and is gone.
  - **A row of the auction house's results waits twice:** for the client's word on its key (`ITEM_KEY_ITEM_INFO_RECEIVED`), then for its item. The key's answer draws only on a row that still shows that key (`RowShowsKey`): the list gives a row to another result as it scrolls.
  - A window that asks with no wait at all (the bags, most bag addons, the lists) is as it was: the queue waits for it and drops a request whose button was since cleared or asked for another place.
  - Offline: `scenarios/decorate.lua`, with faults planted one at a time in the core and the auction file. In a client: the vendor and the auction house on WoW Forever; Dialogue UI, BetterBags' vendor and guild bank, the dressing room, Narcissus, the journal's sets and the conversion slot in none.
- **The auction house's sell slots are cleared where the client empties them.** An item put into a sell slot comes through `AuctionHouseFrame:SetPostItem`, which the feature hooks. Taking it out again does not: a right click on the slot, a drag off it and a posted auction all end in the slot's own display being emptied (`AuctionHouseItemDisplayMixin:SetItemLocation(nil)`, then `SetItemInternal(nil)`; the same in both flavors' source), so the icon of the item that had been there stayed on the empty slot. The feature hooks `SetItemInternal` of both sell frames' displays and clears the slot's button when it is given nothing (`OnSetSellItemDisplay`), unless the display refuses the change as the client's own function does. Seen on WoW Forever before the mend (the icon stayed). Offline: `scenarios/decorate.lua`, against a stand-in written from the client's functions. No client has run the mend.
- **The loot window's rows are asked for when they are built and when the client changes their slot** (`features/Loot.lua`, `AskForRow`). The window builds a row per slot as it opens, and the list tells the feature of each (`ScrollUtil.AddInitializedFrameCallback`); on `LOOT_SLOT_CHANGED` it fills that slot's row in again itself (`frame:Init()` in both flavors' `LootFrame.lua`), without building it, so the list's callback does not come a second time. A slot with no link when its row is built (`GetLootSlotLink` gives none) is cleared, and with the list alone listened to nothing asked for it again while the window stayed open. The feature is the callback's owner at the list: the list keeps one callback per owner, and one registered under `LootFrame` itself is replaced by any addon that names it too. The export's `[Loot Window]` section counts the rows built with and without a link and the slots the client changed, and lists each slot of an open window with what is on its button (a row's tooltip belongs to the row, so `Its Button` has nothing for one). Evidence: offline (`scenarios/loot-window.lua`, which the code from before fails). Not seen in a client: a slot without a link when its row is built, or `LOOT_SLOT_CHANGED` for one. **A row is asked for again when its fade-in has ended, and every row when the window's has** (`WatchFadeIn`, `OnFadedIn`; the `OnFinished` of the row's `ShowAnim` and of `LootFrame.ShowAnim`). The row's `Init` plays a fade of a tenth of a second and the window plays one as it opens, in both flavors' XML, and an icon drawn while one runs comes out of it with no alpha. The queue draws a frame after the request, which is inside the fade; while it waited a tenth of a second first, the draw came after it. Seen (WoW Forever 1.60.1.71091): a quest item's row built with a link, its button asked for and drawn, the icon in place, shown, on screen and of the right texture and size, with alpha 0 where the draw had set 1; a piece of equipment in the same window had its binding text at once, whose alpha was never 0, and its star only with its second draw. Only the core sets that alpha, and none of its calls leaves an icon shown at 0, so it is the client's doing; which of the two fades does it, and whether it takes back what stood when the fade began, is not known. The section says of each row the icon's alpha as each fade ended beside what the draw had set, and where the row is drawn: the strata and level of the window, the list, its shadows, the row, the button and the overlay, and whether overlay and icon are shown, on screen, with what alpha, and how far inside the button and the list. With the row asked for again the quest item's icon is there (the same client and build, the author's eyes); the reading of the alpha at a fade's end was not taken then. Another window that fades its buttons in has the same to fear; none is known.
- **A status's icon is a line of a table** (`STATUS_ICONS` in `CaerdonWardrobe.lua`, drawn by `SetItemButtonStatus` through `SetStatusIcon`): the texture or atlas, its cut and colour, a background, the size, whether it moves, and which of the window's display rules has to allow it (`shownBy`). A Plus form is its plain form's entry in green, and `auction` is `auctionGoods` that is drawn as `sellable` where the window has no auction icon (`otherwise`). The small mark on an icon and the mark for "not yet" on an upgrade to come read the same entries, so an icon is named once. Four statuses are code beside the table because they decide more than an icon: an upgrade (the gear set's icon, the arrow with its gain, a look to gain), `transform`, `collected` (the equal-level icon, a gear set's) and an owned housing item. A new status with a plain icon is a new entry; the Icons page still names its sample textures itself (`config/Icons.lua`). Evidence, offline: two transcripts of `scenarios/draw-path.lua`, from before the table and with it. Every section is the same call for call but those of `otherNoLoot` and `otherPlusNoLoot`, which make the same calls with the icon's colour after its texture, each widget's calls that act on one another in the order they had.
- **One pending request per button.** `UpdateButton()` queues into `waitingToProcess` under the button, and the request carries its location key (`GetRequestKey`: feature and bag slot, or the feature's own key). The key still decides whether a request is current: it is dropped when the button has since been asked for another location. The lists used to be indexed by the location key, so of two buttons of one feature showing the same slot only the one asked for last was drawn: BetterBags' food and drink bar shows two bag items a second time, and after every refresh one button of each pair kept its old icon (seen on Forever with the test addon's rotation, 2 of 47). `pendingTooltipRetries` and the `ContinuableContainer` list are per button for the same reason, and a request coming back from the container never replaces a newer one for its button. Seen on Forever: with the change BetterBags draws all 47 again, a bags snapshot taken before and after has nothing changed, and no stop of the rotation shows anything differently. Retail not seen.
- **A draw reads the transmog info the status hands back.** `GetCaerdonStatus` returns it fifth: the table `GetTransmogInfo()` produced when the verdict was made, kept in the status cache with the verdict. `ProcessItem` used to compute a second one for the drawing (the level gain, the arrow's colour, the equal-level icon) on every draw, cache hit or not, so a piece of equipment cost two computations on its first draw and one on each later one. Now it is one per cache entry and refresh. A draw path does not call `GetTransmogInfo()`; the Debug Frame and the debug tooltip do, for an answer of their own. The table is shared by every button the entry stands for, so it is read and not changed (the two fields written into it, `canEquipForPlayer` and `upgradeStatus`, follow from the same entry). Evidence: offline (`scenarios/equipment-once.lua`). Forever client (1.60.1.70938, a hunter, the bags with a BetterBags vendor open): seven pieces of equipment cost seven computations per refresh where they cost fourteen, and around the reload that loaded the change no icon changed in the bags or the bank. Retail not seen.
- **A button asked for again before its draw keeps its one request,** which takes the new answer (`UpdateButton` writes into the table that is waiting). Windows ask more often than they are drawn: on a Forever toon 149 requests for 69 draws per refresh.
- **The queue works by time, a frame after the request.** `OnUpdate` is only set while there is work (`WakeQueue`), and the first pass comes in a later frame than the request, never the same one: a window asks from inside its own update, and what it does after that call is then done. `GetTime()` names the frame. A pass (`DrainQueue`) takes everything asked for before it as its batch and works through it until `QUEUE_FRAME_BUDGET` (8 ms) is used up, one item at least; the rest goes on in the next frame, and the pass that draws the last item puts the queue to sleep.
  - It used to be twenty items a frame, behind two waits of 0.05 s. Twenty cheap draws (a status already worked out) spread a window over more frames than it needed, twenty dear ones were a frame of 35 ms and more on retail, and nothing was drawn in the first tenth of a second after a window opened.
  - A request made while a batch is worked on waits for the next batch, a frame later at the earliest: a draw that makes its window ask again, or an item that comes back unloaded, cannot keep one pass going.
  - There is no coroutine. An error outside a draw (a feature's `IsSameItem`, the container) reaches the error handler from `OnUpdate`, the request it happened in is gone and the next frame goes on with the rest.
  - `IsQueueIdle()` says whether anything asked for is still undrawn: in the queue, waiting for its item to load, or for its tooltip.
- **A request that is no longer the button's is dropped, and clears nothing** (`ProcessRequest`). Either the button has since been asked for another place, and that newer request is waiting and must not be cleared along with the old one, or its window gave the button up (`ClearButton`, `ClearButtonState`). Only a feature's `IsSameItem` saying no clears a button from the queue.
- Items where `IsItemDataCached()` is false (it needs `IsItemCached()` and `customDataLoaded`) take a `ContinuableContainer` path, one container per feature, sent off when the batch is through; its callbacks route back through `waitingToProcess` and wake the queue.
- **Items find their methods through a metatable** (`CaerdonItem.lua`: the item's table, then `CaerdonItemMixin`, then Blizzard's `ItemMixin`; item data the same way through `CaerdonWardrobeItemDataMixin.CreateItemData`). A window makes a new item for every request, and copying both mixins into each was 76 fields a time. The lookup is live, so a method added to or replaced on `CaerdonItemMixin` later reaches every item, made before or after: the signal modules' entry points and the counters' wrappers rely on that. A method set on one item is still that item's alone. Do not copy an item or walk it with `pairs` for its methods: its own table holds only data.
- **A window is asked for only while it is on screen: `IsVisible()`, not `IsShown()`.** A host addon with a window of its own keeps Blizzard's shown under a hidden parent, and Blizzard's code goes on filling it. BetterBags does that with the bank panel (the client's bank functions want it shown) and with the merchant frame (hiding it would end the visit), and its own vendor keeps its cards shown inside a hidden panel once it has closed. All of it was drawn on every refresh. Forever client, the code from before, two toons under BetterBags: 54 of the 160 draws at the bank were Blizzard's hidden panel, 10 of 67 at a vendor were Blizzard's hidden buttons, and after one vendor visit its 37 cards were drawn on every refresh with no vendor open.
  - **A panel that was never on screen never gets its `OnHide`.** Blizzard's bank panel forgets its tab there, so under BetterBags it kept tab and buttons after the bank had closed, and the Bank feature's `Refresh()` walked them on every refresh: 54 requests for slots the client no longer describes, each ending in a clear.
  - **Skipping is right only where the window asks again when it comes on screen.** Blizzard's bank panel fills its buttons in `OnShow`, the merchant frame updates in `MerchantFrame_OnShow` (both flavors' source), and every BetterBags vendor that opens draws all of its cards (BetterBags 0.9.3, `next/vendor.lua`). BetterBags' bag buttons are the counter-example: an unchanged button is not drawn again when its bag opens, so that feature cannot skip them (next point).
  - **Ask the window, not the button.** Blizzard's bank refreshes a button before it shows it.
  - The Merchant feature's bag cache does not hang on its `Refresh()`: BetterBags' vendor reads the Merchant rules, and `IsItemInBags` reads the bags again by itself after a bag update.
  - Evidence: offline (`standalone/hidden-windows.lua`, which the code from before fails, with faults planted one at a time each failing it). Forever client (1.60.1.70938, a rogue under BetterBags, 2026-11-16): a timing at its bank has no draw by the Bank feature, one at its vendor none by the Merchant feature, the bags after the vendor closed none of the vendor's cards, every window's requests equal its draws, and no icon in the bags or the bank changed. Not seen: Blizzard's own bank and vendor with no bag addon, which rest on Blizzard's source; retail.
- **A window that does not ask again owes its hidden buttons a draw** (`CaerdonWardrobe:AskWhenOnScreen(button, feature, context)`). Asked for anyway, a closed BetterBags bag cost its whole content at every full refresh with nobody looking: on a Forever toon 20 draws, 12.6 ms and 890 KB a refresh, the same as with the bag open. The feature's `Refresh()` hands such a button to the core instead, the icon it has stays, and `feature:OnButtonOnScreen(context)` is called once when the button next comes on screen; the feature asks for the button from there, so it is drawn a frame after its bag opens. However many refreshes went by, that is one draw.
  - **The core hears of it through the overlay's `OnShow`.** The overlay is a shown child of the button, and the client runs a frame's `OnShow` when it comes on screen, whichever of its parents had been hidden. The script is set only while something is owed.
  - **It answers false when it noted nothing,** and the caller asks as usual: the button is on screen, or it was never asked for and has no overlay.
  - **A request or a clear for the button settles what was owed.** An item that arrives in a closed bag is still drawn at once, because BetterBags announces that draw.
  - **The price is a late change, not a late icon.** A verdict that changed while the bag was closed shows the old icon until the owed draw, a frame or more after the bag opens (the queue's 8 ms a frame over the whole bag). Nothing is blank meanwhile, and a refresh with the bag closed rarely changes what is in it.
  - **In a `Refresh()` of its own, only for a window measured to need it.** The other bag addons' features draw what they hold as before; `/cwtest timing closed` names a window that is drawn unseen ("not on screen"), and `/cwtest timing open` what its opening then costs. The default `Refresh()` uses it for every button that is not on screen (next point).
  - Evidence: offline (`scenarios/draw-path.lua` for the core, `standalone/hidden-windows.lua` for BetterBags' `Refresh()`; the code from before fails both, and faults planted one at a time each fail one). Forever client (1.60.1.70938, a rogue under BetterBags, 2026-11-16): a refresh with the bags closed draws nothing (0.01 ms, where it was 20 draws and 12.6 ms); the opening after it asks for the 19 buttons once and draws them in two frames, 13 ms, the last icon 63 ms after the first request, with no button on screen left undrawn; with the bags open a refresh draws 19 and none unseen; no icon in the bags or the bank changed. Not seen: a collapsed section; retail, where a bag is larger and the opening longer.
- **A window with no `Refresh()` of its own is asked again for what its buttons last showed** (`CaerdonWardrobe:AskAgain(feature)`, called by the `Refresh()` every feature inherits from `CaerdonWardrobeFeatureMixin`). `UpdateButton` keeps each request it queues with the button: the item, the feature, the place and the options, as the window gave them. After a full refresh the core asks for every one of the feature's again, so the window needs no code for it and none that knows how the window is built.
  - **What is remembered is what is on the button.** A window asks for a button whenever what it shows changes and clears it when it shows nothing. `ClearButton` and `ClearButtonState` forget the request, and a button another feature asks for becomes that feature's. So a cleared row, a button that was given up and one a second window took over are not asked for, and at the draw the feature's `IsSameItem` still has the last word.
  - **The item object is used again, not made again.** A window makes its item from a link, an ID, a bag slot or an auction row with its own extras, and the core cannot repeat that. An item keeps nothing a refresh has to drop: the verdict, the sources and the signals are the modules' caches, and an item in a bag slot reads its slot live. The not-ready retries have always used the item again.
  - **A button that is not on screen is owed the draw** (`AskWhenOnScreen`, with the base mixin's `OnButtonOnScreen` asking from the same memory). The core cannot know whether a window asks again when it comes on screen. Some do (Blizzard's auction list fills its rows again in `OnShow`); one that is shown again as it was need not. Owing is right for both: the window that asks replaces the request before it is drawn, or its request has settled the debt already, so the button is drawn once either way. A pooled row that is handed out again for another item is asked for by its window like any row.
  - **The two ways to call the base `Refresh`:** `CaerdonWardrobeFeatureMixin:Refresh(self)`, from a feature's own `Refresh()`, only drops that feature's waiting requests, as before. Inherited and called as the feature's own method, it is the default. A feature that walks its buttons and one that leaves it to the core are thereby told apart by whether the feature defines `Refresh` at all.
  - **Every feature's requests are remembered,** also those of a feature that never uses them: one table per button, written in place by every later request. The Debug Frame's item button follows a refresh by the same default.
  - **Each button is asked for through `Contained`,** so a request that raises costs the others nothing.
  - Evidence: offline (`scenarios/default-refresh.lua`, with the core's real queue: on screen, hidden and shown, cleared, taken over, disowned, a request that raises, a feature with its own `Refresh()`, and each of the sixteen feature files loaded and found to inherit the default; the code from before fails it, and faults planted one at a time each failed it). A Forever client has run it (Refresh, "An empty `Refresh()`"); a hidden button's owed draw was not looked at there.
- **Not-ready retries:** when the tooltip still reads "Retrieving item information", `ProcessItem` stashes the request in `pendingTooltipRetries` and one 1 s timer re-runs them. The limit (`NOT_READY_MAX_RETRIES`, 10) is counted per button in `buttonState`. The timer only retries while the button still has the same location key and item ID. `GetRequestKey` builds the key for both paths.
