  - *The line is found by its text, on every line, before anything else is read from the tooltip.* Its type is not the same in every build: retail 12.1.5 gives it as an `ErrorLine`, which the text checks for typed lines never see, and it is red, so it read as a requirement the toon does not meet and the verdict made from it was kept until the next refresh. Evidence: a retail export of a piece that had just come off the toon (one line of type 41, and a status that was "ready" with `unusableItem` set); offline `scenarios/equipment.lua`. Not seen: how long a retail client leaves a piece in that state, and whether ten retries outlast it.
- **A draw asks the window for its display rules once.** `ProcessItem` calls `GetDisplayInfoInternal` and hands the answer to both halves of the drawing (`SetItemButtonStatus`, `SetItemButtonBindType`); a half called by itself, as the layout editor does, asks for itself. With no status and no text nobody asks. A window's declaration costs no table of its own (above, "A window declares what it shows of a draw"). A `GetDisplayInfo` function gets the host's button, for a feature whose answer depends on what the host addon has put on it, and the status and the binding text; the auction house reads the status.
- **The drawing's helpers are plain functions** above `SetItemButtonStatus` (`SetRingIcon`, `DrawUpgradeIcon`, `ShowLevelGain`, ...), handed what they need. Made inside it they were four closures per draw. A new helper goes beside them.
- **Search dimming is one timer per frame.** A slot a bag search has dimmed gets its icon dimmed too, a frame after the draw, because the bag's code can put the overlay on after the draw. Every draw used to start a timer for that; now the buttons drawn in a frame are noted and one timer looks at them all, and only buttons that have a search overlay are noted.
- **Evidence for the points above, from the one request per button down** (the queue, the stale request, the item objects, the display rules, the helpers and the timer): offline, `scenarios/draw-path.lua`, with faults planted one at a time each failing it, and the drawing compared call by call with the code from before (section Offline Harness). Forever client (1.60.1.70938, a hunter and a rogue under BetterBags, forced refreshes with the bags, the bank and a vendor open, against the records from before): no icon changed in the bags or the bank, and the bag addon rotation passed at every stop, Blizzard's bags among them. The addon's memory per refresh fell by 28 to 40% (the bank from 4570 to 2756 KB), nearly all of what the item objects and the drawing had allocated. The first icon came about 0.12 s after a refresh where it had been 0.21 s. The longest pass was 8 to 11 ms, the budget and the one draw over it. Addon time stayed about the same: the drawing got cheaper by a quarter, and the status, which is four fifths of a refresh there, was not touched. Retail has not run them.
  - **A frame rate below the budget's reach stretches a window.** At 30 frames a second (a client in the background) the queue still takes 8 ms of each frame, so 160 draws that fit into 0.1 s at full rate took 0.26 s. Read a timing's frames per run before comparing its icon times with another's.
- **Overlay:** `EnsureCaerdonButton()` creates a child frame with `SetAllPoints`, holding `mogStatus` textures, `bindsOnText` and `upgradeDeltaText`. `buttonState` has weak keys.
- **Binding text:** with `bindingScale < 1` the font string width is divided by the scale; scale is reset to 1 before `GetStringWidth()`.

- **A draw that waits for its tooltip is asked again when the tooltip's lookup is through** (`CaerdonWardrobe:RetryWhenTooltipComes`, `TOOLTIP_DATA_UPDATE`). The status hands the tooltip's instance on with "its tooltip" (`dataInstanceID`, third return value), and the client names that instance when what it was looking up is there ("a sparse or cache lookup has resolved"; with no instance, any; the same in both flavors, where Blizzard's own tooltips fill themselves again by it). Listened to only while a tooltip is waited for, and the button whose tooltip the client names is the one asked again (below). A tooltip with no instance waits for the timer's second as before. Why it was needed: such a button was asked again early only where something else had data coming (a worn piece, the Catalyst's set piece), which nobody had meant. With the Catalyst's answers kept nothing had, and a bank's sixty waiting buttons took 1.2 s on average where it had been 0.4 to 0.6 (retail 12.1.5.70961). Evidence: offline (`scenarios/draw-path.lua`, with a stand-in for the status), and in a retail client (12.1.5.70961, a bank of 213 opened): 60 such events named a tooltip a draw waited for and 25 another, and the 58 draws that waited for a tooltip waited 0.23 s on average, 0.37 s at the longest. The counters keep that count (`Counters:NoteTooltipUpdate`, a line under the waits in the export). That reading was taken while each such event still asked every waiting button. Matched to its button since, the same bank read 56 draws at 0.25 s on average and 0.40 s at the longest, with 66 events for a tooltip waited for and 16 for another, and one set piece's data beside them.
- **What comes is matched to what waits for it** (author decision; `CaerdonWardrobe:RetryWaitingFor(key)`, `RunNotReadyRetries(early, keys)`). A verdict that is put off names what for as the status's third value, and a button that waits keeps that name (`waitsFor`): a tooltip's instance (`tooltip:<n>`), a set piece's data (`item:<ID>`), the client's word on a copy (`conversion:<GUID>`), the pieces it is compared with (`gear`). Whoever hears that it has come gives the queue the same name, and only the buttons that wait for it are asked again; the others go on waiting, and the timer's second still asks all of them. Where an arrival names nothing (a tooltip update with no instance, a caller with no name to give) every waiting button is asked, as before (`RetryNotReadySoon`). Before, every arrival asked every waiting button: a window's sixty for one item's data, and one kind of wait was short only because another kind's data kept arriving, which hid that a tooltip was waited for by the clock alone. A new wait gets a name on both sides, the status's return and the arrival's call; a name on one side only is a button that waits for the timer. Evidence: offline (`scenarios/draw-path.lua` for the queue; `transform-looks.lua` and `pawn-scales.lua` for the names on both sides; faults planted), and for the tooltip's name in a retail client (12.1.5.70961; A draw that waits for its tooltip, above). The other three names have not been seen at work in a client.
## Dressing Room

- `TryHookDressUp()` installs hooks in independent groups, because the globals load from different addons (`DressUpFrameTransmogSetButtonMixin`, `DressUpFrame`, `DressUpOutfitDetailsSlotMixin`); `ADDON_LOADED` retries until all are in. `hooksecurefunc` on a mixin table works for ScrollBox buttons, since `Mixin()` copies the hooked function.
- Items in the SetSelectionPanel are Equipment. `UpdateSetSelectionButton` sets `extraData.appearanceSourceID` from `elementData.itemModifiedAppearanceID`.

## LoadSources

`waitingForItems` is keyed by item ID, so `#waitingForItems == 0` is always true and `callbackFunction()` fires immediately; real completion is handled by `ProcessTheItem` / `FailTheItem` with `not next(waitingForItems)`.

- **Leave it.** Switching to `not next(...)` makes `LoadSources` block the whole `ContinuableContainer`, and every ensemble in the batch then shows as unlearnable.
- **Consequence:** completion is reported twice, so `CaerdonItemMixin:ContinueWithCancelOnItemLoad` wraps its callback to fire once. Blizzard's `ContinuableContainer` crashes on a late duplicate (`bad argument #1 to 'ipairs'`), which surfaced on a fresh Forever install. `ContinueOnItemLoad` is deliberately unguarded: features rely on the re-run, and `WhenItemLoaded` keeps it for them (Processing Pipeline).
